Om. Śrī. Salutation to Śrī Gaṇādhipati.
नागीशाद्याः सुमनसः सर्वार्थानामुपक्रमे । यं नत्वा कृतकृत्याः स्युस्तं नमामि गजाननं ॥ (*nāgīśādyāḥ sumanasaḥ sarvārthānāmupakrame | yaṃ natvā kṛtakṛtyāḥ syustaṃ namāmi gajānanaṃ ‖*) **GLOSS**
यस्य निश्वसितं वेदा यो वेदेभ्योऽखिलं जगत् । निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरं ॥ (*yasya niśvasitaṃ vedā yo vedebhyo 'khilaṃ jagat | nirmame tamahaṃ vaṃde vidyātīrthamaheśvaraṃ ‖*) **GLOSS**
वेदः शिवः शिवो वेदो वेदाध्याया सदाशिवः । तस्मात्सर्वप्रयत्नेन वेदमेव सदा जपेत् ॥ (*vedaḥ śivaḥ śivo vedo vedādhyāyā sadāśivaḥ | tasmātsarvaprayatnena vedameva sadā japet ‖*) **GLOSS**
The Ṛgveda-saṃhitā with the Bhāṣya of Sāyaṇa (with a Kannada translation of the meaning and explanation). Volume 21.
Fourth Aṣṭaka, Seventh Adhyāya.
The Seventh Adhyāya in the Fourth Aṣṭaka; the Twenty-ninth Sūkta of the Sixth Maṇḍala.

**Preface**
This volume of the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā contains four adhyāyas in all: the 7th and 8th adhyāyas of the fourth aṣṭaka and the 1st and 2nd adhyāyas of the fifth aṣṭaka. That is, it takes in the sūktas 29-35 (47) of the sixth maṇḍala **NOTE**, and the sūktas 1-19 of the seventh maṇḍala (66 sūktas in all). Of these, the seventh adhyāya of the fourth aṣṭaka, which begins first (19 sūktas), is wholly devoted to the deity Indra. All the matter concerning Indra we have set out at length in pages 569-582 of the 5th volume and pages 631-683 of the 16th volume of the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, and besides this we have explained, according to the occasion, the passages concerning Indra that come here and there in other volumes, 

### Printed p. 2
fully and at length. Therefore in this adhyāya the matters said before will be left out and only new matters will be described. That is, the Ṛks will be explained with the meaning of the words and the purport, and special topics will be added only in the places where they are needed.

The twenty-ninth sūkta

## Sūkta 29 (Maṇḍala 6)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अथ सप्तमोऽध्याय आरभ्यते । षष्ठस्य मंडलस्य तृतीयेऽनुवाके पंच
> सूक्तानि व्याकृतानि । इंद्रं व इति षडृचं षष्ठं सूक्तं भरद्वाजस्यार्षं त्रैष्टुभमैंद्रं । अनुक्रम्यते च । इंद्रं षडिति । गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that the seventh adhyāya now begins. In the third anuvāka of the sixth maṇḍala five sūktas have been expounded. The sixth sūkta of this series, of six Ṛks, beginning इंद्रं व (*iṃdraṃ va*), has Bharadvāja as its seer, the metre Triṣṭubh, and Indra as its deity. The Anukramaṇī also says so (इंद्रं षट् (*iṃdraṃ ṣaṭ*)). The use in ritual (viniyoga) has already been stated.

Translation (Anuvāda): Now the exposition of the seventh adhyāya of the fourth aṣṭaka is begun. In the third anuvāka of the sixth maṇḍala five sūktas have so far (in the previous adhyāya) been explained. Now the sixth sūkta, "Indraṃ vo naraḥ", is begun. In it there are six Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Bharadvāja; the deity is Indra; the metre is Triṣṭup. In the Anukramaṇī it is said at the beginning, "Indraṃ ṣaṭ". The use in ritual (viniyoga) has been told before.

Sūkta 29. Maṇḍala 6. Anuvāka 3. Sūkta 29. Aṣṭaka 4. Adhyāya 7. Varga 1. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 6. Seer: Bharadvāja Bārhaspatya. Deity: Indra. Metre: Triṣṭup.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रं॑ वो॒ नरः॑ स॒ख्याय॑ सेपुर्म॒हो यंतः॑ सुम॒तये॑  ॒
> चकानाः ।  
> म॒हो हि दा॒ता वज्र॑हस्तो॒ अस्ति॑ म॒हामु॑ र॒ण्वमव॑से  
> यजध्वं ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 3

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रं । वः । नरः । सख्याय । सेपुः । महः । यंतः । सुमतये । चकानाः ।  
> महः । हि । दाता । वज्रहस्तः । अस्ति । महां । ऊं इति । रण्वं । अवसे। यजध्वं ॥१॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे यजमाना वो युष्माकं संबंधिनो नरो नेतार ऋत्विजः सख्याय
> सखिभावार्थमिंद्रं सेपुः । परिचरंति । स्पृशंति वा । कथंभूताः । महो महांति
> स्तोत्राणि यंत उपयंतः प्राप्नुवंतः । कुर्वंत इत्यर्थः । तथा सुमतये ।
> सुमतिः शोभनानुग्रहात्मक तदीया बुद्धिः । तां चकानाः कामयमानाः ॥
> क्रियाग्रहणं कर्तव्यमिति कर्मणः संप्रदानसंज्ञा । चकाना इति कामयतेश्छांदसं
> रूपं ॥ यद्वा । महो महत्कर्म यंतोऽनुतिष्ठंतः सुमतये सुमतिं
> शोभनां स्तुतिं चकानाः शब्दयंतः ॥ कै गै शब्द इत्यस्माल्लिटः कानचि रूपं ॥ हि
> यस्माद्वज्रहस्तो वज्रपाणिरिंद्रो महो महतो धनस्य दातास्ति भवति तस्माद्रण्वं
> रमणीयं महामु महांतमेवावसे रक्षणाय यजध्वं । हविर्भिः पूजयत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O sacrificers, your priests (the leaders, the ṛtvijs) serve Indra, or touch him, for friendship, that is, to become his friends. They do so offering great hymns, approaching and obtaining them, that is, making them; and desiring his good favour, a kindly mind of his towards them. Or else, performing a great rite and desiring (making resound) a good praise. Since Indra, the thunderbolt in his hand, is the giver of great wealth, therefore worship him alone, the delightful, the great, with oblations, for protection.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वः (*vaḥ*)—your relations
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—the priests
- सख्याय (*sakhyāya*)—for the gaining of friendship
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—great hymns
- यंतः (*yaṃtaḥ*)—offering
- सुवःतये (*suvaḥtaye*)—excellent understanding
- चकानाः (*cakānāḥ*)—desiring
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- सेपुः (*sepuḥ*)—they worship
- वज्रहस्तः (*vajrahastaḥ*)—Indra, the bearer of the thunderbolt
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—to venerable wealth
- दाता (*dātā*)—as the giver
- आस्ति (*āsti*)—he is
- रण्वं (*raṇvaṃ*)—the delightful
- महामु (*mahāmu*)—the great-souled Indra himself
- अवसे (*avase*)—for protection
- यजध्वं (*yajadhvaṃ*)—worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O sacrificers, the priests who are your relations worship Indra, offering great hymns for the gaining of friendship with Indra **NOTE**, and desiring excellent understanding. Indra, the bearer of the thunderbolt, is the giver of venerable wealth. Worship Indra himself, the delightful and great-souled, for protection.

##### English translation
Your priests, (oh worshippers), propitiate Indra for his friendship, offering great (praise), and desirous of his favour; for the wielder of the thunderbolt is the giver of vast (wealth); worship him, therefore, who is mighty and benevolent, (to obtain) his protection.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ यस्मि॒न्हस्ते॒ नर्या॑ मिमि॒क्षुरा रथे॑ हिर॒ण्यये॑ रथे॒ष्णाः ।  
> आ र॒श्मयो॒ गभ॑स्तोः स्फू॒रयो॒राध्व॒न्नश्वा॑सो॒ वृष॑णो  ॒
> युजानाः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । यस्मिन् । हस्ते । नर्याः । मिमिक्षुः । आ । रथे । हिरण्यये । रथेऽस्थाः ।  
> आ । रश्मयः । गभस्योः । स्फूरयोः । आ । अध्वन् । अश्वासः । वृषणः । युजानाः ॥२॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यस्मिन्निंद्रे नर्या नृभ्यो हिता राय आ मिमिक्षुः आसिच्यंते आपूर्यंते ॥
> व्यत्ययेन कर्मणि कर्तृप्रत्ययः ॥ इंद्रे कुत्रेति चेत् । हस्ते तदीये बाहौ ।
> यद्वा । एतत्समानाधिकरणमिंद्रस्य विशेषणं । हस्ते हंतर्यस्मिन्निंद्र इत्यर्थः ।
> उक्तं यास्केन । हस्तो हंतेः प्राशुर्हनने । नि. १-७ । इति । यद्वा । ईद्वशे
> यॆस्मिन्निंद्रे वर्तमाना राय आ मिमिक्षुः आसिंचंति अपूरयंति स्तोत्यन् ।
> यश्चेंद्रो रथेष्णा रथे स्थाता तत्स्वभावः सन् हिरण्यये सुवर्णमये रथ आ
> शिष्ठति । यद्वा । रथेष्णा रथेवस्थिता रायो धनानि हिरण्मये रथे तिष्ठन् य इंद्र
> अ सिंचति अभिवर्षति । यस्य च स्फूरयोः स्कूलायोर्विस्तृतयोगर्भस्तोः ।
> बाहुनामैतत् । बाह्वो रश्मयोऽभीशव आ सिच्यंते आयम्यंते । यस्य
> चाश्वासोऽश्वा वृषणः सेक्तारो युवानो युजाना रथे युज्यमाना अध्वन्नॆध्वनि
> मार्ग आ मिमिक्षुः आसिंचंति अपूरयंति । आगच्छंतीत्यर्थः । तमिंद्रं स्तुम इति
> शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: in which Indra the riches that are good for men are poured in, that is, are filled (an active form used in a passive sense by a change of voice). If one asks where in Indra, it is in his hand, that is, his arm. Or else this word is an adjective of Indra in the same case: the slayer, Indra in whom. Yāska has said: "hasta is from han, quick in striking" (Ni. 1-7). Or else, the riches that rest in this Indra are poured in, that is, filled, by the praisers. And the Indra who is by nature a stander in the chariot sits in a golden chariot. Or else, the riches, standing as if in the chariot, remain in the golden chariot, and that Indra pours, showers. And of whom the thick, extended arms (gabhasti is a name of the arm) have the reins, the bridles, poured in, that is, held. And of whom the horses, showerers and young, yoked to the chariot, come, fill, on the road. We praise that Indra: this is the part to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यस्मिन् हस्ते (*yasmin haste*)—in the hand of which Indra
- नर्याः (*naryāḥ*)—riches good for mankind
- आ मिमिक्षुः (*ā mimikṣuḥ*)—are filled
- रथेष्ठाः (*ratheṣṭhāḥ*)—which Indra, who is by nature a stander in the chariot
- हिरण्यये (*hiraṇyaye*)—made of gold
- रथे (*rathe*)—in the chariot
- आ (*ā*) (तिष्ठंति (*tiṣṭhaṃti*))—sits

### Printed p. 5
- स्थूरयोः (*sthūrayoḥ*)—extended
- गभस्त्योः (*gabhastyoḥ*)—in (whose) arms
- रश्मयः (*raśmayaḥ*)—the bridles of the horses
- आ (*ā*) (सिच्यंते (*sicyaṃte*))—are held
- (and) अश्वासः (*aśvāsaḥ*)—(whose) horses
- वृषणः (*vṛṣaṇaḥ*)—powerful
- युजानः (*yujānaḥ*)—and fit to be yoked to the chariot
- अध्वन् (*adhvan*)—on the road
- आ (*ā*) (मिमिक्षुः (*mimikṣuḥ*))—overrun (worship that very Indra)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Worship that Indra in whose hand riches good for mankind are filled; who, being by nature a stander in the chariot, sits in a golden chariot; in whose extended arms the bridles of the horses are held; and whose horses, powerful and of themselves fit to be yoked to the chariot, overrun the very road.

##### English translation
In whose hand (riches) good for man are accumulated, the chariot-mounted in a golden car; in whose arms the rays of light (are collected); whose vigorous horses, yoked (to his car, convey him) on the road (of the firmament).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> श्री॒ये ते॒ पादा॒ दुव॒ आ मि॑मिक्षुर्धृ॒ष्णुर्व॒ज्री शव॑सा॒ दक्षि॑णावान् ।  
> वसा॑नो॒ अत्कं॑ सुर॒भिं दृ॒शे कं स्व१॒॑र्ण नृ॑तविषि॒रो ब॑भूथ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> श्रीये । ते । पादा । दुवः । आ । मिमिक्षुः । धृष्णुः । वज्री । शवसा । दक्षिणवान् ।  
> वसानः । अत्कं । सुरभिं । दृशे । कं । स्वः । न । नृतो इति । इषिरः । बभूथ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र श्रिय ऐश्वर्यार्थं ते त्वदीययोः पादा पादयोर्दुवः
> परिचरणमा मिमिक्षुः । आसिंचंति । समर्पयंति । भरद्वाजः समर्पयति ॥ व्यत्ययेन
> बहुवचनं ॥ यद्वा। ते तव पादौ श्रिये श्रयितुं सेवितुं दुवः परिचरणमासिंचंति।
> अनुतिष्ठंति । यस्त्वं शवसा बलेन धृष्णुः शत्रूणां धर्षको वज्रे वज्रवान्

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, for prosperity, that is, for wealth, they pour on your feet service, that is, they offer it; Bharadvāja offers it (the plural is used by a change of number). Or else, your two feet, to be resorted to, to be served, for prosperity, they pour service, that is, they perform it. You who, by might, by strength, are the overpowerer of enemies, the bearer of the thunderbolt,

### Printed p. 6

> दक्षिणावान् । दक्षिणा स्तोतृभ्यो दातव्यं धनं । तद्वान् हे नृतो नेतरिंद्र स त्वं
> सुरभिं प्रशस्तमत्कं सततगमनशीलमात्मीयं रूपं दृशे सर्वेषां दर्शनार्थं
> वसान आच्छादयॆन् स्वर्ण सूर्य इव इषिरो गंतैषणीयो वा बभूथ । भवसि ।
> कमिति पूरकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

the possessor of the dakṣiṇā, the wealth to be given to the praisers, O leader Indra, you, to be seen by all, clothe yourself, cover yourself, in your own form, excellent (praiseworthy) and ever in motion, like the sun, and have become desirable, or going. The word कमिति (*kamiti*) is a filler.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- श्रिये (*śriye*)—to obtain prosperity
- ते (*te*)—your
- पादा (*pādā*)—to the feet
- दुवः (*duvaḥ*)—worship
- आ मिमिक्षुः (*ā mimikṣuḥ*)—(Bharadvāja) offers
- नृतो (*nṛto*)—O Indra, leader of rites
- शवसा (*śavasā*)—by strength
- धृष्णुः (*dhṛṣṇuḥ*)—the overpowerer of enemies
- वज्री (*vajrī*)—the bearer of the thunderbolt
- दक्षिणावान् (*dakṣiṇāvān*)—you who possess wealth to be given
- सुरभिं (*surabhiṃ*)—praiseworthy
- अत्मं (*atmaṃ*)—the form that ever moves about
- दृशे (*dṛśe*)—so as to be seen by all
- वसानः (*vasānaḥ*)—wearing
- स्वर्न (*svarna*)—like the sun
- इषिरः (*iṣiraḥ*)—of a revolving nature
- बभूथ (*babhūtha*)—you have become

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, Bharadvāja offers worship at your feet for the gaining of prosperity. O Indra, leader of rites, you, by your strength the overpowerer of enemies, the bearer of the thunderbolt, the possessor of wealth to be given, wearing a praiseworthy form that ever moves about, so as to be seen by all, have become revolving like the sun.

##### English translation
They offer adoration at your feet to acquire prosperity, for you are the overthrower of enemies by (your) strength, the wielder of the thunderbolt, the bestower of donations ; leader (of rites), you are like the rolling sun, wearing in the sight (of all) a graceful and ever-moving form.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स सोम॒ आमि॑श्लतमः सु॒तो भू॒द्यस्मि॑न्प॒क्तिः प॒च्यते॒ संति॑ धा॒नाः।  
> इंद्रं॒ नरः॑ स्तु॒वंतो॑ ब्रह्मका॒रा उ॒क्का शंसं॑तो दे॒ववा॑ततमाः ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 7

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । सोमः । आमिश्लतमः। सुतः । भूत् । यस्मिन् । पक्तिः । पच्यते । संति । धानाः।  
> इंद्रं । नरः । स्तुवंतः । ब्रह्मकाराः । उक्षा । शंसंतः । देववाततमाः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यः सोमो वक्ष्यते स सोमः सुतोऽभिषुतः सन् आमिश्लतम अभिमुख्यन
> मिश्रतमो भूत् । इंद्रेणात्यर्थं संगच्छते । यस्मिन् सोमेऽभिषुते सति पक्तिः
> पक्तव्यः पुरोडाशादिः पच्यते । भृष्टयवा धानाः । ताश्च संति हविरर्थं संस्कृता
> भवंति । यस्मिंश्च सोमेऽभिषुते नरो नेतारो ब्रह्मकारा ब्रह्मणोऽन्नस्य
> हविर्लक्षणस्य कर्तार ऋत्विज इंद्रं स्तोत्रैः स्तुवंत उक्कोक्यानि शस्त्राणि च शंसंत
> उच्चारयंतो देववाततमा देवानतिशयेनोपगता भवंति स सोम इंद्रेण
> संमिश्रितोऽभूदित्यन्वयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: that Soma, of which mention is made, when pressed became most perfectly mixed, mixed face to face, that is, it unites most closely with Indra. When this Soma has been pressed, the things to be cooked, the cake and the rest, are cooked; the fried barley, the grains, are there, prepared for the oblation. And when this Soma has been pressed, the leaders, the priests who are makers of the brahma, that is, of the food of the nature of the oblation, praising Indra with hymns and reciting (pronouncing) the uktha-verses and the śastras, become most eminently near to the gods. The construction is: that Soma became mixed with Indra.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यस्मिन् (*yasmin*)—when which Soma is pressed
- पक्तिः (*paktiḥ*)—the cake
- पच्यते (*pacyate*)—is cooked
- धानाः (*dhānāḥ*)—grains of barley and the like
- संति (*saṃti*)—are prepared (and when which juice is pressed)
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—the leaders
- ब्रह्मकाराः (*brahmakārāḥ*)—and praisers, the priests
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- स्तुवंतः (*stuvaṃtaḥ*)—praising
- उक्षा (*ukṣā*)—the śastras
- शंसंतः (*śaṃsaṃtaḥ*)—reciting
- देववाततमाः (*devavātatamāḥ*)—and becoming those who obtain the nearness of the gods
- सः सोमः (*saḥ somaḥ*)—that same Soma juice
- सुतः (*sutaḥ*)—pressed
- आमिश्लतमः भूत् (*āmiślatamaḥ bhūt*)—becomes that which joins Indra

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When which Soma juice is pressed, the cake is cooked for the offering of the oblation, and grains such as barley are prepared; and when which juice is pressed, the priests, who are leaders and praisers, become praisers of Indra, reciters of the śastras, and obtainers of the nearness of the gods: that same Soma juice, pressed, joins Indra.

##### English translation
That libation is most perfectly mixed when, upon its being effused, the cakes are baked, and the barley is fried, and the priests, glorifying

### Printed p. 8
Indra, offering the (sacrificial) food, and reciting holy prayers, are approaching most nigh to the gods.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न ते॒ अंतः॒ शव॑सो धाय्य॒स्य वि तु बा॑बधे॒ रोद॑सी महि॒त्वा ।  
> आ ता सू॒रिः पृ॑णति॒ तूतु॑जानो यू॒थेवा॒प्सु स॒मीज॑मान  ॒
> ऊती ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । ते । अंतः । शवसः । धायि । अस्य । वि । तु । बाबधे । रोदसी इति । महित्वा ।  
> आ । ता । सूरिः । पृणति । तूतुजानः । यूथाइव । अप्सु । संईटमानः ।  
> ऊती ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते तवास्योक्तगुणस्य शवसो बलस्यांतोऽवसानं न धायि । अत्र
> केवलोऽपि दधातिरवपूर्वार्थो द्रष्टव्यः । नावाधायि । अस्माभिर्नाज्ञायीत्यर्थः।
> यद्बलं रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्य महित्वा महत्वेन तु क्षिप्रं वि बाबधे । यस्माद्भलाद्द्यावापृथिव्य बिभीत इत्यर्थः । ता तद्बलं सूरिः स्तोता तूतुजानस्त्वरितः सन्
> ऊती ऊत्या तर्पकेण हविषा समाजमानः सम्यग्यजन् आ पृणति । अपूरयति । तत्र
> दृष्टांतः । यूथेवापूदकेषु गवां यूथानीव यथा गोपालोऽपः पाययन्नद्धिस्तरपयति तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, no end, no limit, of this strength of yours, of the qualities stated, has been established; here the simple root is to be understood as having the sense of the root with the prefix before it, so that the meaning is that it has not been known by us. By the greatness of which strength heaven and earth are quickly shaken; that is, heaven and earth are afraid of that strength. That strength the praiser, hastening, worshipping fully with the satisfying oblation, fills up. The illustration: as a cowherd, giving water to the herds of cows in the waters, satisfies them with the waters, so does the praiser.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) ते (*te*)—your
- अस्य (*asya*)—well-known
- शवसः (*śavasaḥ*)—of strength
- अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)—the limit
- न धायि (*na dhāyi*)—has not been determined
- रोदसी (*rodasī*)—heaven and earth
- महित्वा (*mahitvā*)—by your greatness
- तु (*tu*)—at once
- वि बाबधे (*vi bābadhe*)—are afraid
- ता (*tā*)—that same strength of yours
- सूरिः (*sūriḥ*)—the praiser
- तूतुजानः (*tūtujānaḥ*)—filled with faith
- अप्पु (*appu*)—in the midst of water
- यूथेव (*yūtheva*)—as one satisfies herds of cows with water
- ऊती (*ūtī*)—with satisfying oblations
- समीजमानः (*samījamānaḥ*)—performing the sacrifice
- आ पृणाति (*ā pṛṇāti*)—satisfies you

### Printed p. 9

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, no one can know the limit of your well-known strength. Even heaven and earth are afraid because of your greatness. As a cowherd, in the midst of water, satisfies the herds of cows with water, so your praiser, performing the sacrifice with satisfying oblations, satisfies your powers.

##### English translation
No limit of your strength has been assigned; heaven and earth are intimidated by its greatness: the pious worshipper, hastening (to sacrifice), and earnestly performing worship, gratifies you with the offering, as (the cowkeeper satisfies) the herds with water.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒वेदिंद्रः॑ सु॒हव॑ ऋ॒ष्टो अ॑स्तू॒ती अनू॑ती हिरिशि॒प्रः सत्वा॑ ॥  
> ए॒वा हि जा॒तो अस॑मात्योजाः पु॒रू च॑ वृ॒त्रा ह॑नति॒ नि  ॑
> दस्यून् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एव । इत् । इंद्रः । सुहवः । ऋष्टः । अस्तु । ऊती । अनूती । हरिशिप्रः । सत्वा ।  
> एव । हि । जातः । असमातिओजाः । पुरु । च । वृत्रा । हनति । नि । दस्यून् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एवैवमुक्तेन प्रकारेण ऋष्टो महानिंद्रः सुहवोऽस्तु सुखेनाह्वातव्यो
> भवतु । इदिति पूरणः । हिरिशिप्रः । शिप्रे हनू नासिके वा । हरितवर्णॆ शिप्रे यस्य
> तादृश इंद्र ऊती ऊत्यागमनेनानूती अनूत्यानागमनेन वा : सत्वा गमयिता
> भवति धनानां । स्वयमागतोऽनागतोऽपि स्तोतृभ्यो धनं प्रयच्छतीत्यर्थः। एवा
> ह्येवं हि जातः प्रादुर्भूतोऽयमिंद्रोऽसमात्योजा असमात्यसमानमनुपममुत्कृष्टतरमोजो बलं यस्य तादृशः सन् पुरु पुरूणि बहूनि च वृत्रा
> वृत्राण्यावरकाणि रक्षःप्रभृतीनि हनति । हिनस्तु । तथा दस्युनुपक्षपयित्कन् शत्रुवंश्च
> नि हंतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: in the manner so stated, may the mighty Indra be easily invoked, that is, easy to be called. The word इत् (*it*) is a filler. Hariśipra: the śipras are the jaws or the nostrils; he whose śipras are of tawny colour **NOTE**. Such an Indra, by coming or by not coming, becomes the bringer of riches: whether he has come himself or not, he gives wealth to the praisers. Thus born, that is, manifested, this Indra, being one whose strength is unequalled, incomparable and most excellent, destroys many obstructors, the Rākṣasas and the like; and may he also destroy the Dasyus, the destroyers, and the enemies.

### Printed p. 10

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- एव (*eva*)—in this manner
- ऋष्टः (*ṛṣṭaḥ*)—the great
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- सुहवः (*suhavaḥ*)—favourable to our invocation
- अस्तु (*astu*)—may he be
- हिरिशिप्रः (*hiriśipraḥ*)—Indra, with blue-coloured cheeks
- ऊती (*ūtī*)—whether by his coming
- अनूती (*anūtī*)—or without coming
- सत्वा (*satvā*)—may he be the giver of wealth
- एवा हि (*evā hi*)—in this very manner
- जातः (*jātaḥ*)—Indra, who is born
- असमात्योजाः (*asamātyojāḥ*)—being one of most excellent strength
- पुरु (*puru*)—very many
- वृत्रा (*vṛtrā*)—Asuras
- हनति (*hanati*)—may he destroy
- दस्यून् (*dasyūn*)—and the destroyers
- नि (*ni*) (हंतु (*haṃtu*))—may he destroy

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the great Indra be favourable to our invocation and accept it. May Indra, with blue-coloured cheeks, be the giver of wealth to us, whether by his coming or without coming. In this very manner may Indra, who has appeared, being one of most excellent strength, destroy the Asuras, who are exceedingly numerous.

##### English translation
Thus may the mighty Indra be successfully invoked; he, the azure-chinned, the giver of wealth, whether by coming or nnt coming (to the sacrifice); and may he who is of unequalled strength destroy, as soon as manifested, many opposing (evil spirits) and (hostile) Dasyus.

The thirtieth sūkta

## Sūkta 30 (Maṇḍala 6)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> भूय इदिति पंचर्चं सप्तमं सूक्तं भरद्वाजस्यार्षं त्रैष्टुभमैंद्रं ।
> भूयः पंचेत्यनुक्रांतं ॥ अग्निष्टोमे माध्यंदिनसवनेच्छावाकशस्त्र एतत्तूक्तं
> शंसनीयं । सूत्रितं च । उदिन्वस्य रिच्यते भूय इत् । आ. ५-१६ । इति ॥ महाव्रतेऽपि निष्केवल्य एतत्तूक्तं । तथैव पंचमारण्यके सूत्रितं । भूय इद्दावृधे
> वीर्याय नृणामु त्वा । ऐ. आ. ५-१-६ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: the seventh sūkta, of five Ṛks, beginning भूय इत् (*bhūya it*), has Bharadvāja as its seer, the metre Triṣṭubh, and Indra as its deity. The Anukramaṇī says: भूयः पंच (*bhūyaḥ paṃca*). In the Agniṣṭoma, at the midday pressing, this sūkta is to be recited in the śastra of the Acchāvāka. It is stated in the sūtra: उदिन्वस्य रिच्यते भूय इत् (*udinvasya ricyate bhūya it*) (Ā. 5-16). In the Mahāvrata also this sūkta is used in the niṣkevalya. It is stated likewise in the fifth Āraṇyaka: भूय इद्दावृधे वीर्याय नृणामु त्वा (*bhūya iddāvṛdhe vīryāya nṛṇāmu tvā*) (Ai. Ā. 5-1-6).

Translation (Anuvāda): This sūkta "Bhūya it" is the seventh sūkta in the third anuvāka. In it there are five Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Bharadvāja; the deity is Indra; the metre is Triṣṭup. In the Anukramaṇī it is said "Bhūyaḥ pañca". In the Agniṣṭoma sacrifice, at the time of the midday pressing,

### Printed p. 11
this sūkta is used for the śastra-mantras to be recited by the priest called Acchāvāka; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, उदिन्वस्य रिच्यते भूय इत् (*udinvasya ricyate bhūya it*) (Ā. 5-16). In the rite called Mahāvrata also, this sūkta is used for the niṣkevalya-śastra mantras, as is indicated in the fifth Āraṇyaka of the Aitareya Āraṇyaka by the sentence भूय इद्दावृधे वीर्याय नृणामु त्वा (*bhūya iddāvṛdhe vīryāya nṛṇāmu tvā*) (Ai. Ā. 5-1-6).

Sūkta 30. Maṇḍala 6. Anuvāka 3. Sūkta 30. Aṣṭaka 4. Adhyāya 7. Varga 2. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5. Seer: Bharadvāja Bārhaspatya. Deity: Indra. Metre: Triṣṭup.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> भूय॒ इद्वा॑वृधे वी॒र्या॑य॒ एको॑ अजु॒र्यो द॑यते॒  ॑
> वसूनि ।  
> प्र रि॑रिचे दि॒व इंद्रः॑ पृथि॒व्या अ॒र्धमिद॑स्य॒ प्रति॒ रोद॑सी उ॒भे॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> भूयः । इत् । ववृधे । वीर्याय । एकः । अजुर्यः । दयते । वसूनि ।  
> प्र । रिरिचे । दिवः । इंद्रः । पृथिव्याः । अर्धं । इत् । अस्य । प्रति । रोदसी इति ।  
> उभे इति ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमिंद्रो भूय इद्दहुतरमेव वावृधे । प्रवृद्धोऽभूत् । किमर्थं ।
> वीर्याय वीरकर्माणि वृत्रवधादीनि कर्तुं । एको मुख्योऽजुर्यो जरयितुमशक्यः स चेंद्रो वसूनि धनानि दयते । स्तोतृभ्यो ददाति । तथा दिवो
> द्युलोकात् पृथिव्या भूमेश्चायमिंद्रः प्र रिरिचे । अतिरिच्यते । किंजास्येंद्र-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: this Indra grew exceedingly, he became greatly strong. For what purpose? For heroic deeds, to do heroic acts such as the slaying of Vṛtra. The sole chief, undecaying one, who cannot be made to grow old, that Indra gives riches, he gives them to the praisers. Further, this Indra surpasses heaven and earth, he exceeds them. And of this Indra **NOTE**

### Printed p. 12

> स्योभे रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यावर्धमिदर्धमेव प्रति । अस्येंद्रस्यार्धो भागो
> द्यावापृथिव्योः प्रतिनिधिर्भवतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

both heaven and earth are only a half: the half portion of this Indra becomes the counterpart of heaven and earth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—Indra
- भूय इत् (*bhūya it*)—again and again
- वीर्यायॆ (*vīryāyè*)—to accomplish his heroic deeds
- वावृधे (*vāvṛdhe*)—has grown
- एकः (*ekaḥ*)—the chief
- अजुर्यः (*ajuryaḥ*)—and free from old age
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- वसूनि (*vasūni*)—riches
- दयते (*dayate*)—gives
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—than the heavenly world
- पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*)—than the earth
- प्र रिरिचे (*pra ririce*)—has surpassed and grown
- उभे रोदसी (*ubhe rodasī*)—both heaven and earth
- अस्य (*asya*)—to this Indra
- अर्धमिश् प्रति (*ardhamiś prati*)—are in greatness only the measure of a half

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra has grown again and again to accomplish his heroic deeds. Indra, the chief and free from old age, gives riches. He has grown beyond the heavenly world and the earthly world. Heaven and earth are, for this Indra, only the measure of a half in greatness.

##### English translation
Again has Indra increased (in strength) for (the display of) heroism : he, the chief (of all), the undecayable, bestows riches (on his votaries); Indra surpasses heaven and earth: a mere portion of him is equal to both earth and heaven.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अधा॑ मन्ये बृ॒हद॑सु॒र्य॑मस्य॒ यानि॑ दा॒धार॒ नकि॒रा मि॑नाति ।  
> दि॒वेदि॑वे॒ सूर्यो॑ दर्श॒तो भू॒द्वि सद्मा॑न्युर्वि॒या सु॒क्रतु॑र्धात् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अध । मन्ये । बृहत् । असुर्यं । अस्य । यानि । दाधार । नकिः । आ । मिनाति ।  
> दिवेऽदिवे । सूर्यः । दर्शतः । भूत् । वि । सद्मानि । उर्विया । सुक्रतुः । धात् ॥२॥

### Printed p. 13

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अधाधुनास्येंद्रस्य बृहन्महदसुर्यमसुरहननकुशलं बलं मन्ये । सौमि ॥
> मन्यतिः स्तुतिकर्मा ॥ स चेंद्रो यानि कर्माणि दाधार धारयति तानि नकिरा
> मिनाति । न कश्चिदपि हिनस्ति । कानि पुनस्तानि । उच्यते । दिवे दिवे प्रतिदिनं
> वृत्रेणावृतः सूर्यो दर्शतो दर्शनीयो भूत् । भवति । अवरकस्यासुरस्य वधेन
> सूर्यं प्रकाशितवानित्येकं कर्म । सुक्रतुः शोभनकर्मा स इंद्रः सद्मानि
> भुवनान्युर्वियोरूणि विस्तीर्णानि वि धात् । व्यधात् । अकार्षित् । इदमपरं
> कर्म । एतदादीनि बहूनि कर्माणि कृतवानित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: now I praise this Indra's great strength, skilled in the slaying of Asuras; the word मन्यतिः (*manyatiḥ*) is a word for praise **NOTE**. And whatever deeds he has upheld, these no one diminishes, no one harms. And what are they? It is told: day after day the sun, enveloped by Vṛtra, became visible, that is, becomes visible; by the slaying of the obstructing Asura he made the sun shine: this is one deed. The Indra of good deeds extended the worlds, wide and spacious; he made them: this is another deed. The meaning is that he did many deeds, beginning with these.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अध (*adha*)—now
- अस्य (*asya*)—of Indra
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—great
- असुरं (*asuraṃ*)—the strength that destroys enemies
- मन्ये (*manye*)—I praise
- (इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra)
- यानि (*yāni*)—which deeds
- दाधार (*dādhāra*)—he has upheld (those)
- नकिः (*nakiḥ*)—no one
- आ मिनाति (*ā mināti*)—can harm
- दिवेदिवे (*divedive*)—every day
- सूर्यः (*sūryaḥ*)—the sun
- दर्शतः भूत् (*darśataḥ bhūt*)—becomes visible to all (by the grace of Indra)
- सुक्रतुः (*sukratuḥ*)—Indra, the doer of excellent deeds
- सद्मानि (*sadmāni*)—all the worlds
- उर्विया (*urviyā*)—as wide
- वि धात् (*vi dhāt*)—has made

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I now praise the great strength of Indra, which destroys enemies. No one can harm the deeds of Indra. By the grace of this Indra the sun becomes visible to all every day. Indra, the doer of excellent deeds, has made all the worlds wide.

##### English translation
I now glorify his vast and Asura-destroying (vigour) : those exploits that he has determined (to achieve) no one can resist : (by him) the sun was made daily visible; and he, the doer of great deeds, spread out the spacious regions (of the universe).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒द्या चि॒न्नू चि॒त्तदपो॑ न॒दीनां॒ यदा॑भ्यो॒ अर॑दो गा॒तुमिं॑द्र ।  
> नि पर्व॑ता अद्म॒सदो॒ न से॑दु॒स्त्वया॑ दृ॒ळ्हानि॑ सुक्रतो॒ रजां॑सि॥

**NOTE**

### Printed p. 14

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अद्य । चित् । नु । चित् । तत् । अपः । नदीनां । यत् । आभ्यः । आरदः । गातुं ।  
> इंद्र ।  
> नि । पर्वताः । अद्मसदः । न । सेदुः । त्वया । दृळ्हानि । सुक्रतो इति सुक्रतो ।  
> रजांसि ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र अद्या चिदद्यापि नू चित्पुरापि तव नदीनां संबंधिकं तदपः कर्म
> वर्तते । अभ्यो नदीभ्यो गातुं मार्गमरदः प्रवहनार्थं व्यलिख इति यत्कर्म
> तत्सर्वदानुपक्षीणं सद्वर्तत इत्यर्थः । अपि च त्वदाज्ञया पर्वता गिरयो नि
> षेदुः । निषण्णाः । उत्पतनं परित्यज्य क्वचिदेव स्थाने नैश्चल्येनोपविष्टा बभूवुः ।
> अद्मसदो न । अद्मादनीयमन्नं । तदर्थं सीदंतः पुरुषा इव । यथा ते
> भोजनार्थं नैश्चल्येनासते तथेत्यर्थः । अतः कारणात् हे सुक्रतो शोभनकर्मन्निंद्र त्वया रजांसि सर्वे लोका दृळ्हानि दृढीतानि स्थिरीकृता आसन् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, today and in earlier times too, that act of yours that belongs to the rivers continues: the act by which you cut a path, a way, for these rivers to flow, remains always unimpaired. Moreover, at your command the mountains have settled down: abandoning flight, they sat motionless in one place, like men sitting down for the sake of food that is to be eaten, who sit still for the sake of their meal. For this reason, O Indra of good deeds, by you all the worlds were made firm, made steady.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- आभ्यः (*ābhyaḥ*)—for the rivers
- गातुं (*gātuṃ*)—to flow
- अरदः (*aradaḥ*)—you made a way, the act so called
- यत् (*yat*)—which act there is
- नदीनां (*nadīnāṃ*)—belonging to the rivers
- तदपः (*tadapaḥ*)—those acts of yours
- अद्या चित् (*adyā cit*)—just as they are now
- नू चित् (*nū cit*)—were well known in former times too
- अद्मसदो न (*admasado na*)—like men sitting motionless to eat food
- पर्वताः (*parvatāḥ*)—the mountains
- नि षेदुः (*ni ṣeduḥ*)—stand unmoving
- सुक्रतो (*sukrato*)—O Indra, doer of excellent deeds
- त्वया (*tvayā*)—by you alone
- रजांसि (*rajāṃsi*)—all the worlds
- दृळ्हानि (*dṛḷhāni*)—have been made to stand firm

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, those acts of yours that belong to the rivers, the act so called of making a way for the rivers to flow, were well known in former times just as they are now. The mountains stand unmoving, like men sitting motionless to eat food. O Indra, doer of excellent deeds, by you alone all the worlds have been made to stand firm.

##### English translation
At present, verily as of old, that act, (the liberation) of the rivers, is effective ; whereby you have directed them on their course : the mountains

### Printed p. 15
have settled (at your command) like (men) seated at their meals : doer of great deeds, by you, have the worlds been rendered stationary.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

नू चित् (*nū cit*): the particle नू चित् (*nū cit*) is used when the matter of "new" and "old" is indicated. Yāska has given the explanation on this point:
नूचिदिति निपातः पुराणनवयोर्नूचेति च । अद्याचिन्नू चित्तदपो नदीनां । अद्य च पुरा च तदेव कर्म नदीनां । नू च पुरा च सदनं रयीणां रयिरिति धननाम रातेर्दानकर्मणः ॥ (*nūciditi nipātaḥ purāṇanavayornūceti ca | adyācinnū cittadapo nadīnāṃ | adya ca purā ca tadeva karma nadīnāṃ | nū ca purā ca sadanaṃ rayīṇāṃ rayiriti dhananāma rāterdānakarmaṇaḥ ‖*) (Ni. 4-17).
That is: having said that the particle नूचित् (*nūcit*) is used in the sense of new and old, he has given as an example the sentence in the present Ṛk, अद्याचिन्नु चित्तदपो नदीनां (*adyācinnu cittadapo nadīnāṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-30-3). The action of the river, that is, the action of flowing, is of one kind both in former times and now. That is, the way the river used to flow in former times, so it flows now also: this is the meaning. And having said that the words नू च (*nū ca*) also are used in this same sense, for it he gives:
नू च पुरा च सदनं रयाणां जातस्य च जायमानस्य च क्षामं । सतश्च गोपा भवतश्च भूरेर्देवा अग्निं धारयन् द्रविणोदां ॥ (*nū ca purā ca sadanaṃ rayāṇāṃ jātasya ca jāyamānasya ca kṣāmaṃ | sataśca gopā bhavataśca bhūrerdevā agniṃ dhārayan draviṇodāṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-96-7).
In this Ṛk, in the first pāda, the words नू च (*nū ca*) are used. O Agni, named Draviṇoda, you are, in former times and now, the resting place of wealth: this is the meaning.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒त्यमित्तन्न त्वावा॑ अ॒न्नो अ॒स्तींद्र॑ दे॒वो न मर्त्यो॒  ॑
> ज्यायान् ।  
> अह॒न्नहिं॑ परि॒शया॑न॒मणोऽवा॑स्यजो अ॒पो अच्छा॑ समु॒द्रं॥

### Printed p. 16

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सत्यं । इत् । तत् । न । त्वावान् । अन्यः । अस्ति । इंद्र । देवः । न । मर्त्यः ।  
> ज्यायान् ।  
> अहन् । अहिं । परिशयानं । अर्णः । अव । असृजः । अपः । अच्छ । समुद्रं ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र वक्ष्यमाणं तत्सत्यमित्सत्यमेव नानृतं । त्वावान् त्वत्सदृशोऽन्यो
> देवो नास्ति । त्वत्सदृशोऽन्यो मर्त्यो मनुष्योऽपि नैव विद्यते । ज्यायानधिकोऽपि देवो मनुष्यो वा न खल्वस्ति । इति यद्दीयते तत्सत्यमेवेत्यर्थः ।
> अपि च त्वमर्ण उदकं परिशयानं परिवृत्य शयानमहिं मेघमहन् । अवधीः ।
> हत्वा च समुद्रमंतरिक्षं जलधिं वाच्छाभिमुख्येनाप उदकान्यवासृजः । अवाज्मुखतॆया विसृष्टवानसीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, what is about to be said is true, truly so and not false: no other god like you exists; no other mortal, no man either, like you is found; no god or man who is greater, superior, exists. What is said thus is true: this is the meaning. Moreover, you slew the cloud, the serpent that lay encircling the water. And having slain it, you sent forth the waters toward the sea, the atmosphere or the ocean, facing it, that is, you let them go with their mouths turned downward.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वावान् (*tvāvān*)—equal to you
- अन्यः (*anyaḥ*)—other
- देवः (*devaḥ*)—god
- न अस्ति (*na asti*)—there is not
- मर्त्यः (*martyaḥ*)—nor man
- न (*na*)—there is not
- ज्यायान् (*jyāyān*)—no god superior to you: thus
- तत् (*tat*)—that well-known saying
- सत्यमित् (*satyamit*)—is indeed true
- अर्णः (*arṇaḥ*)—the water
- परिशयानं (*pariśayānaṃ*)—lying encircling
- अहिं (*ahiṃ*)—the Asura
- अहन् (*ahan*)—you have slain
- समुद्रं (*samudraṃ*)—in the atmosphere
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—the waters
- अच्छ (*accha*)—in abundance
- अनासृजः (*anāsṛjaḥ*)—you caused to flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O god, the saying that there is no god or man who is superior or equal to you is indeed true. You have slain the Asura named Vṛtra who lay encircling the water, and you have caused the waters of the atmosphere to flow in abundance.

##### English translation
Verily it is the truth, Indra, that there is no other such as you, no god nor mortal is (your) superior: you have slain Ahi obstructing the waters, you have set them free (to flow) to the ocean.

### Printed p. 17
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वम॒पो वि दुरो॒ विषू॑ची॒रिंद्र॑ दृ॒ळ्ह म॑रुजः॒ पर्व॑तस्य ।  
> राजा॑भवो॒ जग॑तश्चर्षणी॒नां सा॒कं सूर्यं॑ ज॒नय॒न्द्यामु॒  ॑
> षासं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वं । अपः । वि । दुरः । विषूचीः । इंद्र । दृळ्णं । अरुजः । पर्वतस्य ।  
> राजा । अभवः । जगतः । चर्षणीनां । साकं । सूर्यं । जनयन् । द्यां । उषसं॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वं दुरो वृत्रेणावृता अपो विषूचीर्विविधमंचंत्यो यथा
> सर्वशः प्रसृता भवंति तथा व्यसृजः । यतस्त्वं पर्वतस्य पर्ववतो मेघस्य दृळं
> दृढमपां बंधकप्रदेशमरुजः अभांक्षीः । अपि च त्वं जगतः संबंधिनीनां
> चर्षणीनां प्रजानां राजाभवः । अधिपतिर्भवसि । किं कुर्वन् । सूर्यं द्यां
> द्युलोकमुषसं च तमसा प्रावृतमेतत्त्रितयं साकं युगपदेव जनयन्
> प्रादुर्भावयन् । प्रकाशयन्नित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, you released the waters enclosed by Vṛtra in such a way that they flow in various directions, that is, spread out on all sides. For you broke open the firm place of the mountain, that is, of the cloud with its joints, which binds the waters. And you became king, lord, of the people of the world. Doing what? Bringing forth together, at one and the same time, the sun, the sky, the heavenly world, and the dawn, these three that were enveloped in darkness; that is, making them manifest, lighting them.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- दुरः (*duraḥ*)—which had been enclosed by Vṛtra
- आपः (*āpaḥ*)—the waters
- विषोचीः (*viṣocīḥ*)—so that they spread everywhere and flow
- वि (*vi*) (असृजः (*asṛjaḥ*))—you released
- पर्वतस्य (*parvatasya*)—of the cloud
- दृळं (*dṛḷaṃ*)—the firm places that bind
- अरुजः (*arujaḥ*)—you broke open
- जगतः (*jagataḥ*)—of the world
- चर्वणीनां (*carvaṇīnāṃ*)—over the people
- राजा (*rājā*)—as lord
- अभवः (*abhavaḥ*)—you have become
- सूर्यं (*sūryaṃ*)—the sun
- द्यां (*dyāṃ*)—the heavenly world
- उषसं (*uṣasaṃ*)—and the dawn
- साकं (*sākaṃ*)—together
- जनयन् (*janayan*)—bringing forth (you have become lord)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you released the waters that had been enclosed by Vṛtra, so that they spread everywhere and flow. You broke open the firm places of the cloud that bind them. Bringing forth together the sun, the heavenly world and the dawn, you have become lord over all the people of the world.

### Printed p. 18

##### English translation
You have set the obstructed waters free to flow in all directions : you have fractured the solid (barrier) of the cloud ; you are lord over the people of the world, making manifest together the sun, the sky, and the dawn.

The thirty-first sūkta

## Sūkta 31 (Maṇḍala 6)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अभूरेक इति पंचर्चमष्टमं सूक्तं भरद्वाजस्य सुहोत्रस्यार्षं ।
> उपांत्या शक्वरी शिष्टाश्च तस्रस्त्रिष्टुभः । इंद्रो देवता । अनुक्रम्यते च । अभूरेकः
> सुहोत्रस्तु चतुर्थी शक्वरीति ॥ महाव्रतस्य निष्केवल्य इदमादीन्यष्ट सूक्तानि
> शंसनीयानि । तथा पंचमारण्यकं । अभूरेको रयिपते रयाणामित्यष्टौ
> सूक्तानि । ऐ. आ. ५-२-२ । इति ॥ पृष्ठस्य षष्ठेऽहनि निष्केवल्य इदं सूक्तं निविद्वानं।
> सूत्रितं च । एंद्र याहुप नः प्र घा न्वस्याभूरेक इति निष्केवल्यं । आ. ६० ।
> इति ॥ विश्वजिति माध्यंदिनेऽच्छावाकशस्त्रे सामसूक्ताख्यमेतच्छंसनीयं । सूत्रितं
> च । अभूरेको रयिपते रयाणामिति सामसूक्तानि । आ. ८-७ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: the sūkta of five Ṛks beginning अभूरेक (*abhūreka*) is the eighth sūkta, with Suhotra of the Bharadvāja family as its seer. The penultimate Ṛk is Śakvarī and the remaining three are Triṣṭubh. The deity is Indra. The Anukramaṇī says: अभूरेकः सुहोत्रस्तु चतुर्थी शक्वरी (*abhūrekaḥ suhotrastu caturthī śakvarī*). In the niṣkevalya of the Mahāvrata the eight sūktas beginning with this one are to be recited; likewise the fifth Āraṇyaka says: अभूरेको रयिपते रयाणामित्यष्टौ सूक्तानि (*abhūreko rayipate rayāṇāmityaṣṭau sūktāni*) (Ai. Ā. 5-2-2). On the sixth day of the Pṛṣṭha rite this sūkta is the one with the nivid in the niṣkevalya. It is stated in the sūtra: एंद्र याहुप नः प्र घा न्वस्याभूरेक इति निष्केवल्यं । (*eṃdra yāhupa naḥ pra ghā nvasyābhūreka iti niṣkevalyaṃ |*) (Ā. 60) **NOTE**. In the Viśvajit, in the Acchāvāka's śastra at the midday pressing, this is to be recited as the Sāmasūkta. It is stated in the sūtra: अभूरेको रयिपते रयाणामिति सामसूक्तानि (*abhūreko rayipate rayāṇāmiti sāmasūktāni*) (Ā. 8-7).

Translation (Anuvāda): This sūkta "Abhūr ekaḥ" is the eighth sūkta in the third anuvāka. In it there are five Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is a person named Suhotra of the Bharadvāja gotra; Indra is the deity; the Ṛk before the last, that is, the fourth Ṛk of this sūkta, is in the Śakvarī metre; the remaining four Ṛks are in the Triṣṭup metre. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: अभूरेकः सुहोत्रस्तु चतुर्थी शक्वरी (*abhūrekaḥ suhotrastu caturthī śakvarī*). In the rite called Mahāvrata, the use of the eight sūktas from this sūkta onward, for the niṣkevalya-śastra mantras, is indicated in the fifth Āraṇyaka of the Aitareya Āraṇyaka by the sentence अभूरेको रयिपते रयाणामित्यष्टौ सूक्तानि (*abhūreko rayipate rayāṇāmityaṣṭau sūktāni*) (Ai. Ā. 5-2-2). On the sixth day of the Pṛṣṭha sacrifice also, this sūkta is to be recited, inserted among the niṣkevalya-śastra mantras as a nivid-bearing sūkta; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, ऐंद्र या ह्युप नः प्र घान्वस्याभूरेक इति निष्केवल्यं (*aiṃdra yā hyupa naḥ pra ghānvasyābhūreka iti niṣkevalyaṃ*) (Ā. 8-1). In the Viśvajit rite also, at the time of the midday pressing, in the śastra-mantras recited by the priest called Acchāvāka, this sūkta is to be recited as a Sāmasūkta; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, अभूरेको रयिपते रयाणामिति सामसूक्तानि (*abhūreko rayipate rayāṇāmiti sāmasūktāni*) (Ā. 8-7).

### Printed p. 19
Sūkta 31. Maṇḍala 6. Anuvāka 3. Sūkta 31. Aṣṭaka 4. Adhyāya 6 **NOTE**. Varga 3. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5. Seer: Suhotra. Deity: Indra. Metre: 1, 3, 5 Triṣṭup; 4 Śakvarī **NOTE**.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अभू॒रेको॑ रयिपते रयी॒णामा हस्त॑योरधिथा इंद्र  ॒
> कृ॒ष्टीः ।  
> वि तो॒के अ॒प्पु तन॑ये च॒ सूरेऽवो॑चंत चर्ष॒णयो॒  ॑
> विवाचः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अभूः । एकः । रयिपते । रयाणां । आ । हस्तयोः । अधिथाः । इंद्र । कृष्टीः ।  
> वि । तोके । अप्सु । तनये । च । :रे । अवोचंत । चर्षणयः । विवाचः ॥१॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे रयिपते धनस्य पालकेंद्र एकः प्रधानभूतस्त्वं रयाणां धनानां
> स्वाम्यभूः । भवसि । यद्वा । रयाणां रयिपत इति संबंधः । एको रयिशब्दोऽनुवादो यथा गवामसि गोपतिरेक इंद्र । ऋग्वे. ७-९८-६ । इति । तदानीमेको
> मुख्यस्त्वं भवसीति योजनीयं । अपि च हे इंद्र त्वं हस्तयोरात्मीययोर्बाह्वोः कृष्णीः प्रजा आधिथाः । आधत्से ! सर्वं जगत्तवाज्ञायां वर्तत
> इत्यर्थः । किंच चर्षणयो मनुष्यास्तोके पुत्रेऽप्सूदकेषु सूरे शत्रूणां
> प्रेरके तनये पौत्रेषु चैतेषु निमित्तभूतेषु सत्सु विवाचो विशिष्टाः स्तुतीर्व्यवोचंत । विविधं ब्रुवंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O lord of wealth, protector of wealth, Indra, you, the single chief, became the master of riches. Or else the connection is "lord of the riches": the word for wealth is repeated, as in यथा गवामसि गोपतिरेक इंद्र (*yathā gavāmasi gopatireka iṃdra*) (Ṛgve. 7-98-6) **NOTE**; then it is to be construed that you alone are the chief. And, O Indra, you hold in your two arms the peoples; that is, the whole world abides in your command. Further, men, in the matters of a son, of waters, of a hero, the impeller of enemies, and of grandsons, these being the occasions, utter various special praises, that is, they speak in manifold ways.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रयिपते (*rayipate*)—O Indra, lord of all wealth
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- एकः (*ekaḥ*)—you, the chief, alone
- रयीणां (*rayīṇāṃ*)—of all wealth
- अभूः (*abhūḥ*)—you have become the master
- हस्तयोः (*hastayoḥ*)—your two arms

### Printed p. 20

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कृष्णी (*kṛṣṇī*)—all the peoples
- आ अधिथाः (*ā adhithāḥ*)—you have lifted up and hold
- चर्षणयः (*carṣaṇayaḥ*)—men
- तोके (*toke*)—for the sake of a son
- अप्पु (*appu*)—for the sake of water
- सूरे (*sūre*)—of the valiant
- तनये (*tanaye*)—for the sake of a grandson
- विवाचः (*vivācaḥ*)—praises of many kinds
- वि अवोचंत (*vi avocaṃta*)—they recite

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, lord of wealth, you alone, the chief among the gods, have become the master of all riches. With your two arms you have lifted up and hold all the peoples (you protect them). Men, for the sake of a son, for the sake of water, and for the sake of valiant grandsons, recite in your name praises of many kinds.

##### English translation
You, lord of riches. are the chief (sovereign) over riches: you hold men in your two hands, and men glorify you with various praises for (the sake of obtaining) sons. and valiant grandsons, and rain.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वद्भि॒येंद्र॒ पार्थि॑वानि॒ विश्वाच्यु॑ता चिच्छावयंते॒ रजां॑सि ।  
> द्यावा॒क्षामा॒ पर्व॑तासो॒ वना॑नि॒ विश्वं॑ दृ॒ळ्णं भ॑यते॒ अज्म॒न्ना  ॑
> ते ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वत् । भिया । इंद्र । पार्थिवानि । विश्वा । अच्युता । चित् । च्यवयंते । रजांसि ।  
> द्यावाक्षामा । पर्वतासः । वनानि । विश्वं । दृळं । भयते । अज्मन् । आ । ते ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वत्त्वत्तो भिया भयेन विश्वा विश्वानि व्यापकानि पार्थिवानि । पृथिव्यंतरिक्षनाम । तत्र भवानि रजांस्युदकान्यच्युता चित् च्यावयितुमशक्यान्यपि
> च्यावयंते । मेघाः प्रच्यावयंति । वर्षंतीत्यर्थः । अपि च हे इंद्र ते तवा
> अज्मन्नागमने सति द्यावाक्षामा द्यावापृथिव्यौ पर्वतासो गिरयो वनानि वृक्षाश्च
> भयं प्राप्नुवंति । किं बहुना विश्वं सर्वं दृश्यं स्थिरं प्राणिजातं भयते बिभेति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, through fear of you the waters (the word for them is explained as the three worlds' waters) that belong to the earth and the mid-region, all-pervading, and by nature impossible to dislodge, are nevertheless made to move: the clouds shake them down; that is, they rain. Further, O Indra, when you come, heaven and earth, the mountains, and the forests, that is the trees, are seized with fear. Why say more: the whole visible, stable world of living creatures trembles. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 21

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वत् (*tvat*)—your
- भिया (*bhiyā*)—through fear
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—all-pervading
- पार्थिवानि (*pārthivāni*)—earth and the rest
- रजांसि (*rajāṃsi*)—the three worlds also
- अच्युता चित् (*acyutā cit*)—though devoid of movement
- च्यावयंते (*cyāvayaṃte*)—shake the clouds and cause rain to pour
- ते (*te*)—your
- अ अज्मन् (*a ajman*)—at the time of coming
- द्यावाक्षामा (*dyāvākṣāmā*)—heaven and earth
- पर्वतासः (*parvatāsaḥ*)—the mountains
- वनानि (*vanāni*)—the trees
- विश्वं (*viśvaṃ*)—all
- दृळ्हं (*dṛḷhaṃ*)—the stable things also
- भयते (*bhayate*)—are seized with fear

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, through fear of you the three worlds, earth and the rest, all-pervading, shake the motionless clouds and cause rain to pour. At the time of your coming, heaven and earth, the mountains, the trees, and all stable things are seized with fear.

##### English translation
Through fear of you, Indra, all the regions of the firmament cause the unfallen (rain) to descend : the heavens, the earth, the mountains, the forests, all the solid (universe) is alarmed at your approach.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वं कुत्से॑ना॒भि शुष्ण॑मिंद्रा॒शुषं॑ युध्य॒ कुय॑वं॒ गवि॑ष्टौ ।  
> दश॑ प्रसि॒त्वे अध॒ सूर्य॑स्य मुषा॒यश्च॒क्रमवि॑वे॒ रपां॑सि ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वं । कुत्सेन । अभि । शुष्णं । इंद्र । अशुषं । युध्य । कुयवं । गोऽइष्टौ ।  
> दश । प्रपित्वे । अध । सूर्यस्य । मुषायः । चक्रं । अविवेः । रपांसि ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वं कुत्सेन सहाशुषं शोषयितुमशक्यं प्रबलं शुष्णं सर्वस्य
> शोषकमेतत्सज्ञमसुरमभि युध्य । अभ्ययुध्यः । कुत्सस्य साहाय्यार्थं तेनासुरेण सह युद्धं कृतवानसीत्यर्थः । तथा गविष्टौ । गावो बाणा इष्यंति गच्छंतीति

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, you, together with Kutsa, fought against the asura named Śuṣṇa, who is unwithering, that is, impossible to wither, mighty, the one who withers everything; the meaning is that to help Kutsa you made war with that asura. Likewise in the *gaviṣṭi*: the "cows" are arrows, which go; hence *gaviṣṭi* is a battle.

### Printed p. 22

> गविष्टिः संग्रामः । तस्मिन्कुयवमेतत्संज्ञमसुरं च दश । अदशः । हिंसितवानसि ॥
> दन्य दशन इत्येतस्माच्छांदसे लोटि रूपं ॥ अधापि च प्रपित्वे प्रपतने युद्धे
> सूर्यस्य चक्रं रथावयवभूतं मुषायः । अमुष्णाः । तदाप्रभृति सूर्यस्य रथ
> एकचक्रोऽभूत् । तथा चाम्नातं । सप्त युंजंति रथमेकचक्रं । ऋग्वे. १.१६४-२ ।
> इति । एतशाख्यस्य ऋषेः सूर्येण सह युद्धे तत्साहाय्यार्थमागत इंद्रः
> सूर्यस्य चक्रमपहृतवानित्याख्यातं । सूरश्चक्रं प्र वृहज्जात ओजसा । ऋग्वे.
> १-१३०-९ । इति च निगमांतरं । अपि च रपांसि पापकारीणि रक्षःप्रभृतीन्यविवेः ।
> अस्माल्लोकादगमयः । अवधीरित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In that battle you also slew the asura named Kuyava. Further, in the battle, the "falling" (*prapitva*), you carried off the wheel of the sun, a part of his chariot; from that time the sun's chariot has had one wheel. So it is handed down: सप्त युंजंति रथमेकचक्रं (*sapta yuṃjaṃti rathamekacakraṃ*) (Ṛgve. 1.164-2). It is related that when the seer called Etaśa fought with the sun, Indra came to his help and took away the sun's wheel; and another passage of the Veda says: सूरश्चक्रं प्र वृहज्जात ओजसा (*sūraścakraṃ pra vṛhajjāta ojasā*) (Ṛgve. 1-130-9). Moreover, you drove the sinful ones, the rākṣasas and the rest, away from this world; that is, you slew them.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- कुत्सेन (*kutsena*)—together with Kutsa
- अशुषं (*aśuṣaṃ*)—the mighty
- शुष्णं (*śuṣṇaṃ*)—the asura called Śuṣṇa, facing him
- अभि युध्य (*abhi yudhya*)—you have waged war
- गविष्टौ (*gaviṣṭau*)—in the battle for cows
- कुयवं (*kuyavaṃ*)—the asura called Kuyava
- दश (*daśa*)—you have slain
- अध (*adha*)—afterwards
- प्रपित्वे (*prapitve*)—at the time of battle
- सूर्यस्य (*sūryasya*)—of the sun
- चक्रं (*cakraṃ*)—the wheel
- मुषायः (*muṣāyaḥ*)—you have carried off
- रपांसि (*rapāṃsi*)—the sinful rākṣasas
- अविवेः (*aviveḥ*)—you have driven far away

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, together with Kutsa you have waged war against the mighty asura called Śuṣṇa, facing him. In the battle for cows you have slain the asura called Kuyava. At the time of battle you have carried off the wheel of the sun, and you have driven the sinful rākṣasas far away.

##### English translation
You, Indra, with Kutsa, have warred against the inexhaustible Sushna: you have ovhrthrown Kuyava in battle : in conflict you have carried off the wheel (of the chariot) of the sun : you have driven away the malignant (spirits).

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वं श॒तान्यव॒ शंब॑रस्य॒ पुरो॑ जघंथाप्र॒तीनि॒ दस्योः॑ ।  
> अशि॑क्षो॒ यत्र॒ शच्या॑ शचीवो॒ दिवो॑दासाय सुन्व॒ते सु॑तक्रे  
> भ॒रद्वा॑जाय गृण॒ते वसू॑नि ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 23

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वं । शतानि । अव । शंबरस्य । पुरः । जघंथ । अप्रतीनि । दस्योः ।  
> अशिक्षः । यत्र । शच्या । शचीवः । दिवःदासाय । सुन्वते । सुतक्रे । भरत्वाजाय ।  
> गृणते । वसूनि ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वं शतानि शतसंख्यानि दस्योरुपक्षपयितुरसुरस्य शंबरस्यैतत्संज्ञस्य
> पुरः पुरीरप्रतीनि ॥ लिंगव्यत्ययः ॥ केनाप्यप्रतिगता अव जघंथ । अवाहन् । हे
> शचीवः प्रज्ञावन् हे सुतक्रे अभिषुतेन सोमेन क्रीतेंद्र यत्र यस्मिन्काले
> सुन्वते सोमाभिषवं कुर्वते दिवोदासाय शच्या प्रज्ञयाशिक्षो धनानि प्रादा
> गृणते स्तुवते भरद्वाजाय च वसूनि प्रादाः तस्मिन्काले शांबरीः पुरोऽव
> जघंथेत्यन्वयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, you struck down the hundred cities of the Dasyu, the asura named Śambara, the destroyer; the cities were such that nobody could withstand them. O possessor of wisdom (*śacīvān*), O Sutakra, Indra, bought with the pressed Soma: at the time when you, by your wisdom, gave riches to Divodāsa who was pressing the Soma, and gave riches also to Bharadvāja who praises you, at that time you struck down the cities of Śambara. This is the construction.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शचीवः (*śacīvaḥ*)—O wise one
- सुतक्रे (*sutakre*)—O Indra, bought with Soma
- यत्र (*yatra*)—at which time
- सुन्वते (*sunvate*)—to the one pressing the Soma juice
- दिवोदासाय (*divodāsāya*)—to Divodāsa
- शच्या (*śacyā*)—by wisdom
- अशिक्षः (*aśikṣaḥ*)—you gave wealth
- गृणते (*gṛṇate*)—to the one who praises you
- भरद्वाजायॆ (*bharadvājāyè*)—to Bharadvāja
- वसूनि (*vasūni*)—riches (you gave), at that time
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- दस्योः (*dasyoḥ*)—of the violent one
- शंबरस्य (*śaṃbarasya*)—of Śambara
- शतानि (*śatāni*)—hundreds
- अप्रतीनि (*apratīni*)—unassailable
- पुरः (*puraḥ*)—cities
- अव जघंथ (*ava jaghaṃtha*)—you tore down and destroyed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O wise Indra, bought with Soma: at the time when you gave wealth, by your wisdom, to Divodāsa who presses the Soma juice, and gave riches to Bharadvāja who praises you, at that time you tore down and destroyed the hundred unassailable cities of the violent Śambara.

##### English translation
You have destroyed the hundred impregnable cities of the Dasyu, Sambara, when, sagacious Indra, you, who are brought by the libation, have bestowed in your liberality riches upon Divodasa presenting to you libations, and upon Bharadwaja hymning your praise.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स स॑त्यसत्त्वन्मह॒ते रणा॑य॒ रथ॒मा ति॑ष्ठ तुविनृष्ण भी॒मं ।  
> या॒हि प्र॑पथि॒न्नव॒सोप॑ म॒द्रिक्प्र च॑ श्रुत श्रावय चर्ष॒णिभ्यः॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । सत्यसत्वन् । महते । रणाय । रथं । आ । तिष्ठ । तुविनृम्न । भीमं ।  
> याहि । प्रपथिन् । अवसा । उप । मद्रिक् । प्र । च । श्रुत । श्रवय । चर्षणिभ्यः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सत्यसत्वन् । सत्याः सत्वानो भटा यस्य स तथोक्तः । हे तुविन्नम्म ।
> तुवि बहुलं नृम्मं धनं यस्य स तथोक्तः । हे तादृशेंद्र स त्वं महते रणाय
> संग्रामाय तदर्थं भीमं भयंकरमात्मीयं रथमा तिष्ठ । हे प्रपथिन् प्रकृष्टमार्गेंद्र अवसा रक्षणेन सह तेन च रथेनोप याहि आगच्छ मदभिमुखः।
> कथा हे श्रुत विश्रुतेंद्र चर्षणिभ्यः प्रजाभ्यः प्र श्रावय च । अस्मान् प्रख्यापय
> च । सर्वासु प्रजासु मध्येऽस्मान्प्रख्यातान् कुर्वित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, you whose warriors are true (*satyasatvan*), and whose wealth is abundant (*tuvinṛmṇa*): you, such as you are, mount your own formidable, terrible chariot for the great battle. O Indra of the excellent road (*prapathin*), come with that chariot, with your protection, towards me. And, O renowned Indra, make us heard of among the peoples, that is, make us famous; the meaning is: make us celebrated in the midst of all the subjects.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सत्यसत्वन् (*satyasatvan*)—O you who have heroes of true strength
- तुविनृम्म (*tuvinṛmma*)—O Indra, possessor of abundant wealth
- सः (*saḥ*)—you
- महते (*mahate*)—for the great
- रणाय (*raṇāya*)—battle
- भीमं (*bhīmaṃ*)—terrible
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—chariot
- आ तिष्ठ (*ā tiṣṭha*)—mount and sit
- प्रपथिन् (*prapathin*)—O Indra of the excellent road
- अवसा (*avasā*)—with your protection
- मद्रिक् (*madrik*)—towards me
- उप याहि (*upa yāhi*)—be gracious
- श्रुत (*śruta*)—O renowned Indra
- चर्षणिभ्यः (*carṣaṇibhyaḥ*)—before the other peoples
- प्र श्रावय (*pra śrāvaya*)—make us famous

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, who have heroes of true strength and abundant wealth, mount and sit in your terrible chariot for the great battle. O Indra of the excellent road, come towards us together with your protection. O renowned Indra, make us famous before the other peoples.

### Printed p. 25

##### English translation
Leader of true heroes, possessor of infinite wealth, mount your formidable car for the arduous conflict : come to me, pursuer of a forward path, for my protection: do you, who are renowned, proclaim (our renown) amongst men.

The thirty-second sūkta

## Sūkta 32 (Maṇḍala 6)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अपूर्व्येति पंचर्चं नवमं सूक्तं त्रैष्टुभमैंद्रं । द्वितीये छांदो
> मिकेऽहनि निष्केवल्य एतत्सूक्तं । सूत्रितं च । अपूर्व्या पुरुतमानि तां सु ते
> कीर्तिं । आ. ८-७ । इति ॥ महाव्रतेऽपि निष्केवल्येऽस्य सूक्तस्य विनियोग उक्तः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta of five Ṛks beginning अपूर्व्या (*apūrvyā*) is the ninth sūkta; it is in the Triṣṭubh metre and addressed to Indra. On the second Chandoma day this sūkta belongs to the Niṣkevalya litany. It is also laid down in the sūtra: अपूर्व्या पुरुतमानि तां सु ते कीर्तिं (*apūrvyā purutamāni tāṃ su te kīrtiṃ*) (Ā. 8-7). The application of this sūkta to the Niṣkevalya litany in the Mahāvrata is also stated.

Anuvāda: This sūkta, beginning with अपूर्व्या (*apūrvyā*), is the ninth sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has five Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Suhotra, the seer of the preceding sūkta; the deity is Indra; the metre is Triṣṭup. In the Anukramaṇī it is stated as beginning with अपूर्व्या (*apūrvyā*). That this sūkta is applied to the Niṣkevalya-śastra mantras on the day of reciting the second Chandoma mantras is set out in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra by the sūtra अपूर्व्या पुरुतमानि तां सु ते कीर्तिं (*apūrvyā purutamāni tāṃ su te kīrtiṃ*) (Ā. 8-7). It has been said earlier that this sūkta is applied to the Niṣkevalya-śastra mantras also in the rite called Mahāvrata.

Sūkta 32. Maṇḍala 6. Anuvāka 3. Sūkta 32. Aṣṭaka 4. Adhyāya 7. Varga 4. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5. Seer: Suhotra. Deity: Indra. Metre: Triṣṭup.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अपू॑र्व्यान् पुरु॒षमा॑न्यस्यै म॒हे वी॒राय॑ त॒वसे॑ तु॒राय॑ ।  
> वि॒र॒प्पिने॑ व॒ज्रणे॒ शंत॑मानि॒ वचां॑स्या॒सा स्थवि॑राय तक्षं ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 26

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अपूर्व्या । पुरुतमानि । अस्मै । महे । वीराय । तवसे । तुराय ।  
> विरप्यिने । वज्रिणे । शंतमानि । वचांसि । आसा । स्थविराय । तक्षं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अपूर्व्यापूर्व्याणि पूर्वैरकृतानि नूतनानि पुरुतमानि बहुतमानि शंतमानि
> सुखकृत्तमानि वचांसि स्तुतिरूपाणि वाक्यान्यासास्येनास्मा इंद्राय तक्षं । अशक्षं ।
> करोमि । कीदृशाय । महे महते वीराय विविधशत्रूणां मारयित्रे तवसे
> तवस्विने बलवते तुराय त्वरमाणाय विरष्यने विशेषेण स्तुत्याय वज्रिणे वज्रवते
> स्थविराय प्रवृद्धाय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

I fashion with my mouth for this Indra words of praise that are unprecedented, that is, new, not made by earlier singers; most numerous; and most productive of happiness. For what kind of Indra: great; a hero, the slayer of various enemies; mighty; swift; to be praised in a special degree **NOTE**; the wielder of the thunderbolt; and grown great.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- महे (*mahe*)—who is of great nature
- वीराय (*vīrāya*)—the valiant
- तवसे (*tavase*)—the mighty
- तुराय (*turāya*)—the swift
- विरप्पिने (*virappine*)—the greatly praised
- वज्रिणे (*vajriṇe*)—the bearer of the thunderbolt
- स्थविराय (*sthavirāya*)—the one who is growing
- अस्यॆ (*asyè*)—to this Indra
- अपूर्व्या (*apūrvyā*)—unprecedented
- पुरुतमानि (*purutamāni*)—very abundant
- शंतमानि (*śaṃtamāni*)—productive of happiness
- वचांसि (*vacāṃsi*)—words of praise
- आसा (*āsā*)—from my mouth
- तक्षं (*takṣaṃ*)—I fashion

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

To Indra, who is of great nature, valiant, mighty, swift, greatly praised, the bearer of the thunderbolt, and growing in strength, I fashion from my mouth words of praise that are unprecedented (new), most abundant, and productive of happiness.

##### English translation
I have fabricated with my mouth unprecedented, comprehensive, and gratifying prasies to that mighty, heroic, powerful, rapid, adorable, and ancient wielder of the thunderbolt.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स मा॒तरा॒ सूर्ये॑णा कवी॒नामवा॑सयद्रु॒जदद्रिं॑ गृणा॒नः ।  
> स्वा॒धीभि॒र्ऋक्व॑भिर्वावशा॒न उदु॒स्रिया॑णामसृजन्नि॒ दानं॑ ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 27

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । मातरा । सूर्येण । कवीनां । अवासयत् । रुजत् । अद्रिं । गृणानः ।  
> सुआधीभिः । ऋक्वभिः । वावशानः । उत् । उस्रियाणां । असृजत् । निदानं ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स इंद्रो मातरा मातरौ दिवं च पृथिवीं च कवीनां क्रांतदर्शिनामंगिरसामर्थाय सूर्येणावासयत् । प्राकाशयत् । किं कुर्वन् । अद्रिं गवामदर्शनाय बलेन स्थापितं पर्वतं रुजद्भंजन् । यद्वा । कवीनामंगिरसामिति
> तृतीयार्थे षष्ठी । अंगिरोभिर्गृणानः स्तूयमान इति संबंधः । अपि च स्वाधीभिः
> शोभनध्यानैर्युक्वभिः स्तोतृभिरंगिरोभिर्वावशानः पुनःपुनः काम्यमानः सन्
> उस्रियाणां वलस्य भृत्यैः पणिभिरपहृतानां गवां निदानं बंधनमुदसृजत् ।
> अमोचयत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Indra made the two mothers, heaven and earth, shine with the sun, for the sake of the far-seeing Aṅgirases. How did he do so? By splitting the mountain that had been set by force so that the cows could not be seen. Or else, the genitive "of the far-seeing Aṅgirases" stands for the instrumental sense, and the connection is: he was praised by the Aṅgirases. Further, he was repeatedly desired by the Aṅgirases, the singers, who are of good meditation, and so he released the confinement of the cows that had been carried off by the Paṇis, the servants of Vala.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सः (*saḥ*)—Indra
- कवीनां (*kavīnāṃ*)—by the far-seeing Aṅgirases
- गृणानः (*gṛṇānaḥ*)—being praised
- अद्रिं (*adriṃ*)—the mountain
- रुजन् (*rujan*)—splitting
- मातरा (*mātarā*)—heaven and earth
- सूर्येण (*sūryeṇa*)—with the help of the sun
- आवासयत् (*āvāsayat*)—he made shine
- स्वाधीभिः (*svādhībhiḥ*)—by those intent on excellent meditation
- ऋक्वभिः (*ṛkvabhiḥ*)—and by the singers of praise, the Aṅgirases
- नावशानः (*nāvaśānaḥ*)—desired again and again
- उस्रियाणां (*usriyāṇāṃ*)—of the cows that had been carried off
- निदानं (*nidānaṃ*)—the bond
- उदसृजत् (*udasṛjat*)—he released

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, praised by the far-seeing Aṅgirases, splitting the mountain, made heaven and earth shine with the help of the sun. Desired again and again by the Aṅgirases, who are intent on excellent meditation and are singers of praise, he released the bond of the cows that had been carried off.

##### English translation
He has obtained :the parent (worlds, heaven and earth), with the sun, for the sake of the sages, (the Angirasas), and glorified (by them). he has shattered the mountain ; repeatedly wished for by his adorers intently meditating (upon him), he has cast off the fetters of the cows.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स वह्नि॑भि॒र्ऋक्व॑भि॒र्गोषु॒ शश्व॑न्म॒तज्नु॑भिः पुरु॒कृत्वा॑ जिगाय ।  
> पुरः॑ पुरो॒हा सखि॑भिः सखी॒यन्‌दृ॒ळ्हा रु॑रोज क॒विभिः॑क॒विः  
> सन् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । वह्निभिः । ऋक्वभिः । गोषु । शश्वत् । मितज्नुभिः । पुरुकृत्वा । जिगाय ।  
> पुरः । पुरःहा । सखिभिः । सखियान् । दृळ्हाः । रुरोज । कविभिः । कविः । सन् ॥३॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पुरुकृत्वा बहुकर्मकृत्स इंद्रो वह्निभिर्हविषां वोढृभिर्यक्वभिः स्तोतृभिः
> शश्वत्सर्वदा मितज्नुभिः संकुचितजानुभिरंगिरोभिः सह गोषु निमित्तभूतेषु
> जिगाय । असुरान् जितवान् । जित्वा च पुरोहा पुराणां हंता स इंद्रः सखिभिः
> समानख्यानैः कविभिः क्रांतप्रज्ञैरंगिरोभिः सह सखीयन् सखित्वमात्मन इच्छन्
> कविः सन् स्वयमपि क्रांतप्रज्ञो भवन् दृळ्हाः स्थिराः पुर आसुरीः पुरी रुरोज ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Indra, the doer of many deeds, together with the Aṅgirases, bearers of the oblations, singers of praise, who always bend the knee, conquered the asuras on account of the cows. Having conquered, the destroyer of cities, Indra, wishing friendship with his friends, the Aṅgirases, who are of the same fame and far-seeing in wisdom, and being himself a seer, far-seeing in wisdom, broke the firm cities of the asuras.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुकृत्वा (*purukṛtvā*)—doer of many deeds
- सः (*saḥ*)—Indra
- वह्निभिः (*vahnibhiḥ*)—with those who bear the oblations
- ऋक्वभिः (*ṛkvabhiḥ*)—the singers of praise
- शश्वत् (*śaśvat*)—always
- मितज्जुभिः (*mitajjubhiḥ*)—who bow down, kneeling, the Aṅgirases
- गोषु (*goṣu*)—on account of the cows
- जिगाय (*jigāya*)—he conquered the asuras
- पुरोहा (*purohā*)—Indra, destroyer of the enemy's cities
- सखिभिः (*sakhibhiḥ*)—with those who are his friends
- कविभिः (*kavibhiḥ*)—and far-seeing, the Aṅgirases
- सखीयन् (*sakhīyan*)—desiring friendship
- कविः सन् (*kaviḥ san*)—himself also, being far-seeing
- दृळ्हाः (*dṛḷhāḥ*)—exceedingly hard
- पुरः (*puraḥ*)—the cities of the enemy
- रुरोज (*ruroja*)—he destroyed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, the doer of many deeds, together with the Aṅgirases, who bear the oblations, sing praises, and always bow down kneeling, conquered the asuras on account of the cows. Indra, the destroyer of the enemy's cities, desiring friendship with the Aṅgirases, his friends, who are far-seeing, and being himself far-seeing, destroyed the exceedingly hard cities of the enemy.

### Printed p. 29

##### English translation
He, the achiever of many deeds, together with his worshippers ever offering oblations upon bended knees, has overcome (the Asuras) for (the rescue of) the cows: friendly with his friends (the Angirasas), far-seeing with the far-seeing, the destroyer of cities has demolished the strong cities (of the Asuras).

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स नी॒व्या॑भिर्जरि॒तार॒मच्छा॑ म॒हो वाजे॑भिर्म॒हद्भि॑श्च॒ शुष्मैः॑ ।  
> पु॒रु॒वीरा॑भिर्वृषभ क्षिती॒नामा गि॑र्वणः सुवि॒ताय॒ प्र या॑हि ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । नीव्याभिः । जरितारं । अच्छ । महः। वाजेभिः । महत् 5भिः । च । शुष्मैः ।  
> पुरुऽवीराभिः । वृषभ । क्षितीनां । आ । गिर्वणः । सुविताय । प्र । याहि ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वृषभ कामानां वर्षक हे गिर्वणो गिरा स्तुत्या संभजनीयेंद्र स
> त्वं महो महद्भिर्वाजेभिर्वाजैरन्नैर्महद्धिः शुष्मैर्बलैश्च सह क्षितीनां
> प्रजानां मध्यॆ जरितारं स्तोतारमच्छाभिमुख्येन नीव्याभिर्नव्याभिर्नवतराभिः
> पुरुवीराभिः पुरुणां बहूनां वीरयित्रिभिर्वडवाभिरा प्र याहि । आगच्छ ।
> किमर्थं । सुविताय शोभनाय । सुखप्राप्त्यर्थमित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O showerer of desires, O Indra who are to be served with song, you, together with great food and great strengths, come, in the midst of the peoples, towards the praiser, with new mares that bring forth many heroes. What for? For a good thing, that is, for the attainment of happiness.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृषभ (*vṛṣabha*)—O showerer of desires
- गिर्वणः (*girvaṇaḥ*)—O Indra, honoured with praises
- सः (*saḥ*)—you
- महद्भिः (*mahadbhiḥ*)—great
- वाजेभिः (*vājebhiḥ*)—with foods
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—exceeding
- शुष्मैः (*śuṣmaiḥ*)—with strengths
- च (*ca*)—and
- नीव्याभिः (*nīvyābhiḥ*)—new
- पुरुवीराभिः (*puruvīrābhiḥ*)—accompanied by many heroes, with horses
- जरितारं अच्छ (*jaritāraṃ accha*)—towards the praiser
- क्षितीनां (*kṣitīnāṃ*)—in the midst of the peoples
- सुविताय (*suvitāya*)—for his welfare
- आ प्र याहि (*ā pra yāhi*)—be gracious and come

### Printed p. 30

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, showerer of desires and honoured with praises, with great foods and exceeding strengths, and with new horses accompanied by many heroes, be gracious and come, in the midst of the peoples, towards the praiser, for his welfare.

##### English translation
Showerer (of benefits), propitiated by praise, come to him who glorifies you, to make him happy amongst men with abundant food, with exceeding strength, and with young (mares) with numerous colts.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स सर्गे॑ण॒ शव॑सा त॒क्तो अत्यै॑र॒प इंद्रो॑ दक्षिण॒तस्तु॑रा॒षाट् ।  
> इ॒त्था सृ॑जा॒ना अन॑पावृ॒दर्थं॑ दि॒वेदि॑वे विविषुरप्रमृ॒ष्यं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । सर्गेण । शवसा । तक्तः । अत्यैः । अपः । इंद्रः । दक्षिणतः । तुराषाट् ।  
> इत्था । सृजानाः । अनपवृत् । अर्थं । दिवेऽदिवे । विविषुः । अप्रमृष्यं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तुराषाट् तुराणां हिंसकानां सोढाभिभविता स इंद्रः सर्गेण सर्वदोद्युक्तेन शवसा बलेनात्यैरश्वैः सततगामिभिस्तेजोभिर्वा तक्तः संगतः सन्
> दक्षिणतो दक्षिणायनेऽप उदकानि विसृजतीति शेषः । इत्येत्थमनेन प्रकारेण
> सृजानाः सृज्यमानास्ता आपोऽर्थं गंतव्यमप्रमृष्यमन्वैरक्षोभ्यं समुद्रं
> दिवे दिवे प्रतिदिनमनसावृत् अपावर्तनं पुनरागमनं यथा न भवति तथा विविषुः ।
> व्याप्नुवंति ॥ अर्तेस्थन्प्रत्ययांतस्य रूपमर्थमिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, the overcomer of the violent, united with his strength that is always ready for action, and with swift horses or with ever-moving lights, in the southern course releases the waters; this is to be supplied. Thus released, those waters go each day to their goal, the ocean, which is not stirred by others, in such a way that there is no turning back, no return; they pervade it. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- तुराषाट् (*turāṣāṭ*)—who defeats enemies
- सः इंद्रः (*saḥ iṃdraḥ*)—that same Indra
- सर्गेण (*sargeṇa*)—natural to him
- शवसा (*śavasā*)—with strength
- अत्यैः (*atyaiḥ*)—with horses
- तक्तः (*taktaḥ*)—united
- दक्षिणतः (*dakṣiṇataḥ*)—in the southern course
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—the waters (he made them flow)
- इत्या (*ityā*)—in this manner

### Printed p. 31

- सृजानाः (*sṛjānāḥ*)—the waters released from bondage
- अर्थं (*arthaṃ*)—their destination, too
- अप्रमृष्यं (*apramṛṣyaṃ*)—the ocean, which does not dry up
- दिवे दिवे (*dive dive*)—every day
- अनपावृत् (*anapāvṛt*)—without return
- विविषुः (*viviṣuḥ*)—they enter

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

That same Indra, the assailer of enemies, united with horses, with his natural strength, released the waters in the southern course and made them flow. In this manner the waters, released from bondage, enter every day, without return, the ocean, their destination, which does not dry up.

##### English translation
Endowed with natural force, possessed of (swift) horses, Indra, the overcomer of adversaries, (sets free) the waters at the southern (declination): you liberated the waters expanded daily to the insatiable goal whence there is no returning.

The thirty-third sūkta

## Sūkta 33 (Maṇḍala 6)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> य ओजिष्ठ इति पंचर्चं दशमं सूक्तं शुनहोत्रस्यार्षं त्रिष्टुभमैंद्रं ।
> अनुक्रम्यते च । य ओजिष्ठः शुनहोत्रस्त्विति ॥ महाव्रतेऽपि निष्केवल्य उक्तो
> विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta of five Ṛks beginning य ओजिष्ठः (*ya ojiṣṭhaḥ*) is the tenth sūkta, with Śunahotra as its seer; it is in the Triṣṭubh metre and addressed to Indra. The Anukramaṇī says: य ओजिष्ठः शुनहोत्रस्त्विति (*ya ojiṣṭhaḥ śunahotrastviti*). The application to the Niṣkevalya litany in the Mahāvrata has also been stated.

Anuvāda: This sūkta, beginning with य ओजिष्ठः (*ya ojiṣṭhaḥ*), is the tenth sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has five Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Śunahotra, born in the Bhāradvāja lineage; the metre is Triṣṭup. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: य ओ जिष्ठः शुनहोत्रस्तु (*ya o jiṣṭhaḥ śunahotrastu*). In the rite called Mahāvrata this sūkta is applied to the Niṣkevalya-śastra mantras.

Sūkta 33. Maṇḍala 6. Anuvāka 3. Sūkta 23 **NOTE**. Aṣṭaka 4. Adhyāya 7. Varga 5. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5. Seer: Śunahotra. Deity: Indra. Metre: Triṣṭup.

### Printed p. 32

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> य ओजि॑ष्ठ इंद्र॒ तं सु नो॑ दा॒ मदो॑ वृषन्तस्वभि॒ष्टिर्दास्वा॑न् ।  
> सौव॑श्व्यं॒ यो व॒नव॒त्स्वश्वो॑ वृ॒त्रा स॒मत्तु॑ सा॒सह॑द॒मित्रा॑न्॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । ओजिष्ठः । इंद्र । तं । सु । नः । दाः । मदः । वृषन् । सुअभिष्टिः । दास्वान् ।  
> सौवश्व्यं । यः । वनवत् । सुअश्वः । वृत्रा । समत्सु । ससहत् । अमित्रान् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वृषन् कामानां वर्षयितरिंद्र त्वं यः पुत्र ओजिष्ठ ओजस्वितमो बलवत्तमो मदस्तव मादयिता । स्तुतिभिः स्तोतेत्यर्थः । स्वभिष्टिः शोभनाभ्येषणो
> दास्वान् । हविषां दानं दाः । तद्वान् । एवंगुणविशिष्टं तं पुत्रं नोऽस्मभ्यं
> सु ; सुष्टु दाः । देहि । यः पुत्रः स्वश्वः सन् समत्सु संग्रामेषु सौवश्वं
> शोभनाश्वानां समूहं वनवत् हिंस्यात् । तथा वृत्रा वृत्रान् वारयित्यनमित्रान्
> शत्रुंश्च सासहत् अत्यर्थमभिभवेत् तं दा इत्यन्वयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, showerer of desires: give us well that son who is most mighty and strong, your gladdener, that is, one who delights you by his praises; of good purpose (*svabhiṣṭi*, "one whose seeking is fair") **NOTE**; and a giver, that is, one who gives oblations. Give us a son endowed with such qualities, who, being possessed of good horses, shall in battles destroy the host of the enemy's good horses, and shall utterly overcome the obstructing enemies. This is the construction: give us him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृषन् (*vṛṣan*)—O showerer of desires
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- यः (*yaḥ*)—the son who
- ओजिष्ठः (*ojiṣṭhaḥ*)—is exceedingly mighty
- मदः (*madaḥ*)—a giver of delight to you by praise
- स्वभिष्टिः (*svabhiṣṭiḥ*)—an excellent sacrificer
- दास्वान् (*dāsvān*)—an offerer of oblations
- यः (*yaḥ*)—the son who
- स्वश्वः (*svaśvaḥ*)—possessed of good horses
- समत्सु (*samatsu*)—in battles
- सौवश्वं (*sauvaśvaṃ*)—the good horses of the enemies
- वनवत् (*vanavat*)—destroying
- वृत्रा (*vṛtrā*)—who are hostile
- अमित्रान् (*amitrān*)—the enemies
- सासहत् (*sāsahat*)—shall defeat
- तं (*taṃ*)—such a son
- सु (*su*)—abundantly
- दाः (*dāḥ*)—grant to us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, showerer of desires: the son who is exceedingly mighty, a giver of delight to you by praise, an excellent sacrificer, and an offerer of oblations, and who, possessed of good horses, shall in battles destroy the enemies' good horses and defeat the hostile enemies: grant us such a son.

### Printed p. 33

##### English translation
Showerer (of benefits), Indra, grant us a son who shall be most vigorous, a delighter (of you by praise), a pious sacrificer, a liberal giver, who mounted on a good steed, shall overthrow numerous good steeds, and conquer opposing enemies in combats.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वां ही॒३॒ं॑३ द्राव॑से॒ विवा॑चो॒ हवं॑ते चर्ष॒णयः॒ शूर॑सात् ।  
> त्वं विप्रे॑भि॒र्वि प॒णीर॑शाय॒स्वोत॒ इत्सनि॑ता॒ वाज॒मवर् ॥ २ ॥॑

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वां । हि । इंद्र । अवसे । विवाचः । हवंते । चर्षणयः । शूरसातौ ।  
> त्वं । विप्रेभिः । वि । पणीन् । अशायः । त्वाऊतः । इत् । सनिता । वाजं । अर्वा॥२॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वां हि त्वामेव विवाचो विविधाः स्तुतिरूपा वाचो येषां
> तादृशाश्वर्षणयो मनुष्याः शूरसात् शूरैः संभजनीये संग्रामेवसे
> रक्षणार्थं हवंते । आह्वयंति । किंच त्वं विप्रेभिर्विप्रैर्मेधाविभिरंगिरोभिः
> सार्धं पणीन् । वलस्यानुचरा असुराः पणयः । तान्वशायः । विशेषेणाशाययः।
> हतवानित्यर्थः । अतस्तोत इत् त्वयोतो रक्षित एव सनिता संभक्ता पुरुषो
> वाजमन्नमर्वाभिगंता भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, it is you alone whom men, who have various praises as their speech, call to their protection in the battle that is to be shared by heroes. And you, together with the wise Aṅgirases, destroyed the Paṇis, the asura followers of Vala; the meaning is that you slew them. Therefore the man who is protected by you, the one who shares in the worship, becomes one who obtains food.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- विनाचः (*vinācaḥ*)—men of various kinds of praises
- चर्षणयः (*carṣaṇayaḥ*)—men
- शूरसात् (*śūrasāt*)—in battle
- अवसे (*avase*)—for protection
- त्वां हि (*tvāṃ hi*)—you alone
- हवंते (*havaṃte*)—they invoke with prayer
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- विप्रेभिः (*viprebhiḥ*)—together with the wise Aṅgirases
- पणीन् (*paṇīn*)—the Paṇis
- वि आशायः (*vi āśāyaḥ*)—you destroyed
- त्वोत इत् (*tvota it*)—only the man protected by you
- सनिता (*sanitā*)—as your worshipper
- वाजं (*vājaṃ*)—food
- अर्वा (*arvā*)—obtains

### Printed p. 34

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, men who have various kinds of praises invoke you alone with prayer for protection in battle. You, together with the wise Aṅgirases, destroyed the Paṇis. Only the man protected by you, as your worshipper, obtains food.

##### English translation
Men of various speech invoke you, Indra, for their defence in war ; you, with the sages, (the Angirasas), have slain the Panis : protected by you, the liberal (worshipper) obtains food.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वं ता इं॑द्रो॒भया॑ अ॒मित्रा॒न्दासा॑ वृ॒त्राण्यार्या॑ च शूर ।  
> वधी॒र्वने॑व॒ सुधि॑तेभि॒रत्कै॒रा पृ॒त्सु द॑र्षि नृ॒णां नृ॑तम ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वं! तान् । इंद्र । उभयान् । अमित्रान् । दासा । वृत्राणि । आर्या । च । शूर ।  
> वधीः । वनाइव । सुधितेभिः । अत्कैः । आ । पृत्सु । दर्षि । नृणां । नृतम ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वं तानुभयानुभयविधानमित्रान् शत्रुन्वधीः । अहिंसीः । के त
> उभयविधाः । दासोपक्षपयित्यन्नर्मविरोधिनो वलप्रभृतीनसुरान् ॥ सुपां
> सुलुगिति शस आकारः ॥ आर्यायाणि कर्मानुष्ठातृत्वेन श्रेष्ठानि वृत्राण्यावरकाणि
> विश्वरूपादीनि च तानुभयविधान् हे शूर त्वं हतवानित्यर्थः । नृणां नेतृणां
> मध्ये नृतमातिशयेन नेतर्हे इंद्र पृत्सु संग्रामेषु सुधितेभिः सुष्टु निहितैरत्कैरात्मीयैरायुधैरा दर्षि । अन्यानपि शत्रुनादृणासि । विदारयसि । तत्र दृष्टांतः।
> वनेव वनानीव । यथा वृक्षजातानि कुठारादिभिश्चिनत्ति तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, you slew those enemies of both kinds. Which are the two kinds? The Dāsas, the destroyers, who are opposed to the rites, Vala and the other asuras; and the Āryas, who are excellent through the performance of rites, and the Vṛtras, the obstructers, Viśvarūpa and the others. Those enemies of both kinds, O hero, you have slain. O Indra, most leader-like among leaders, in battles you cleave with your well-placed weapons; you tear apart other enemies also. The illustration: like forests; as a man cuts the growths of trees with axes and the like, so you do. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- दासा (*dāsā*)—those opposed to the rites
- आर्या (*āryā*)—those belonging to the group of the Āryas
- वृत्राणि (*vṛtrāṇi*)—and pervading
- तान् उभयान् (*tān ubhayān*)—those two kinds of

### Printed p. 35

- अमित्रान् (*amitrān*)—enemies
- वधीः (*vadhīḥ*)—you have slain
- नृणां (*nṛṇāṃ*)—among leaders
- नृतम (*nṛtama*)—O Indra, the most excellent leader
- पृत्सु (*pṛtsu*)—in battles
- वनेव (*vaneva*)—as one splits trees with an axe
- सुधितेभिः (*sudhitebhiḥ*)—sharpened
- अत्कैः (*atkaiḥ*)—with weapons
- आ दर्षि (*ā darṣi*)—you have split the enemies

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you have slain the enemies of two kinds: the Dasyus, who are opposed to the rites, and those belonging to the group of the Āryas devoted to the rites, and pervading ones. O Indra, the most excellent leader among leaders, in battles you have split the enemies with sharpened weapons, as one splits trees with an axe.

##### English translation
You, hero, Indra, destroyed both (classes of) enemies, (both) Dasa and Arya, adversaries; chief leader of leaders, you cut your foes in pieces in battles with well-pleid weapons, as (wood-cutters fell) the forests.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स त्वं न॑ इं॒द्राक॑वाभिरू॒ती सखा॑ वि॒श्वायु॑रवि॒ता वृ॒धे भूः॑ ।  
> स्व॑र्षाता॒ यद्ध्वया॑मसि त्वा॒ युध्यं॑तो ने॒मधि॑ता पृ॒त्सु  ॑
> शूर ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । त्वं । नः । इंद्र । आकवाभिः । ऊती । सखा । विश्वआयुः । अविता । वृधे । भूः ।  
> स्वःसाता । यत् । ह्वयामसि । त्वा । युध्यंतः । नेमधिता । पृथ्सु । शूर ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र स तादृशस्त्वमकवाभिरकुत्सिताभिरूती ऊतिभी रक्षाभिर्नोऽस्माकं
> वृधे वर्धनायाविता रक्षिता भूः । भव । तथा विश्वायुः सर्वतो गंता त्वमस्माकं
> सखा च भव । नेमधिता । नेमशब्दोऽर्धवाची । उक्तं च । त्वो नेम इत्यर्थस्य ।
> नि. ३-२० । इति । आर्धाः कतिपयाः पुरुषा धीयंत एष्विति नेमधितयः संग्रामाः ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, such as you are, become the protector for our growth with unblamed protections. Likewise, be, as the one who goes everywhere, our friend. The word *nema* means "half"; it is said त्वो नेम इत्यर्थस्य (*tvo nema ityarthasya*) (Ni. 3-20). Battles are *nemadhiti*, because in them some half-number of men are laid down.

### Printed p. 36

> तादृशेषु पृत्सु पृतनासु संग्रामेषु युध्यंतो युद्धं कुर्वंतो वयं
> स्वर्षाता स्वः सुष्वरणीयं धनं तस्य संभजनार्थं हे शूर त्वां यद्यदा
> ह्वयामसि आह्वयामः तदानीं त्वमविता सखा च भवेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In such battles, fighting, we call you, O hero, to share in the choicest wealth, *svar*; whenever we call you, at that time be our protector and friend. This is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- सः त्वं (*saḥ tvaṃ*)—you, such as you are
- अकवाभिः (*akavābhiḥ*)—unblamed
- ऊती (*ūtī*)—with protections
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- वृधे (*vṛdhe*)—for growth
- अविता (*avitā*)—as protector
- भूः (*bhūḥ*)—be
- शूर (*śūra*)—O valiant Indra
- नेमधिता (*nemadhitā*)—destroying some men
- पृत्सु (*pṛtsu*)—in battles
- युध्यंतः (*yudhyaṃtaḥ*)—fighting
