O Indra, when, in a battle for the sake of abundant wealth, at the time of the effort, you urge our horses to run swiftly on a road full of pits and mounds and uneven, as falcons that seek food in the form of flesh fly swiftly on the crooked path of the sky, then give protective armour.

##### English translation
(At the time) when, in the effort (made) in an arduous conflict; urge our horses over an uneven road, like falcons darting upon their food through the difficult path (of the firmament).

### Printed p. 179

#### Ṛk 14

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सिंधू॑रिव प्रव॒ण आ॑शु॒या य॒तो यदि॒ क्लोश॒मनु॒ स्वणि॑ ।  
> आ ये वयो॒ न वर्वृ॑त॒त्यामि॑षि गृभी॒ता बा॒ह्वर्गवि॑ ॥ १४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सिंधून् इव । प्रवणे । आशुया । यतः । यदि । क्लोशं । अनु । स्वनि ।  
> आ । ये । वयः । न । वर्वृतति । आमिषि । गृभीताः । बाह्वोः । गवि ॥ १४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उक्तस्यैवार्थस्यातिशयप्रतिपादनार्थेयं । प्रवणे निम्म देशे सिंधूनिव
> स्यंदमाना नदीरिवाशुयाशु शीघ्रं यतो गच्छतोऽश्वान् हे इंद्र यदि
> चोदयासे प्रेरयसि । कदा । क्लोशमनु । क्लोश इति भयनाम । भयमनुलक्ष्य
> स्वनि स्वन आक्रोशे सति । कीदृशास्तेऽश्वाः । बाह्वोर्बाहुमूलयोर्गृभीताः
> कक्षया बद्धा येऽश्वा गवि गोरूपे धने जेतव्ये सत्या वर्वृतति पुनः पुनरावर्तंते । आमिष्यामिषे मांसे ग्रहीतव्ये सति वयो न यथा श्येनादयः दक्षिणः
> पुनः पुनरावर्तंते तद्वत् । एवंभूतानश्वान् भयेन क्रोशे सति यदा त्वं
> प्रेरयसि तदानीमस्मदीयेभ्यश्वर्दिः प्रयच्छेति संबंधः । यद्यप्यषेयूर्द्धे
> प्रसक्तिर्नास्ति तथापि राजपुरोहितत्वेन राजकीयानां पुरुषाणां जयः प्रार्थ्यत
> इति न कश्चिद्विरोधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Ṛk is meant to establish with emphasis the meaning already stated. O Indra, if you urge horses that go swiftly (आशुया (*āśuyā*)), like streams (सिंधूनिव (*siṃdhūniva*)) flowing down a slope (प्रवणे (*pravaṇe*)); when? क्लोशमनु (*klośamanu*): क्लोश (*klośa*) is a name for fear; with regard to fear, when there is a cry (स्वनि (*svani*), in the sense of a cry). What kind are those horses? Those that are held (गृभीताः (*gṛbhītāḥ*)) at the arm-roots (बाह्वोः (*bāhvoḥ*)), that is, bound by the girth, and that, when wealth in the form of cattle (गवि (*gavi*)) is to be won, turn back again and again (वर्वृतति (*varvṛtati*)); when flesh (आमिषि (*āmiṣi*)) is to be seized they turn again and again, like birds (वयो न (*vayo na*)) such as falcons. When you urge such horses while there is a cry through fear, then give armour for those who belong to us: this is the connection. And though the ṛṣi has no occasion in war **NOTE**, yet, since he is the king's chief priest, victory is prayed for the king's men, so there is no contradiction.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) आमिषि (*āmiṣi*)—to obtain flesh
- वयो न (*vayo na*)—as falcons and other birds come running again and again
- बाह्वोः (*bāhvoḥ*)—at the arm-roots
- गृभीताः (*gṛbhītāḥ*)—which are bound with a girth
- ये (*ye*)—those horses
- गवि (*gavi*)—for the sake of cattle
- आ वर्वृतति (*ā varvṛtati*)—which come again and again
- क्लोशमनु (*klośamanu*)—from fear
- स्वनि (*svani*)—even though they are neighing
- प्रवणे (*pravaṇe*)—in low-lying places
- सिंधूनिव (*siṃdhūniva*)—like rapidly flowing rivers
- आशुया (*āśuyā*)—very swiftly
- यतः (*yataḥ*)—as they run
- यदि (*yadi*)—when you urge (then give armour for war)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, those horses which are bound with a girth at the arm-roots, and which come back again and again for the sake of cattle, as falcons and other birds come running again and again to obtain flesh, even though those horses are neighing through fear, when you urge them to run very swiftly, like rivers flowing rapidly in low-lying places, then give armour for war.

### Printed p. 180

##### English translation
Rushing rapidly like rivers in their downward course, and although neighing loudly through terror, they yet, tight-girthed, return repeatedly (to the conflict) for cattle, like birds darting on their prey.

## Sūkta 47 (Maṇḍala 6)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स्वादुरित्येकत्रिंशदृचं चतुर्थं सूक्तं भरद्वाजपुत्रस्य गर्गस्यार्षं। प्रस्तोक
> इन्नित्येषा त्रिष्टुप् दशाश्वानित्यनुष्टुप् दश रथानिति गायत्री महि राध इति द्विपदा
> द्वैकादशका युजानो हरितेत्येषा बृहती दिवस्पृथिव्या इति जगती शिष्टास्त्रिष्टुभः ।
> आदितः पंचानामृचां सोमो देवता । अगव्यूति क्षेत्यमित्यर्धर्चप्रथमपादे
> देवा उत्तरस्य भूमिस्कृतीयस्य बृहस्पतिश्चतुर्थस्येंद्रः । प्रस्तोक इत्याद्याश्चतस्रः
> सृंजयपुत्रस्य राज्यो दानस्तुतिरूपत्वात्तद्देवताकाः । वनस्पते वीड्वंग इत्ययं
> तृचो रथदेवत्यः । उप श्वासयेति तृचो दुंदुभिदेवत्यः । समश्वपर्णा इत्यर्धर्च
> ऐंद्रः । शिष्टा इंद्रदेवताकाः । तथा चानुक्रांतं । स्वादुरेकत्रिंशद्गर्गः पंचादौ
> सौम्योऽगप्यूत्यर्धर्चो लिंगोक्तदेवतः प्रस्तोक इति त्रिष्टुबनुष्टुब्जायत्री
> द्विपदा सार्ङ्मयस्य प्रस्तोकस्य दानस्तुतिः परौ तृचौ रथदुंदुभिदेवत्यावैंद्रोऽत्यर्थर्चो युजानो बृहती दिवस्पृथिव्या जगती ॥ अग्निमारुत
> आदितश्चतस्रः शंसनीयाः । सूत्रितं च । स्वादुष्किलायमिति चतस्रो मध्यॆ जाह्वानं ।
> अ. ५-२० । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The fourth sūkta, beginning स्वादुः (*svāduḥ*), has thirty-one Ṛks; its seer is Garga, son of Bharadvāja. The Ṛk प्रस्तोक इन्नु (*prastoka innu*) is in the triṣṭubh metre, दशाश्वान् (*daśāśvān*) in anuṣṭubh, दश रथान् (*daśa rathān*) in gāyatrī, महि राधः (*mahi rādhaḥ*) a dvipadā of two lines of eleven syllables, युजानो हरित (*yujāno harita*) a bṛhatī, दिवस्पृथिव्याः (*divaspṛthivyāḥ*) a jagatī, and the rest are triṣṭubh. The deity of the first five Ṛks is Soma. In the first line of the half-verse अगव्यूति क्षेत्रं (*agavyūti kṣetraṃ*) the deities are the gods named in it; the next line has Bhūmi, the third Bṛhaspati and the fourth Indra. The four Ṛks beginning प्रस्तोक (*prastoka*) are in praise of the gift of the king Prastoka, son of Sṛñjaya, and so have that as deity. The three beginning वनस्पते वीड्वंगः (*vanaspate vīḍvaṃgaḥ*) have the chariot as deity; the three beginning उप श्वासय (*upa śvāsaya*) have the drum (dundubhi) as deity. The half-verse समश्वपर्णाः (*samaśvaparṇāḥ*) is addressed to Indra; the rest have Indra as deity. He quotes the Anukramaṇī to the same effect. He adds that it is prescribed in the sūtra: the four Ṛks beginning स्वादुष्किलायं (*svāduṣkilāyaṃ*) are recited in the middle (of the invocations), reference A. 5-20. **NOTE**

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning स्वादुः (*svāduḥ*) is the fourth sūkta in the fourth Anuvāka. It has thirty-one Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Garga, son of the ṛṣi Bharadvāja. Of this sūkta, the Ṛk beginning "Prastoka inn" (the 22nd) is in the triṣṭubh metre; the Ṛk beginning "daśāśvān" (the 23rd) is in the anuṣṭubh metre; the Ṛk beginning "daśa rathān" (the 24th) is in the gāyatrī metre; the Ṛk beginning "mahi rādhaḥ" (the 25th) is in the metre called dvipadā, with two lines of eleven syllables; the Ṛk beginning "yujāno daritā" (the 19th) **NOTE** is in the bṛhatī metre; the Ṛk beginning "divaspṛthivyāḥ" (the 27th) is in the jagatī metre. The remaining Ṛks are in the triṣṭubh metre. The deity of the first five Ṛks of this sūkta is Soma. For the first line of the first half of the twentieth Ṛk, "agavyūti kṣetram", the deities are those mentioned in it; for the second line the deities are Bhūmi, for the third line Bṛhaspati, and for the fourth line Indra. In the four Ṛks beginning with "Prastoka" (22-25) there is praise of the gift of the king named Prastoka, son of Sṛñjaya, so the deity of those four Ṛks is the praise of Prastoka's gift. For the three Ṛks beginning with "vanaspate vīḍvaṅgaḥ" (26-28) the chariot is the deity. For the three beginning with "upaśvāsaya" (24-31) **NOTE**, except the latter half of the last Ṛk, the drum is the deity. For the latter half of the thirty-first Ṛk, "samaśvaparṇāḥ", Indra is the deity. For all the remaining Ṛks Indra is the deity. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: स्वादुरेकत्रिंशद्गर्गः पंचादौ सौम्योऽगव्यूत्यर्धर्चो लिंगोक्तदेवतः प्रस्तोक इति त्रिष्टुबनुष्टुब्बायत्री द्विपदा सांजयस्य प्रस्तोकस्य दानस्तुतिः परौ तृचौ रथदुंदुभिदेवत्यावैंद्रोऽन्त्यर्थर्चो युजानो बृहती त्रिष्टुम्महि द्विपदा दिवस्पृथिव्या जगती (*svādurekatriṃśadgargaḥ paṃcādau saumyo 'gavyūtyardharco liṃgoktadevataḥ prastoka iti triṣṭubanuṣṭubbāyatrī dvipadā sāṃjayasya prastokasya dānastutiḥ parau tṛcau rathaduṃdubhidevatyāvaiṃdro 'ntyartharco yujāno bṛhatī triṣṭummahi dvipadā divaspṛthivyā jagatī*). That the first four Ṛks of this sūkta are to be recited for the Āgnimāruta śastra mantras is made clear by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, स्वादुष्किलायमिति चतस्रो मध्यॆ चाह्वानां (*svāduṣkilāyamiti catasro madhyè cāhvānāṃ*) (Ā. 5-20).

### Printed p. 181

##### Particulars

Sūkta 47. Maṇḍala 6. Anuvāka 4. Sūkta 47. Aṣṭaka 4. Adhyāya 7. Vargas 30, 31, 32, 33, 34, 35. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 31. Seer: Garga **NOTE**. Deity: Ṛks 1-5, Soma; Ṛks 6-19, 21 and the latter half of 31, Indra; for the first line of Ṛk 20 the deities named in it, for the second line Bhūmi, for the third line Bṛhaspati, for the fourth line Indra; Ṛks 22-25, the praise of the gift of Prastoka Sārjaya **NOTE**; Ṛks 26-28, the chariot; Ṛks 29-30 and the first half of 31, the drum. Metre: Ṛks 1-18, 20-22, 26 and 28-31, triṣṭubh; 19, bṛhatī; 23, anuṣṭubh; 24, gāyatrī; 25, dvipadā; 27, jagatī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्वा॒दुष्किला॒यं मधु॑मा उ॒तायं ती॒व्रः किला॒यं रस॑वा  ॒
> उतायं ।  
> उ॒तो न्व१॒ं॑स्य प॑पि॒वांस॒मिंद्रं॒ न कश्च॒न स॑हत आह॒वेषु॑ ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 182

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्वादुः । किल । अयं । मधुमान् । उत । अयं । तीव्रः । किल । आयं । रसवान् ।  
> उत । अयं ।  
> उतो इति । नु । अस्य । पपिवांसं । इंद्रं । न । कः । चन । सहते । आहवेषु ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमभिषुतः सोमः स्वादुरास्वादनीयः किल भवति । उतापि च मधुमान्माधुर्यवांश्चायं सोमो भवति ! तथायं सोमस्तीव्रः किल मदोत्पादने
> तीक्ष्णः खलु भवति । उतापि चायं सोमो रसवान् सारवांश्च भवति । अनेन
> वाक्यचतुष्टयेन सोमस्य माधुर्यातिशयत्वं च प्रतिपादितं ! उतो आपि चास्य
> सोमस्य !! द्वितीयार्थे षष्ठी ॥ इमं सोमं पपिवांसं पीतवंतमिंद्रमाहवेषु
> संग्रामेषु न कश्चन न कोऽपि सहते अभिभवति । नु इति पूरकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This pressed Soma is indeed sweet to the taste (स्वादुः (*svāduḥ*)). It is also honeyed (मधुमान् (*madhumān*)), that is, full of sweetness. This Soma is likewise keen (तीव्रः (*tīvraḥ*)): in producing intoxication it is sharp. And it is full of juice (रसवान् (*rasavān*)), that is, full of essence. By these four statements the surpassing sweetness of Soma is set forth. Further, no one in battles can overcome Indra, who has drunk (पपिवांसं (*papivāṃsaṃ*)) this Soma. The word नु (*nu*) is a mere filler.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this pressed Soma
- स्वादुः किल (*svāduḥ kila*)—is certainly savoury
- उत (*uta*)—and
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this
- मधुमान् (*madhumān*)—is endowed with sweetness
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this same Soma juice
- तीव्रः किल (*tīvraḥ kila*)—has a sharpness that causes intoxication
- उत (*uta*)—moreover
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this same Soma
- रसवान् (*rasavān*)—is full of essence
- उतो (*uto*)—and
- अस्य (*asya*)—this Soma
- पपिवांसं (*papivāṃsaṃ*)—who drinks
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- आहवेषु (*āhaveṣu*)—in battles
- कश्चन (*kaścana*)—no one at all
- न सहते नु (*na sahate nu*)—can withstand and defeat

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

This pressed Soma is certainly savoury. It is endowed with sweetness and full of juice, and moreover has a sharpness that causes intoxication. No one in battles can withstand and defeat Indra who drinks this Soma juice.

##### English translation
Savoury indeed is this (Soma); sweet is it, sharp, and full of flavour: no one is able to encounter Indra in battles after he has been quaffing this (beverage).

### Printed p. 183

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒यं स्वा॒दुरि॒ह मदि॑ष्ट आस॒ यस्येंद्रो॑ वृत्र॒हत्ये॑ म॒माद॑ ।  
> पु॒रूणि॒ यश्चा॒त्ना शंब॑रस्य॒ विन॑व॒तिं नव॑ च दे॒ह्यो॒३॒॑३ हन्॥२॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अयं । स्वादुः । इह । मदिष्टः । आस । यस्य । इंद्रः । वृत्रहत्ये । ममाद ।  
> पुरूणि । यः । च्यौत्ना । शंबरस्य । वि । नवतिं । नव । च । देह्यः । हन् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इहास्मिन्यज्ञेयं सोमः स्वादुरास्वादितः पीतः सन् मदिष्टोऽतिशयेन
> मदयितास । बभूव । यस्य सोमस्य पानेनेंद्रो वृत्रहत्ये वृत्रहननकाले
> ममाद मत्तो बभूव । यः सोमः शंबरस्यासुरस्य पुरूणि बहूनि च्यौत्ना
> च्यौत्नानि बलानि जघान । तथा देहो दिग्धा उपचिता आसुरीः पुरीः तदीयान्
> देहान् वा नवतिं नव च नवोत्तरनवतिसंख्याका यः सोमो वि हन् व्यहन् अयं
> सोम इति प्रथमपादेन संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Here, in this sacrifice, this Soma, tasted, that is, drunk, has become most intoxicating, intoxicating to the highest degree. By drinking this Soma Indra became exhilarated at the time of the slaying of Vṛtra. This Soma also destroyed the many strengths (च्यौत्ना (*cyautnā*)) of the asura Śambara. Likewise, the Soma destroyed ninety-nine (नवतिं नव च (*navatiṃ nava ca*)) of the demons' strongholds, built-up and accumulated ones (देहो (*deho*)), or else their bodies. This is connected with अयं सोमः (*ayaṃ somaḥ*), "this Soma", of the first line.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यस्य (*yasya*)—by drinking which Soma
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- वृत्रहत्ये (*vṛtrahatye*)—at the time of slaying Vṛtra
- ममाद (*mamāda*)—obtained exhilaration
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Soma
- शंबरस्य (*śaṃbarasya*)—of the asura Śambara
- पुरूणि (*purūṇi*)—of the many
- च्यौत्ना (*cyautnā*)—armies (became the means of slaying)
- and (यस्य (*yasya*)—by drinking which Soma)
- नवतिं नव च (*navatiṃ nava ca*)—ninety-nine in number
- देह्यः (*dehyaḥ*)—and firm
- asura cities
- वि हन् (*vi han*)—he slew; such
- आयं (*āyaṃ*)—this Soma
- इह (*iha*)—in this sacrifice
- स्वादुः (*svāduḥ*)—is savoury
- मदिष्टः (*madiṣṭaḥ*)—and intoxicating
- आस (*āsa*)—is

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

By drinking which Soma Indra obtained exhilaration at the time of slaying the asura Vṛtra, which Soma became the means of killing the numerous armies of the asura Śambara, and by drinking which he slew the ninety-nine firm cities of the asuras, this Soma is, in this sacrifice, savoury and intoxicating.

### Printed p. 184

##### English translation
This savoury Soma, drunk on this occasion, has been most exhilarating : dy drinking of it Indra, has been elevated to the slaying of Vritra, and it has destroyed the numerous hosts of Sambara and the ninety-nine cities.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒यं मे॑ पी॒त उदि॑यर्ति॒ वाच॑म॒यं म॑नी॒षामु॑श॒तीम॑जीगति ।  
> अ॒यं षु॒र्वीर॑मिमित॒ धीरो॒ न याभ्यो॒ भुव॑नं॒ कच्च॒नारे॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अयं । मे । पीतः । उत् । इयर्ति । वाचं । अयं । मनीषां । उशतीं ।  
> अजीगरिति ।  
> अयं । षट् । उर्वीष । अमिमात । धीरः । याभ्यः । भुवनं । कत् । चन । आरे ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयं सोमः पीतः सन् मे मम वाचमुदियर्ति । उद्गमयति ।
> अयमेव सोम उशतीं कांतां मनीषां बुद्धिमुदजीगः । उद्गारयति ।
> प्रकाशयति ॥ गृ निगरण इत्यस्माण्ण्यंताल्लुजि चजा रूपं ॥ अपि चायं धीरो धीमान्
> सोमः सळुर्विः षण्मोर्वीरंहसस्पांतु द्यौष्ट पृथिवी चाहश्च रात्रिश्चापश्चाधयश्चेति प्रतिपादिता द्युपृथिव्याद्याः षडमिमीत । निर्मितवान् । याभ्य उर्विभ्य आरे
> दूरे विप्रकृष्टे देशे कच्चन किंचन भुवनं भूतजातं न भवति अपि त्वास्वेव सर्वं
> भूतजातं वर्तते तावृशीरुर्निर्निर्मितवान् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Soma, when drunk, brings forth, that is, makes manifest, my speech (वाचं (*vācaṃ*)). This same Soma brings forth and makes manifest the desired (उशतीं (*uśatīṃ*)), lovely intelligence (मनीषां (*manīṣāṃ*)). And this wise Soma created the six wide ones **NOTE**, which are set forth as heaven and earth, day and night, the waters and the plants. Than these wide ones, far away, in a distant place, there is no other being or world whatever; rather, all beings exist in these very ones. Such wide ones he created.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this Soma
- पीतः (*pītaḥ*)—having been drunk
- मे (*me*)—my
- वाचं (*vācaṃ*)—speech
- उदियर्ति (*udiyarti*)—makes manifest
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this same Soma
- उततीं (*utatīṃ*)—attractive
- मनीषां (*manīṣāṃ*)—intelligence
- अजीगः (*ajīgaḥ*)—makes grow sharp
- याभ्यः (*yābhyaḥ*)—than which principles

### Printed p. 185

- आरे (*āre*)—surpassing
- कच्चन भुवनं (*kaccana bhuvanaṃ*)—any other world
- न (*na*)—there is not, such
- षट्.उर्विः (*ṣaṭ.urviḥ*)—six worlds beginning with the heavenly world
- धीरः (*dhīraḥ*)—wise
- आयं (*āyaṃ*)—this Soma king
- अमिमीत (*amimīta*)—created

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

This Soma juice, when drunk, makes my speech manifest. This same Soma makes the attractive intelligence grow sharp. This wise Soma king created the six principles, the heavenly world, the earth, day, night, waters and plants, beyond which there is no other world.

##### English translation
This beverage inspires my speech; this develops the desired intelligence: this sagacious (Soma) has created the six vast conditions, from which no creature is distinct.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒यं स यो व॑रि॒माणं॑ पृथि॒व्या व॒र्ष्णाणं॑ दि॒वो अकृ॑णोद॒यं सः ।  
> अ॒यं पी॒यूषं॑ ति॒सृषु॑ प्र॒वत्सु॒ सोमो॑ दाधारो॒र्व१॒॑ १०तरि॑क्षं ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अयं । सः । यः । वरिमाणं । पृथिव्याः । वर्ष्णाणं । दिवः । अकृणोत् । आयं । सः।  
> अयं । पीयूषं । तिसृषु । प्रवत्सु । सोमः । दाधार । उरु । अंतरिक्षं ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स खल्वयं सोमो यः पृथिव्या वरिमाणमुरुत्वं विस्तृतत्वमकृणोत्
> अकरोत् । तथायं सोमो दिवो द्युलोकस्य वर्ष्णाणं संहतत्वं दृढत्वमकृणोत् अकरोत् । अयं सोम॥ स हि भवति । अपि चायं सोमस्ति सृष्टोषधीष्टप्पु गोषु च प्रवत्सु प्रकृष्टास्वेतासु पीयूषं रसं दाधार । धारयॆति ।

### Printed p. 186

> तथोरु विस्तीर्णमंतरिक्षं च धारयति । कथा च मंत्रातरं । त्वमिमा ओषधीः
> सोम विश्वास्त्वमपो अजनयस्त्वं गाः । त्वमा ततंथोर्वंतरिक्षं ऋग्वे.
> १-९१-२२ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This is that Soma who made the extent (वरिमाणं (*varimāṇaṃ*)), the wideness and expansion, of the earth. Likewise this Soma made the compactness (वर्ष्णाणं (*varṣṇāṇaṃ*)), the firmness, of the heaven. **NOTE** This Soma also holds the juice, the ambrosia (पीयूषं (*pīyūṣaṃ*)), in the three **NOTE**, in these excellent ones (प्रवत्सु (*pravatsu*)). And he holds up the wide firmament. He quotes another mantra: त्वमिमा ओषधीः सोम विश्वास्त्वमपो अजनयस्त्वं गाः । त्वमा ततंथोर्वंतरिक्षं (*tvamimā oṣadhīḥ soma viśvāstvamapo ajanayastvaṃ gāḥ | tvamā tataṃthorvaṃtarikṣaṃ*) (Ṛgve. 1-91-22) **GLOSS** **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—who
- पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*)—of the earth
- परिमाणं (*parimāṇaṃ*)—the extent
- अकृणोत् (*akṛṇot*)—has made
- सः (*saḥ*)—he
- आयं सोमः (*āyaṃ somaḥ*)—is this Soma king
- आयं सः (*āyaṃ saḥ*)—this same Soma
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—of the heavenly world
- वर्ष्णाणं (*varṣṇāṇaṃ*)—the firmness (he made)
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this Soma
- प्रवत्सु (*pravatsu*)—in the excellent
- तिसृषु (*tisṛṣu*)—plants, waters and cows
- पीयूषं (*pīyūṣaṃ*)—the juice of the nature of ambrosia
- दाधार (*dādhāra*)—has filled
- उरु (*uru*)—the wide
- अंतरिक्षं (*aṃtarikṣaṃ*)—firmament (he has upheld)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

He who made the earth exceedingly wide, he who made the heavenly world stand firm, is this Soma king. This same Soma has filled the most excellent plants, waters and cows with the juice of the nature of ambrosia. And he has upheld the wide firmament.

##### English translation
This it is which has formed the earth, the compactness of the heaven : this soma has deposited the ambrosia in its three principal (receptacles). and has upheld the spacious firmament.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒यं वि॑दच्चित्र॒दृशी॑क॒मर्णः॑ शु॒क्रस॑द्मनामु॒षसा॒मनी॑के ।  
> अ॒यं म॒हान्म॑ह॒ता स्कंभ॑ने॒नोद्द्याम॑स्तभ्नाद्वृष॒भोः  ॒॑
> मरुत्वान् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अयं । विदत् । चित्रदृशीकं । अर्णः । शुक्रसद्मनां । उषसां । अनीके ।  
> अयं । महान् । महता । स्कंधनेन । उत् । द्यां । अस्तभ्नात् । वृषभः । मरुत्वान् ॥

### Printed p. 187

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयं सोमश्चित्रदृशीकं विचित्रदर्शनं यद्वाश्चर्यत्वेन दर्शनीयमर्णः
> सर्वस्य प्रेरकं सौरं ज्योतिर्विदत् । लंभयति । कस्मिन्काले ! शुक्रसद्मनां शुक्रं
> निर्मलमंतरिक्षं सद्म सदनं यासां तासामुषसामनीके प्रमुखे । उषःकाल
> इत्यर्थः । अपि च महान् प्रवृद्योयं सोमो महता स्थविष्ठेन स्कंभनेन
> स्तंभनसाधनेन मध्यवर्तिनांतरिक्षेण द्यां द्युलोकमुदस्तभ्नात् । ऊर्ध्वमस्थापयत् । यथाधो न पतति तथांतरिक्षं तस्याधारत्वेन कृतवानित्यर्थः । कीदृशः
> सोमः । वृषभो वृष्टेर्जनयिता । सोमाहुत्या हि वृष्टिर्जन्यते । मरुत्वान्मरुद्धिर्युक्तः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Soma obtains (विदत् (*vidat*)), that is, causes to be obtained, the wonderfully visible (चित्रदृशीकं (*citradṛśīkaṃ*)), or marvellously beautiful, solar light (अर्णः (*arṇaḥ*)), the impeller of all. At what time? At the front, at the head, of the dawns (उषसां (*uṣasāṃ*)) whose seat is the pure firmament; that is, at the time of dawn. And this great (महान् (*mahān*)), grown Soma has propped up (उदस्तभ्नात् (*udastabhnāt*)) the heavenly world with a great, most massive prop, namely the firmament that stands in the middle: he set it upright, and made the firmament its support so that it does not fall downward. What kind of Soma? A bull (वृषभः (*vṛṣabhaḥ*)), the producer of rain; for by the oblation of Soma rain is produced. He is accompanied by the Maruts (मरुत्वान् (*marutvān*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शुक्रसद्मनां (*śukrasadmanāṃ*)—those whose dwelling is the shining firmament
- उषसां (*uṣasāṃ*)—of the dawns
- अनीके (*anīke*)—at the time of their appearing
- आयं (*āyaṃ*)—this Soma king
- चित्रदृशीकं (*citradṛśīkaṃ*)—the most attractively visible
- अर्ण (*arṇa*)—solar light
- विदत् (*vidat*)—makes manifest
- वृषभः (*vṛṣabhaḥ*)—the producer of rain
- मरुत्वान् (*marutvān*)—accompanied by the Maruts
- महान् (*mahān*)—and great-souled
- आयं (*āyaṃ*)—this Soma king
- महता (*mahatā*)—by the most firm
- स्कंभनेन (*skaṃbhanena*)—firmament that is like a supporting pillar
- द्यां (*dyāṃ*)—the heavenly world
- उदस्तभ्नात् (*udastabhnāt*)—has established and made to stand

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

At the time of the appearing of the dawns, whose dwelling is the shining firmament, this Soma king makes manifest the most attractively visible light of the sun. This Soma king, the producer of rain, accompanied by the Maruts, and great-souled, has established and made to stand the heavenly world with the help of the firmament, which is like a most firm supporting pillar.

##### English translation
This makes known the wonderfully beautiful and inspiring (solar radiance) at the appearance of the dawns, whose dwelling is the firmament : this mighty (Soma) has sustained the heaven with a powerful support, the sender of rain, the leader of the winds.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> धृ॒षत्पि॑ब क॒लशे॒ सोम॑मिंद्र वृत्र॒हा शू॑र सम॒रे वसू॑नां ।  
> माध्यं॑दिने॒ सव॑न॒ आ वृ॑षस्व रयि॒स्थानो॑ र॒यिम॒स्मासु॑ धेहि ॥६॥

### Printed p. 188

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> धृषत् । पिब । कलशे । सोमं । इंद्र । वृत्रहा । शूर । संअरे । वसूनां ।  
> माध्यंदिने । सवने । आ । वृषस्व । रयिस्थानः । रयिं । आस्मासु । धेहि ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे शूर शौर्यवन्निंद्र वृत्रहा वृत्राणामावरकाणां शूत्रूणां हंता त्वं
> वसूनां धनानां समरे संग्रामे निमित्तभूते सति कलशे द्रोणकलशेवस्थितं
> सोमं धृषत् धृष्टं यथा । भवति तथा पिब । अपि च माध्यंदिने सवन आ वृषस्व ।
> आसिंचस्व । जठरं सोमेन पूरय ! सर्वैंद्रं हि माध्यंदिनं सवनं । किं च
> रयिस्थानो रयीणां धनानां स्थानभूतस्त्वं रयिं धनमस्मासु धेहि । धारय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O hero, O Indra, you who slay the Vṛtras, the enemies who obstruct, when a battle for wealth has come about, drink boldly (धृषत् (*dhṛṣat*)), that is, in such a way that you become bold, the Soma standing in the Droṇa-kalaśa (कलशे (*kalaśe*)). Also pour in (आ वृषस्व (*ā vṛṣasva*)), that is, fill your belly with Soma at the midday pressing; for the midday pressing belongs wholly to Indra. And, as the abode of riches (रयिस्थानः (*rayisthānaḥ*)), place riches in us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शूर (*śūra*)—O valiant one
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- वृत्रहा (*vṛtrahā*)—you, the slayer of Vṛtra
- वसूनां (*vasūnāṃ*)—that is fought for riches
- समरे (*samare*)—in order to take part in the battle
- कलशे (*kalaśe*)—which is in the Droṇa-kalaśa
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma
- धृषत् (*dhṛṣat*)—so that strength and courage are filled
- पिब (*piba*)—drink
- माध्यंदिने सवने (*mādhyaṃdine savane*)—also at the time of the midday pressing
- आ वृषस्व (*ā vṛṣasva*)—fill the belly fully with Soma
- रयिस्थानः (*rayisthānaḥ*)—you, the place of origin of riches
- अस्मासु (*asmāsu*)—in us
- रयिं (*rayiṃ*)—riches
- धेहि (*dhehi*)—place

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O valiant Indra, you, the slayer of Vṛtra, drink the Soma that is in the Droṇa-kalaśa, so that you are filled with strength and courage, in order to take part in the battle that is fought for riches. At the time of the midday pressing also fill your belly fully with Soma. You, the place of origin of riches, fill us fully with riches.

##### English translation
Hero, Indra, you are the slayer of foes in contests for (the acquirement of) treasures, drink boldly from the pitcher : drink copiously at the noon-day rite : receptacle of riches, bestow riches upon us.

### Printed p. 189

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॒ प्र णः॑ पुरुषि॒तेव॑ पश्य॒ प्र नो॑ नय प्रत॒रं वस्यो॒ अच्छ॑।  
> भवा॑ सुपा॒रो अ॑तिपार॒यो नो॒ भवा॒ सुनी॑तिरु॒त ना॒मनी॑तिः॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्र । प्र । नः । पुरएता इव । पश्य । प्र । नः । नय । प्रतरं । वस्यः । अच्छ ।  
> भव । सुपारः । अतिपारयः । नः । भव । सुनीतिः । उत । वामनीतिः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वं पुरएतेव पुरतो गंतेव नोऽस्मान् प्र पश्य । प्रकर्षेणेकस्य ।
> यथा मार्गरक्षकः स्वयं पुरतो गच्छन्ननुगच्छतो रक्षणीयान् पथिकान्पश्यति तथा
> पश्येत्यर्थः । तथा वस्यो वसीयः श्रेष्ठं धनमच्छाभिमुख्येन प्रतरं प्रकृष्टतरमतिशयेन प्र णय । अस्मान्प्राय । तथा सुपारः सुष्टु पारयिता दुःखेभ्यस्तारयिता
> भव । तथा नोऽस्मान् स्तोत्थनतिपारयः । शत्रूनतिक्रामय । सुनीति शोभनयनश्चास्माकं भव । उतापि च वामनीतिर्वामानां वननीयानां धनानां नेता भव ।
> यद्वा । अस्मदर्थं श्रेष्ठप्रापणो भव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, look upon us (प्र पश्य (*pra paśya*)) like one who goes in front (पुरएतेव (*puraeteva*)). **NOTE** As a guardian of the road, going in front himself, sees the travellers who follow and are to be protected, so look upon us: this is the meaning. Likewise lead us (प्र णय (*pra ṇaya*)) towards the best, most excellent wealth (वस्यः (*vasyaḥ*)), facing us, to an excess. Be a good ferrier (सुपारः (*supāraḥ*)), one who takes us across sorrows. Make us pass beyond (अतिपारयः (*atipārayaḥ*)) the enemies **NOTE**. Be for us a good leader (सुनीतिः (*sunītiḥ*)), and also a leader of the desirable (वामानां (*vāmānāṃ*)), enjoyable riches (वामनीतिः (*vāmanītiḥ*)). Or else: be for our sake the conveyer to the best.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- पुर एतेव (*pura eteva*)—like one who goes right in front of us
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- प्र पश्य (*pra paśya*)—look upon fully
- वस्यः (*vasyaḥ*)—the excellent wealth
- अच्छ (*accha*)—towards us
- प्रतरं (*prataraṃ*)—abundantly
- प्र णय (*pra ṇaya*)—bring and supply
- सुपारः (*supāraḥ*)—as one who brings to an end from sorrow
- भव (*bhava*)—be
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- अतिपारयः (*atipārayaḥ*)—free from the grip of the enemies
- सुनीतिः (*sunītiḥ*)—as an excellent guide for us
- भव (*bhava*)—be
- उत (*uta*)—and
- वामनीतिः (*vāmanītiḥ*)—be also one who supplies attractive wealth

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, be right in front of us and look upon us fully. Supply us with excellent wealth. Bring us to the end of sorrow. Free us from the grip of the enemies. Be for us an excellent guide and also one who supplies attractive wealth.

### Printed p. 190

##### English translation
Like one who goes before us, Indra, (on the road), look out, bring before us infinite wealth ; be our conductor beyond the bounds (of want) convey us safely over (peril); be our careful guide, our guide to desirable (affluence).

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒रुं नो॑ लो॒कमनु॑ नॆषि वि॒द्वान्स॑र्व॒ज्योति॒रभ॑यं  
> स्व॒स्ति ।  
> ऋ॒ष्वा त॑ इंद्र॒ स्थवि॑रस्य बा॒हू उप॑ स्थेयाम शर॒णा  ॒॑
> बृहंता ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उरुं । नः । लोकं । अनु । नेषि । विद्वान् । स्वःवत् । ज्योतिः । अभयं । स्वस्ति ।  
> ऋष्वा । ते । इंद्र । स्थविरस्य । बाहू इति । उप । स्थेयाम । शरणा । बृहंता ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऐंद्रे पशावुरुं न इत्येषा वपाया याज्या । सूत्रितं च । उरुं नो
> लोकमनु नेषि विद्वान् प्र ससाहिषे पुरुहूत शत्रून् । आ. ३-७ । इति ॥ होत्रकैः
> सदःप्रसर्पणसमयेऽप्येषा जप्या । सूत्रितं च । उरुं नो लोकमनु नेषि
> विद्वानिति जपंतः । आ. ५-३ । इति ॥ चातुर्विंशिकेऽहन्यहर्गणेषु च द्वितीयादिष्वहःसु
> माध्यंदिनसवनेऽच्छवाकस्यैषा चारंभणीया सूत्रितं च । उरुं नो लोकमनु
> नेषि विद्वानिति कद्वद्भ्य आरंभणीयाः । आ. ७-४. इति ॥
> हे इंद्र विद्वान् जानंस्त्वमुरुं विस्तीर्णं लोकं स्थानं स्वर्गाख्यं
> नोऽस्माननु नेषि । अनुगमय । तथा स्वर्वत्सुखवदभयं भयरहितं ज्योतिश्च स्वस्ति
> क्षेमेणानु नेषि । आस्माननुगमय । हे इंद्र स्थविरस्य स्कूलस्य वृद्धस्य ते तव
> संबंधिनावृष्ट्वा दर्शनीयौ बृहंता महांतौ बाहू त्वदीयौ हस्तौ शरणा शरणौ
> रक्षकौ रक्षकतयोपस्थेयाम । उपतिष्ठेम । सेवेमहि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the animal sacrifice to Indra this Ṛk, उरुं नः (*uruṃ naḥ*), is the yājyā for the omentum (vapā); and it is prescribed in the sūtra: उरुं नो लोकमनु नेषि विद्वान् प्र ससाहिषे पुरुहूत शत्रून् (*uruṃ no lokamanu neṣi vidvān pra sasāhiṣe puruhūta śatrūn*) (Ā. 3-7). The assistants of the Hotṛ also mutter it when they approach the sadas, as the sūtra says: उरुं नो लोकमनु नेषि विद्वानिति जपंतः (*uruṃ no lokamanu neṣi vidvāniti japaṃtaḥ*) (Ā. 5-3). On the Cāturviṃśika day, and on the second and following days in the ahargaṇas, at the midday pressing it is the opening verse (ārambhaṇīyā) of the Acchāvāka, as the sūtra says: उरुं नो लोकमनु नेषि विद्वानिति कद्वद्भ्य आरंभणीयाः (*uruṃ no lokamanu neṣi vidvāniti kadvadbhya āraṃbhaṇīyāḥ*) (Ā. 7-4). O Indra, you who know, lead us (अनु नेषि (*anu neṣi*)), cause us to reach, the wide (उरुं (*uruṃ*)) world (लोकं (*lokaṃ*)), the place called heaven; and, with well-being (स्वस्ति (*svasti*)), lead us to light that is full of happiness (स्वर्वत् (*svarvat*)) and free from fear. O Indra, your two arms, lovely (ऋष्वा (*ṛṣvā*)) to behold and great (बृहंता (*bṛhaṃtā*)), belonging to you who are stout (स्थविरस्य (*sthavirasya*)), that is, big and old, as protectors (शरणा (*śaraṇā*)): may we resort to, serve, those arms of yours (उपस्थेयाम (*upastheyāma*)).

### Printed p. 191

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—you who know everything
- स्वस्ति (*svasti*)—so that well-being comes to us
- स्वर्वत् (*svarvat*)—that is full of happiness
- अभयं (*abhayaṃ*)—that is free from fear
- ज्योतिः (*jyotiḥ*)—that is radiant
- उरुं (*uruṃ*)—and wide
- लोकं (*lokaṃ*)—to the place
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- अनु नेषि (*anu neṣi*)—lead and bring
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- स्थविरस्य (*sthavirasya*)—of you who are of firm body
- ते (*te*)—your
- ऋष्वा (*ṛṣvā*)—attractive
- बृहंता (*bṛhaṃtā*)—and long
- बाहू (*bāhū*)—arms
- शरणा (*śaraṇā*)—so that they are protectors for us
- उपस्थेयाम (*upastheyāma*)—may we take refuge in them and be happy

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you who know everything, lead us and bring us, so that well-being comes to us, to a place that is full of happiness, free from fear, peaceful and wide. O Indra, may we take refuge in your attractive and long arms, you who are of firm body, so that they are protectors for us, and be happy.

##### English translation
Do you, Indra, who are wise, conduct us to the spacious world (of heaven), to a blessed state of happiness, light, and safety : may we recline in the graceful, protecting, and mighty arms of you the ancient one.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Although there is a difference of opinion on the question whether the gods have hands, feet and other limbs as human beings have, it is seen in the Ṛgveda that the seers praised Indra and the other gods, thinking of them as having hands, feet and other limbs. In this matter Yāska has given the explanation: अथाकारचिंतनं देवतानां । पुरुषविधाः स्युरित्येकं चेतनावद्वद्धि स्तुतयो भवंति । तथाभिधानानि । अथापि पौरुषविधिकैरंगैः संस्तूयंते । ऋष्वात इंद्र स्थविरस्य बाहू । यत्संगृभ्णा मघवन् काशिरित्ते ॥ (*athākāraciṃtanaṃ devatānāṃ | puruṣavidhāḥ syurityekaṃ cetanāvadvaddhi stutayo bhavaṃti | tathābhidhānāni | athāpi pauruṣavidhikairaṃgaiḥ saṃstūyaṃte | ṛṣvāta iṃdra sthavirasya bāhū | yatsaṃgṛbhṇā maghavan kāśiritte ‖*) (Ni. 7-6). Now let us examine the form, shape and so on of the gods. It is the opinion of some that the gods have hands and other limbs, as men have. Thinking that, as men have consciousness, so the gods also have consciousness, intelligence and so on, and since the gods also have limbs such as hands, and a form, as men have, they are thought of as endowed with consciousness,

### Printed p. 192

and they are praised accordingly. For this very reason it is the custom, when describing Indra and the other gods, to describe their hands and so on. In the third pāda of the present Ṛk, ऋष्वात इंद्र स्थविरस्य बाहू (*ṛṣvāta iṃdra sthavirasya bāhū*), there is a description of the arms of Indra. Likewise the authors of the Nirukta have cited, as one in which also there is a description of the limbs of Indra, the Ṛk: उताभये पुरुहूत श्रवोभिरेको दृळ्मवदो वृत्रहा सन् । इमे चिदिंद्र रोदसी अपारे यत्संगृभ्णा मघवन् काशिरित्ते ॥ (*utābhaye puruhūta śravobhireko dṛḷmavado vṛtrahā san | ime cidiṃdra rodasī apāre yatsaṃgṛbhṇā maghavan kāśiritte ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-30-5) **GLOSS**

There are many descriptions saying that the gods have, besides the forms of men and the like, also union with various objects, as men have: अथापि पौरुषविधिकैर्द्रव्यसंयोगैः (*athāpi pauruṣavidhikairdravyasaṃyogaiḥ*) (Ni. 7-6). In this matter, after giving the example of the Ṛk आ द्वाभ्यां हरिभ्यामिंद्र याह्या चतुर्भिरा षड्भिर्हूयमानः । आष्टाभिर्दशभिः सोमपेयमयं सुतः सुमख मा मृधस्कः । (*ā dvābhyāṃ haribhyāmiṃdra yāhyā caturbhirā ṣaḍbhirhūyamānaḥ | āṣṭābhirdaśabhiḥ somapeyamayaṃ sutaḥ sumakha mā mṛdhaskaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-18-4), it is clearly explained in this Ṛk that, as men have cows, horses and the like, so Indra and the other gods also have them. "O Indra, come to our sacrifice, seated in your chariot, together with your two horses named Hari; or else come here to drink Soma together with four, six, eight or ten horses": since this is said, the idea becomes evident that, as in worldly dealings men have possessions, so the gods also have horses and the like. And further: अपाः सोममस्तमिंद्र प्र याहि कल्याणीर्जाया सुरणं गृहे ते । यत्रा रथस्य बृहतो निधानं विमोचनं वाजिनो दक्षिणावत् ॥ (*apāḥ somamastamiṃdra pra yāhi kalyāṇīrjāyā suraṇaṃ gṛhe te | yatrā rathasya bṛhato nidhānaṃ vimocanaṃ vājino dakṣiṇāvat ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-53-6). In this Ṛk there is a mention of the fact that, as men have, so Indra also has at home a wife, a chariot and horses.

Further, they have defended, by giving some examples, the view that the gods also have actions like the actions of men: अथापि पौरुषविधिकैः कर्मभिः (*athāpi pauruṣavidhikaiḥ karmabhiḥ*) (Ni. 7-6).

### Printed p. 193

इदं हविर्मघवन् तुभ्यं रातं प्रति सम्राळहृणानो गृभाय । तुभ्यं सुतो मघवन् तुभ्यं पक्वोत्थींद्र पिब च प्रस्थितस्य ॥ (*idaṃ havirmaghavan tubhyaṃ rātaṃ prati samrāḷahṛṇāno gṛbhāya | tubhyaṃ suto maghavan tubhyaṃ pakvotthīṃdra piba ca prasthitasya ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-16-7). आश्रुत्कर्ण श्रुधी हवं नू चिद्दधिष्ट मे गिरः । इंद्र स्तोममिमं मम कृष्णा युजश्चिदंतरं ॥ (*āśrutkarṇa śrudhī havaṃ nū ciddadhiṣṭa me giraḥ | iṃdra stomamimaṃ mama kṛṣṇā yujaścidaṃtaraṃ ‖*). These Ṛks have been given as examples. In the first Ṛk it is said that an oblation and Soma have been offered for Indra, and it is prayed that Indra should drink it. In the second Ṛk there is a prayer that he should hear our invocation and our hymn. That is, actions fitting to men, such as eating the oblation as men do, drinking the Soma juice, and listening to and hearing the invocation and the hymn, are ascribed also to Indra and the other gods in praising them. In this way it is the contention of some that, like men, the gods also have form, hands and other limbs, and actions such as eating. Others hold that the gods have no form and so on as men have, that their actions are also different from the actions that men perform, and that the gods are described in that way, by exaggeration, in order to describe their greatness: अपुरुषविधाः स्युरित्यपरं (*apuruṣavidhāḥ syurityaparaṃ*) (Ni. 7-7).

Still others hold that the gods have no form, shape, actions and the like as men have, and that their distinguishing marks are of another kind. How? अपि तु यद्दृश्यतेऽपुरुषविधं तद्यथाग्निर्वायुरादित्यः पृथिवी चंद्रमा इति ॥ (*api tu yaddṛśyate 'puruṣavidhaṃ tadyathāgnirvāyurādityaḥ pṛthivī caṃdramā iti ‖*) (Ni. 7-7). Although the forms and actions of some gods appear as those of men, they are not so. Although the forms and actions of such gods as Agni, Vāyu, Āditya, Pṛthivī and Candramā appear like the actions of men, their manner is different. There is a particular difference between those actions and the actions of men.

यथो एतच्चेतनावद्वद्धि स्तुतयो भवंतीत्यचेतनान्यप्येपं स्तूयंते यथाक्षप्रभृतीनोषधिपर्यंतानि ॥ (*yatho etaccetanāvadvaddhi stutayo bhavaṃtītyacetanānyapyepaṃ stūyaṃte yathākṣaprabhṛtīnoṣadhiparyaṃtāni ‖*) (Ni. 7-7). That is, when gods such as Agni are praised, they are praised as though they had consciousness. When gambling, people address the dice; though the dice are devoid of consciousness, they address them as living beings with consciousness. They address plants as well, that is, shrubs, trees and creepers, thinking of them as having consciousness.

### Printed p. 194

यथो एतत्पौरुषविधिकैरंगैः संस्तूयंत इत्यचेतनेष्वप्येतद्भवत्यभिक्रदंति हरितेभिरासभिरिति ग्रावस्तुतिः ॥ (*yatho etatpauruṣavidhikairaṃgaiḥ saṃstūyaṃta ityacetaneṣvapyetadbhavatyabhikradaṃti haritebhirāsabhiriti grāvastutiḥ ‖*) (Ni. 7-7). In the same way, things without consciousness are also praised, as though they had limbs belonging to men. Plants and the stones that crush Soma are without consciousness. Yet the sound that arises when the Soma plants are crushed with stones is described by saying that those stones cry out with their green mouths. Since stones are without consciousness, they have no mouths as living beings have. Yet the seer has described in a figurative way the sound that is made when a plant is placed on a stone and crushed, as the stones making it with their own mouths. When Soma plants are crushed, the green colour of those plants clings to the stones; to suggest that the mouths of the stones are of green colour, a description with exaggeration has been made, that the mouths of the stones are of green colour.

यथो एतत्पौरुषविधिकैर्द्रव्यसंयोगैरित्येतदपि तादृशमेव । सुखं रथं युयुजे सिंधुरश्विनं इति नदीस्तुतिः ॥ (*yatho etatpauruṣavidhikairdravyasaṃyogairityetadapi tādṛśameva | sukhaṃ rathaṃ yuyuje siṃdhuraśvinaṃ iti nadīstutiḥ ‖*) (Ni. 7-7). In the matter of things without consciousness also having union with objects, as men have: as in sentences such as "the river (Sindhu) yoked her horses to a pleasant chariot", the river, being a thing without consciousness, has no chariot. Yet, in praising the river, the seer has praised it by ascribing to the river, as to men, union with objects (the possession of property).

यथो एतत्पौरुषविधिकैः कर्मभिरित्येतदपि तादृशमेव । होतुश्चित्पूर्वे हविरद्यमाशत इति ग्रावस्तु तिरेव ॥ (*yatho etatpauruṣavidhikaiḥ karmabhirityetadapi tādṛśameva | hotuścitpūrve haviradyamāśata iti grāvastu tireva ‖*) (Ni. 7-7). In the matter of actions also, agency in action has been ascribed, as to men, to things without consciousness such as stones, and they have been praised. When the Soma plant is crushed with a stone, it is natural that the juice that comes out of the plant touches the stone. This is what the seer has described by saying that the stones, the means of pressing the Soma, taste the Soma juice before the sacrificers such as the Hotṛ. A stone has no mouth, and it has no capacity to taste. Yet, since the juice that comes out when the plant is crushed with the stone touches the stone, the description that the stone tastes the Soma juice before everyone is made with exaggeration and in a figurative way. That stones, being without consciousness, have neither a mouth nor the capacity to taste is a matter established by the experience of all.

अपि वा पुरुषविधानामेव सतां कर्मात्मन एते स्युर्यथा यज्ञो यजमानस्य । एष चाख्यानसमयः ॥ (*api vā puruṣavidhānāmeva satāṃ karmātmana ete syuryathā yajño yajamānasya | eṣa cākhyānasamayaḥ ‖*) (Ni. 7-7) **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 195

Therefore there is room for both kinds of views. Although the earth and the other such things are without consciousness, they act as though they were endowed with consciousness; so, although there is a difference of opinion in regarding them as conscious or as unconscious, both ways may be accepted as right. In the sentence यज्ञो यज्ञमानस्य (*yajño yajñamānasya*) the sacrificer is endowed with consciousness, while the sacrifice is without consciousness. Yet when the sacrifice is spoken of, in sentences such as "the sacrifice provides the sacrificer with heaven, the best of places", the sacrifice is treated just as if it were endowed with consciousness. Therefore there is no fault in describing unconscious things as if they were endowed with consciousness. To give an example: पृथिवी स्त्रीरूपेण भारावतरणाय ब्रह्माणं ययाचे । अग्निश्च ब्राह्मणरूपेण वासुदेवार्जुनावुभौ खांडवं ययाचे । पुरुषरूपेणाग्निरूपेण च खांडवं ददाह । (*pṛthivī strīrūpeṇa bhārāvataraṇāya brahmāṇaṃ yayāce | agniśca brāhmaṇarūpeṇa vāsudevārjunāvubhau khāṃḍavaṃ yayāce | puruṣarūpeṇāgnirūpeṇa ca khāṃḍavaṃ dadāha |*) — the earth, taking the form of a woman, prayed to Brahmā that her burden be lifted; Agni, in the form of a brāhmaṇa, approached Kṛṣṇa and Arjuna and begged of them Indra's Khāṇḍava forest; Agni, in the form of a man and of fire, burnt the Khāṇḍava forest. In such sentences, and in the Purāṇas and other works, there are many descriptions saying that lifeless things such as Agni and the earth, or their presiding deities, took the form of persons and performed various acts. Therefore the main purport of the Nirukta is that the conclusion is that it is right to regard these as both conscious and unconscious, in two ways.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वरि॑ष्ठे न इंद्र वं॒धुरे॑ धा॒ वहि॑ष्टयोः शताव॒न्नश्व॑यो॒रा ।  
> इष॒मा व॑क्षी॒षां वर्षि॑ष्ठां॒ मा न॑स्तारीन्मघव॒न्रायो॑ अ॒र्यः॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वरिष्टे । नः । इंद्र । वंधुरे । धाः । वहिष्टयोः । शतवन् । अश्वयोः । आ ।  
> इषं । आ । वक्षि । इषां । वर्षिष्ठां । मा । नः । तारीत् । मघवन् । रायः । अर्यः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र वरिष्ठ उरुतमे विस्तीर्णतमे वंधुर आत्मीये रथे नोऽस्मान्
> धाः । धेहि ! धारय । अपि च हे शतावन् शतसंख्यधनेंद्र वहिष्ठयोर्वोढृतमयोः । आकारः समुच्चये । अश्वयोरा त्वदीययोरश्वयोश्चास्मान् धारय ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa glosses the verse thus. O Indra, in your own chariot, which is the most spacious (वरिष्ठे (*variṣṭhe*) means the most extensive), place us, hold us. And, O Śatāvan, Indra of a hundredfold wealth, place us also upon your two horses, the best at bearing; the particle आ (*ā*) has the sense of "and", so that he asks to be set upon the chariot and upon his horses as well.

### Printed p. 196

> तथेषामन्नानां मध्ये वर्षिष्टां वृद्धतमामिषमन्नमा वक्षि । अस्मदर्थमावह ।
> आनय । हे मघवन् धनवन्निंद्र अर्यो धनस्य स्वाम्यन्यः कश्चिन्नोस्माकं रायो
> धनानि मा तारीत् । मा हिंसीत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Likewise, from among these foods, bring the most abundant food, इषं (*iṣaṃ*), for us: carry it here, fetch it. O Maghavan, wealthy Indra, let no other, any master of wealth (अर्यः (*aryaḥ*)), harm our riches (रायः (*rāyaḥ*), "wealth"): the verb मा तारीत् (*mā tārīt*) is explained as "may he not injure".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- वरिष्ठे (*variṣṭhe*)—in the most extensive
- वंधुरे (*vaṃdhure*)—in your chariot
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- धाः (*dhāḥ*)—seat
- शतावन् (*śatāvan*)—O Indra, possessor of wealth reckoned in hundreds
- वहिष्ठयोः (*vahiṣṭhayoḥ*)—fit to bear your chariot
- अश्वयोः (*aśvayoḥ*)—behind the horses
- आ (*ā*)—(place) us
- इषं (*iṣaṃ*)—from among food of all kinds
- वर्षिष्टां (*varṣiṣṭāṃ*)—the most excellent
- इषं (*iṣaṃ*)—food
- आ वक्षि (*ā vakṣi*)—provide to us
- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O Indra, possessed of wealth
- अर्यः (*aryaḥ*)—no other who is lord of wealth
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- रायः (*rāyaḥ*)—wealth
- मा तारीत् (*mā tārīt*)—let him not harm

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, possessor of wealth reckoned in hundreds, seat us in your most spacious chariot, behind the horses fit to bear your chariot. From among food of all kinds, provide us with the most excellent food. O wealthy Indra, let no other who is lord of wealth harm (carry off) our wealth.

##### English translation
Place us, possessor of riches, in your ample chariot, (behind) your powerful horses ; bring to us from among all viands the most excellent food : let not, Maghavan, any opulent man surpass us in wealth.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॑ मृ॒ळ मह्यं॑ जी॒वातु॑मिच्छ चो॒दय॒ धिय॒मय॑सो॒  ॑
> न धारां ।  
> यत्किं चा॒हं त्वा॒युरि॒दं वदा॑मि॒ तज्जु॑षस्व कृ॒धि मा॑ दे॒ववं॑तं ॥ १० ॥

### Printed p. 197

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्र । मृळ । मह्यं । जीवातुं । इच्छ । चोदय । धियं । आयसः । न । धारां ।  
> यत् । किं । च । अहं । त्वायुः । इदं । वदामि । तत् । जुषस्व । कृधि । मा ।  
> देववंतं ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र मृळ । मृळय । अस्मान् सुखय । जीवातुं जीवनौषधं च मह्यं
> दातुमिच्छ । तथा धियं बुद्धिं चोदय । स्तुतिविषयां कर्मविषयां वा प्रेरय ।
> तीक्ष्मीकुरु । आयसो न धारामयोमयस्य खड्गादेर्धारामिव । सा यथा सूक्ष्मा
> तद्वत्सूक्ष्मविषयां धियं चोदयेत्यर्थः । किं च त्वायुस्मामात्मन इच्छन्
> इदमिदानीं यत्किंचनाहं वदामि समीचीनमसमीचीनं वा तज्जुषस्व । मा मां
> च देववंतं रक्षकैर्देवैरुपेतं कृधि । कुरु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, make us happy, gladden us. Wish to give me also जीवातुं (*jīvātuṃ*), the medicine of life. Likewise urge on my mind (धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)), whether it is directed to praise or to ritual action: sharpen it. आयसो (*āyaso*) न (*na*) धारामयोमयस्य (*dhārāmayomayasya*) means like the edge of a sword and the like made of iron; as that edge is fine, so urge a mind directed to fine matters. Further, desiring you for myself (त्वायुः (*tvāyuḥ*)), whatever I now say, whether fitting or unfitting, take pleasure in it. And make me one who has the gods, the protectors, with him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- मृळ (*mṛḷa*)—make me happy
- मह्यं (*mahyaṃ*)—to me
- जीवातुं (*jīvātuṃ*)—long life
- इच्छॆ (*icchè*)—desire
- अयसः (*ayasaḥ*)—of the sword made of iron
- धारां (*dhārāṃ*)—as the edge is made sharp
- धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)—my mind, directed to ritual action, make sharp
- त्वायुः (*tvāyuḥ*)—desiring your help
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- यत् किं च (*yat kiṃ ca*)—whatever, fitting or unfitting
- इदं वदामि (*idaṃ vadāmi*)—whatever praise of you I recite
- तत् (*tat*)—that
- जुषस्व (*juṣasva*)—enjoy
- मा (*mā*)—me
- देववंतं (*devavaṃtaṃ*)—as one joined with the protections of the gods
- कृधि (*kṛdhi*)—make

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, make me happy. Desire long life for me. As the edge of a sword made of iron is made sharp, make my mind, directed to ritual action, sharp. Desiring your help, whatever praise I offer, whether it be good or not good, enjoy it. Make me one joined with the protections of the gods.

##### English translation
Make me happy, Indra ; be pleased to prolong my life : sharpen my intellect like the edge of an iron sword : whatever, desirous (propitiating) you, I may utter, be pleased by it : render me the object of divine protection.

### Printed p. 198

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्रा॒तार॒मिंद्र॑मवि॒तार॒मिंद्रं॒ हवे॑हवे सु॒हवं॒ शूर॒मिंद्रं॑।  
> ह्वया॑मि श॒क्रं पु॑रुहू॒तमिंद्रं॑ स्व॒स्ति नो॑ म॒घवा॑ धा॒त्विंद्रः॑॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्रातारं । इद्रं । अवितारं । इंद्रं । हवेऽहवे । सुहवं । शूरं । इंद्रं ।  
> ह्वयामि । शक्रं । पुरुहूतं । इंद्रं । स्वस्ति । नः । मघवा । धातु । इंद्रः ॥११॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आयुष्कामेष्ट्यमिंद्रस्य त्रातुस्त्रातारमित्यनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च । प्रातारमिंद्रमवितारमिंद्रं मा ते अस्यां सहसावन्परिष्टौ । आ. २-१० । इति ॥
> त्रातारं शत्रुभ्यः पालयितारमिंद्रमाह्वयामि । तथावितारं कामैस्तर्पयितारमिंद्रमाह्वयामि हवे हवे सर्वेष्वाहवनेषु सुहवं सुखेनाह्वातुं शक्यं
> शूरं शौर्यवंतं शक्रं सर्वकार्येषु शक्तं पुरुहूतं पुरुभिर्बहुभिः पालनार्थमाहूतमेवंविधमिंद्रं हृयामि । आह्वयामि । एवमाहूतो मघवा धनवान् स
> इंद्रः स्वस्त्यविनाशं नोऽस्मभ्यं धातु । ददातु । यद्वा । अस्माकं विदधातु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the Āyuṣkāmeṣṭi, the ritual for one who desires long life, the verse त्रातारं (*trātāraṃ*), addressed to Indra the protector, is the anuvākyā. It is also laid down in the sūtra: प्रातारमिंद्रमवितारमिंद्रं मा ते अस्यां सहसावन्परिष्टौ । (*prātāramiṃdramavitāramiṃdraṃ mā te asyāṃ sahasāvanpariṣṭau |*) (Ā. 2-10). **NOTE**. I call Indra, the protector who guards from enemies; likewise Indra, the satisfier who gratifies with desired things. At every invocation, at all calls, he is सुहवं (*suhavaṃ*), easily called; शूरं (*śūraṃ*), heroic; शक्रं (*śakraṃ*), able in all undertakings; पुरुहूतं (*puruhūtaṃ*), called by many for protection. Such an Indra I call. Thus invoked, may Indra, the wealthy मघवा (*maghavā*), grant us स्वस्ति (*svasti*), freedom from destruction; or else, may he arrange it for us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- त्रातारं (*trātāraṃ*)—the protector
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- ह्ययामि (*hyayāmi*)—I call with praise
- अवितारं (*avitāraṃ*)—who gives desired things and satisfies
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra (I call)
- हवेहवे (*havehave*)—at every time of invocation
- सुहवं (*suhavaṃ*)—who readily grants our prayer
- शूरं (*śūraṃ*)—the valiant
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—the Lord of all
- शक्रं (*śakraṃ*)—capable in all acts
- पुरुहूतं (*puruhūtaṃ*)—called by many
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra (I call with praise)
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—wealthy
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- स्वस्ति (*svasti*)—welfare
- धातु (*dhātu*)—let him bring about

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In this sacrifice I call with praise Indra the protector. I call Indra who gives desired things and satisfies. At every time of invocation, who readily grants our prayer,

### Printed p. 199

who is valiant, the Lord of all, capable in all acts, called by many, Indra, I call with praise. May the wealthy Indra bring about welfare for us.

##### English translation
I invoke, at repeated sacrifices, Indra, the preserver. the protector, the hero, who is easily propitiated, Indra the powerful, the invoked of many : may Indra, the lord of affluence, bestow upon us prosperity.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The sacrificer is to recite this verse beginning त्रातारं (*trātāraṃ*) as the anuvākyā mantra in the Āyuṣkāmeṣṭi; this is set out in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra by the sūtra त्रातारमिंद्रमवितारमिंद्रं मा ते आस्त्यां सहसावन्परिष्टौ (*trātāramiṃdramavitāramiṃdraṃ mā te āstyāṃ sahasāvanpariṣṭau*) (Ā. 2-10).

#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रः॑ सु॒त्रामा॒ स्ववा॒ अवो॑भिः सुमृळी॒को भ॑वतु वि॒श्ववे॑दाः।  
> बाध॑तां॒ द्वेषो॒ अभ॑यं कृणोतु सु॒वीर्य॑स्य॒ पत॑यः  
> स्याम ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रः । सुत्रामा । स्ववान् । अवभिः । सुवृळीकः । भवतु । विश्ववेदाः ।  
> बाधतां । द्वेषः । अभयं । कृणोतु । सुवीर्यस्य । पतयः । स्याम ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सुत्रामा सुष्टु त्राता स्ववान्थनवान् स इंद्रोऽवोभी रक्षणैः सुमृळीकः
> सुष्टु सुखयिता भवतु । तथा च विश्ववेदाः सर्वधनः सर्वविद्वान्वा स इंद्रो
> द्वेषो द्वेष्टन् शत्र्यान् बाधतां । हिनस्तु । अभयं भयरहितं चास्माकं
> कृणोतु । करोतु । वयं च तत्पसादात्सुवीर्यस्य शोभनवीर्यस्य पतयः
> स्याम । भवेम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

सुत्रामा (*sutrāmā*) is the good protector, स्ववान् (*svavān*) the wealthy one. May that Indra, with his protections (अवोभिः (*avobhiḥ*)), be a good giver of happiness (सुमृळीकः (*sumṛḷīkaḥ*)). And likewise may Indra, विश्ववेदाः (*viśvavedāḥ*), he who has all wealth or who knows everything, oppress those who hate us, our enemies (द्वेषः (*dveṣaḥ*)). May he make us free from fear (अभयं (*abhayaṃ*)). And by his grace may we become lords (पतयः (*patayaḥ*)) of सुवीर्यस्य (*suvīryasya*), good heroism.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सुत्रामा (*sutrāmā*)—who protects exceedingly
- स्ववान् (*svavān*)—wealthy
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- अवोभिः (*avobhiḥ*)—with his protections
- सुमृळीकः (*sumṛḷīkaḥ*)—as one who brings excellent happiness

### Printed p. 200

- भवतु (*bhavatu*)—let him be
- विश्ववेदाः (*viśvavedāḥ*)—Indra who knows everything
- द्वेषः (*dveṣaḥ*)—the enemies who hate us
- बाधतां (*bādhatāṃ*)—let him harm
- आभयं (*ābhayaṃ*)—freedom from fear for us
- कृणोतु (*kṛṇotu*)—let him bring about
- सुवीरस्य (*suvīrasya*)—of all the wealth that is excellent and strong
- पतयः (*patayaḥ*)—as lords
- स्याम (*syāma*)—may he favour us that we become

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May Indra, who protects exceedingly and is wealthy, be, with his protections, one who brings excellent happiness. May Indra, who knows everything, harm the enemies who hate us. May he bring about freedom from fear for us. May we become lords of all the wealth that is excellent and strong.

##### English translation
May the protecting, opulent Indra be the bestower of feiicity by his protections : may he, who is all-knowing, foil our adversaries : may he keep us out of danger, and may we be the possessors of excellent posterity. **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 13

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तस्य॑ व॒यं सु॑म॒तौ य॒ज्जिय॒स्यापि॑ भ॒द्रॆ सौ॑मन॒से स्या॑म ।  
> स सु॒त्रामा॒ स्ववा॒ इंद्रो॑ अ॒स्मे आ॒राचि॒द्द्वेषः॑ सनु॒तर्यु॑योतु ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तस्य । वयं । सुमतौ । यज्जियस्य । अपि । भद्रॆ । सौमनसे । स्याम ।  
> स । सुत्रामा । स्ववान् । इंद्रः । अस्मे इति । आरात् । चित् । द्वेषः सनुतः ।  
> युयोतु ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यज्ञियस्य यज्ञार्हस्य सुमतौ शोभनायामनुग्रहात्मिकायां बुद्धौ
> वयं स्याम । तद्विषयभूता भवेम । तथा भद्रे कल्याणे सौमनसे सुमनसो
> भावेऽपि तदीये स्याम । भवेम । स इंद्रोऽस्मास्वनुग्रहात्मिकां बुद्धिं सौमनस्यं

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Of the one worthy of sacrifice (यज्ञियस्य (*yajñiyasya*)), that is, deserving the sacrifice, may we be in his good (सुमतौ (*sumatau*)), gracious thought, a mind of favour; may we become the objects of it. Likewise may we be in his auspicious good-mindedness (सौमनसे (*saumanase*)), that is, in the state of a good mind, belonging to him. That Indra, towards us, a mind of favour, good-mindedness.

### Printed p. 201

> च करोत्वित्यर्थः । सुत्रामा सुष्टु त्राता स्ववान्थनवान् स इंद्रश्चास्मे अस्मत्त
> आराच्चित् दूरदेश एव द्वेषो द्वेष्टान् सनुतः । अंतर्हितनामैतत् । अंतर्हितानुयोतु । पृथक्करोतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

च करोत्वित्यर्थः (*ca karotvityarthaḥ*): "and may he make it so" is the sense of what precedes. सुत्रामा (*sutrāmā*) is the good protector, स्ववान् (*svavān*) the wealthy one; that Indra, far from us (अस्मे (*asme*) is the same as "from us"), at a distance (आराच्चित् (*ārāccit*), in a distant region only), the haters (द्वेषः (*dveṣaḥ*), those who hate us) सनुतः (*sanutaḥ*), a word meaning "hidden": may he separate them hidden away (युयोतु (*yuyotu*), may he put them apart).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- तस्य यज्ञियस्य (*tasya yajñiyasya*)—of that Indra who is worthy of worship
- सुमत् (*sumat*)—becoming objects of his gracious thought
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- स्याम (*syāma*)—may we be
- भद्रे (*bhadre*)—that bestows welfare
- सौ मनसे अपि (*sau manase api*)—may we be objects of his affection also
- सुत्रामा (*sutrāmā*)—who protects exceedingly
- स्ववान् (*svavān*)—and is wealthy
- स इंद्रः (*sa iṃdraḥ*)—that same Indra
- द्वेषः (*dveṣaḥ*)—the enemies who hate us
- अस्मे (*asme*)—than ourselves
- आराच्चित् (*ārāccit*)—in a most distant region only
- सनुतः (*sanutaḥ*)—so that they are not seen by the eye
- युयोतु (*yuyotu*)—let him keep them apart, so that they do not join us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May we be the objects of the gracious thought of that same Indra, who is worthy of worship. May we be the objects of his bestowing affection also. May that same Indra, who protects exceedingly and is wealthy, keep the enemies who hate us in a most distant region, far from us, out of our sight, and apart, so that they do not join us.

##### English translation
May we continue in the favour of that adorable (deity) even in his auspicious good-will : may that protecting and opulent Indra drive far from us, into extinction, all those who hate us.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The word अस्मे (*asme*): Yāska says that this word is used in all seven case-meanings, and as an example he cites from this Ṛk the passage अस्मे आराच्चिद्वेषः सनुतर्युयोतु (*asme ārāccidveṣaḥ sanutaryuyotu*). We have explained the seven kinds of case-usage of the word अस्मे (*asme*) in detail in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 10, p. 170.

#### Ṛk 14

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अव॒ त्वे इं॑द्र प्र॒वतो॒ नोर्मिगिरो॒ ब्रह्मा॑णि नि॒युतो॑  
> धवंते ।  
> उ॒रू न राधः॒ सव॑ना पु॒रुण्य॒पोगा व॑ज्रिन्युवसे॒ समिंदू॑न्॥

### Printed p. 202

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आव । त्वे इति । इंद्र । प्रवतः । न । ऊर्मिः । गिरः । ब्रह्माणि । नियुतः । धवंते ।  
> उरु । न । राधः । सवना । पुरूणि । अपः । गाः । वज्रिन् । युवसे । सं । इंदून् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वे त्वां नियुतः स्तोतुर्गिर उक्तात्मिका वाचो ब्रह्माणि
> सामरूपाणि स्तोत्राणि चाव धवंते । अवगच्छंति । तत्र दृष्टांतः । ऊर्मिर्जलसंघः
> प्रवतो न प्रवणदेशानिव । यथोच्चित्तात्‌स्थलान्निम्म देशं शीघ्रं गच्छति] तद्वत् ।
> नशब्दः समुच्चये । उरूरूणि बहु राधो राधांसि च हविर्लक्षणानि धनानि
> पुरूणि बहूनि सवना सवनानि बहवोऽभिषुताः सोमाश्च त्वामेवाभिगच्छंति । हे
> वज्रिन् वज्रवन्निंद्र अपो वसतीवर्याख्या गा गोविकारान् कृतदध्यादीन्
> श्रपणार्थानिंदून् सोमांश्च सं युवसे । सम्यक् मिश्रयसि । त्वां यष्टुं
> वसतीवरीभिरभिषुत्य तं सोममाशिरादिभिः श्रपणद्रव्यैः संस्कुर्वंतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, to you (त्वे (*tve*) is "to you") the speech of the praiser (नियुतः (*niyutaḥ*) is glossed as "of the praiser"), that is, utterance in words, and the stotras in the form of Sāman (ब्रह्माणि (*brahmāṇi*)), अव धवंते (*ava dhavaṃte*), run toward you. The illustration is the wave, a mass of water, as it runs quickly to low-lying places, from a high place to a low region; so they run. The word न (*na*) here is in the sense of "and" (समुच्चये (*samuccaye*)). Much wealth (राधः (*rādhaḥ*)), riches in the form of oblations, and many pressings (सवना (*savanā*)), the many pressed Soma juices, come to you alone. O Vajrin, Indra bearing the thunderbolt, you mingle (सं युवसे (*saṃ yuvase*), you blend well) the waters called वसतीवरी (*vasatīvarī*), the products of the cow, curds and the like that serve for cooking, and the Soma juices. The meaning is that, for the sacrificer who offers to you, they press the Soma with the वसतीवरी (*vasatīvarī*) waters and prepare that Soma with the cooking materials āśir and the like.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- ऊर्मिः (*ūrmiḥ*)—a flow of water
- प्रवतो न (*pravato na*)—as it runs swiftly in low-lying places
- नियुतः (*niyutaḥ*)—of the praiser
- गिरः (*giraḥ*)—words of utterance
- ब्रह्माणि (*brahmāṇi*)—and stotras in the form of Sāman
- त्वे (*tve*)—to you
- अव धवंते (*ava dhavaṃte*)—come swiftly and join
- वज्रिन् (*vajrin*)—O Indra, bearer of the thunderbolt
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- उरु (*uru*)—abundantly
- राधः (*rādhaḥ*)—riches in the form of oblations
- पुरूणि (*purūṇi*)—of many kinds
- सवना (*savanā*)—pressed Soma juices
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—waters called वसतीवरी (*vasatīvarī*)
- गाः (*gāḥ*)—milk and the like, products of the cow
- इंदून् (*iṃdūn*)—Soma juices
- सं युवसे (*saṃ yuvase*)—you bring together

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, as a flow of water runs swiftly in low-lying places, so the words of the praiser and the stotras in the form of Sāman come swiftly and join you. O Indra, bearer of the thunderbolt, you bring together abundantly the riches in the form of oblations, the many kinds of pressed Soma juices, the waters called वसतीवरी (*vasatīvarī*), milk and the like, products of the cow, and the Soma juices (you partake of them).

### Printed p. 203

##### English translation
To you the praises and paryers of the worshipper hasten like a torrent down a declivity ; and you, thunderer, aggregate the immense wealth (of sacrificial offerings), copious libations, and milk, and the juices of the Soma.

#### Ṛk 15

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क ईं॑ स्तव॒त्कः पृ॑णा॒त्क्य य॑जाते॒ यदु॒ग्रमिन्म॒घवा॑ वि॒श्वहावे॑त् ।  
> पादा॑विन प्र॒हर॑न्न॒ न्यम॑न्यं कृ॒णोति॒ पूर्व॒मप॑रं॒ शची॑भिः ॥१५॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कः । ईं । स्तवत् । कः । पृणात् । कः । यजाते । यत् । उग्रं । इत् । मघवा ।  
> विश्वहा । अवेत् ।  
> पादौइव । प्रहरन् । अन्यंअन्यं । कृणोति । पूर्वं । अपरं । शचीभिः ॥ १५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> कः स्तोतेमेनमिंद्रं स्तवत् । स्तुयात् । स्तोतुं शक्नुयात् । को वा
> पृणात् । एनं प्रीणयेत् । कश्च यजाते । एनं हविर्भिर्यजेत । यद्यस्मादयं
> मघवा धनवानिंद्र उग्रमिदुद्दूर्णमेव विश्वहा सर्वदावेत् आत्मानं वेत्ति जानाति
> सर्वदोग्र एव भवति तस्मात्कारणात् न कोऽप्येनं स्तोतुं प्रीणयितुं यष्टुं वा
> शक्कोतीति भावः । इतोऽपि स्तुत्यादेरविषय इत्याह यस्मादयमिंद्रः शचीभिरात्मीयाभिः प्रज्ञाभिः पूर्वं प्रथमभाविनं संतं स्तोतारमपरं पश्चाद्भाविनं
> जान्यमन्यं परस्परव्यतिहारेण कृणोति करोति । यः प्रथमभावी मुख्यः स्तोता
> तं जघन्यं करोति यश्च जघन्यः स्तोता तं मुख्यं करोति । तत्र दृष्टांतः ।
> प्रहरन् पादाविव । यथा पादौ प्रहरन् मार्गे प्रक्षिपन् पुरुषः पूर्वं पादमपरं
> करोश्यपरं च पूर्वं तद्वत् । अतोऽपि कारणादिंद्रः स्तुत्यादेरविषय इति भावः ।
> एतदादिकेन पंचर्चेनेंद्रस्यातिस्वातंत्रं प्रतिपाद्यते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Who, a praiser, could praise this Indra, that is, be able to praise him? Or who could please him? And who could sacrifice to him with oblations? Because this wealthy Indra knows himself always (विश्वहा (*viśvahā*), at all times) as fierce, as lofty (उग्रमित् (*ugramit*)), and is always fierce, for that reason no one is able to praise, to please or to sacrifice to him: this is the sense. He is beyond praise and the like for another reason also: this Indra, by his own powers of mind (शचीभिः (*śacībhiḥ*)), makes the praiser who was first, the one who came before, into the later one, and the later one into the first (जघन्यं (*jaghanyaṃ*) "the last"), each in turn by exchange. The praiser who is first and chief he makes the last, and the praiser who is last he makes the chief. The illustration: as a man walking on the road throws out his two feet (प्रहरन् पादाविव (*praharan pādāviva*)), making the first foot the later one and the later one the first, so is it here. For this reason also Indra is beyond praise and the like. By this group of five verses beginning with this one, Indra's great independence is set forth.

### Printed p. 204

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कः (*kaḥ*)—what praiser
- ईं (*īṃ*)—this Indra
- स्तवत् (*stavat*)—could praise to his satisfaction
- कः (*kaḥ*)—what worshipper
- सृणात् (*sṛṇāt*)—could please him
- कः (*kaḥ*)—what sacrificer
- यजाते (*yajāte*)—could worship him with satisfying oblations
- यत् (*yat*)—because
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—Indra, the wealthy
- उग्रमित् (*ugramit*)—himself as one of exceeding strength
- विश्वहा (*viśvahā*)—always
- अवेत् (*avet*)—has known
- प्रहरन् (*praharan*)—a man travelling on the road
- पादाविव (*pādāviva*)—as he places his feet, one after the other, in turn
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this Indra
- शचीभिः (*śacībhiḥ*)—by his powers of mind
- पूर्वं (*pūrvaṃ*)—first one act
- अपरं (*aparaṃ*)—afterwards
- अन्यं अन्यं (*anyaṃ anyaṃ*)—other and different acts (in turn)
- कृणोति (*kṛṇoti*)—does

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

This Indra always knows himself as one of exceeding strength. What praiser could praise this Indra to his satisfaction? What worshipper could please him? What sacrificer could worship him with satisfying oblations? (All these are in ordinary acts.) As a man travelling on the road places his feet, one after the other, changing them in turn, so this Indra, by his powers of mind, does first one act and afterwards another act, and thus does it in turn. What was behind may be seen to be in front, and the very one that was first appears again at another time as being the first. All this is the greatness of Indra.

##### English translation
Who may (adequately) praise him ? who may satisfy him ? who offer worthy adoration ? since Maghavas is daily conscious of his own terrible (power) : by his acts he makes first one and then the other precede and follow, as (a man) throws out his feet (alternately in walking).

#### Ṛk 16

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> शृ॒ण्वे वी॒र उ॒ग्रमु॑ग्रं दमा॒यन्न॒न्यम॑न्यमतिनेनी॒यमा॑नः ।  
> वि॒ध॒मा॒न॒द्विळु॒भय॑स्य॒ राजा॑ चोषू॒यते॒ विश॒ इंद्रो॑  
> मनु॒ष्या॑न् ॥ १६ ॥

### Printed p. 205

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शृण्वे । वीरः । उग्रंऽउग्रं । दमयन् । अन्यंऽअन्यं । अतिनेनीयमानः ।  
> विधमानद्विट् । उभयस्य । राजा । चोषूयते । विशः । इंद्रः । मनुष्यान् ॥१६॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमिंद्रो वीरो वीर्यवानिति शृण्वे । श्रूयते । किं कुर्वन् ।
> उग्रमुग्रमुद्गूर्णमुद्गूर्णं बलिनं शत्रुं प्रति दमायन् । दमः दमनं ।
> बाधनमिच्छन् । अन्यमन्यं प्रथमं नेतव्यमन्यं पश्चान्नेतव्यं चान्यं पूर्वमपरं
> परस्परव्यतिहारेण स्तोत्थनतिनेनीयमानोऽत्यंतं पुनः पुनर्नयन् । आपि
> चैधमानद्विट् विधमानानां वर्धमानानामसुन्वतां द्वेष्टोभयस्य दिव्यस्य
> पार्थिवस्य च धनस्य राजेश्वरोऽयमिंद्रो विशो निवेशयित्कन् स्वस्य परिचारकान्मनुष्यान् चोषूयते । रक्षणार्थं पुनः पुनराह्वयति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra is heard (शृण्वे (*śṛṇve*), he is heard of) as a hero, one possessed of heroic power. What is he doing? Subduing (दमायन् (*damāyan*); दमः (*damaḥ*) is taming, wishing to oppress) toward each mighty enemy, one who has raised his weapon and is strong. One is to be led first, another later, and another still: first and last, by mutual exchange, he leads his praisers again and again to excess (अतिनेनीयमानः (*atinenīyamānaḥ*)). Moreover, he is the hater of those who prosper (एधमानद्विट् (*edhamānadviṭ*)), of those who press no Soma and are growing, and is the king, the lord, of both kinds of wealth, heavenly and earthly: this Indra, settling the men who are his own attendants (विशः मनुष्यान् (*viśaḥ manuṣyān*)), calls them again and again (चोषूयते (*coṣūyate*)) for protection.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उग्रमुग्रं (*ugramugraṃ*)—even the enemy of exceedingly terrible strength
- दमयन् (*damayan*)—Indra, who is harming him
- अन्यमन्यं (*anyamanyaṃ*)—and placing different praisers
- अतिनेनीयमानः (*atinenīyamānaḥ*)—in turn, behind and in front
- वीरः (*vīraḥ*)—a mighty one
- कृष्णे (*kṛṣṇe*)—is famous **NOTE**
- एधमानद्विट् (*edhamānadviṭ*)—one who hates the wealthy who flourish with arrogance
- उभयस्य (*ubhayasya*)—of both heaven and earth
- राजा (*rājā*)—who is also the lord
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- विशः मनुष्यान् (*viśaḥ manuṣyān*)—his own praising people
- चोष्ययते (*coṣyayate*)—calls again and again for protection

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, who harms even the enemy of exceedingly terrible strength and who places different praisers in turn, behind and in front, is famous as a mighty one. Indra, who hates the wealthy who flourish with arrogance and who is lord of both heaven and earth, calls his own praising people again and again, and gives them encouragement and protection.

##### English translation
The hero Indra is renowned ; humiliating every formidable (foe), and repeatedly changing the place of one (worshiper) with that of another ; Indra, the enemy of the arrogant, the sovereign of both (heaven and earth), calls again and again (to encourage) the men who are his worshippers.

### Printed p. 206

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Explaining the word चोषूयते (*coṣūyate*) in this Ṛk, Yāska gives the explanation of the second half of this Ṛk: चोषूयमाणः । चोषूयते । चोषूयमाण इंद्र भूरि वामं । दददिंद्र बहु वननीयं विधमानद्विळुभयस्य राजा चोषूयते विश इंद्रो मनुष्यान् ॥ व्युदस्यत्येधमानानहर्द्वेष्ट्यसुन्वतः सुन्वतोS भ्यादधात्युभयस्य राजा दिव्यस्य च पार्थिवस्य च चोषूयमाण इति । चोषूयतेश्चर्करीतवृत्तं ॥ (*coṣūyamāṇaḥ | coṣūyate | coṣūyamāṇa iṃdra bhūri vāmaṃ | dadadiṃdra bahu vananīyaṃ vidhamānadviḷubhayasya rājā coṣūyate viśa iṃdro manuṣyān ‖ vyudasyatyedhamānānahardveṣṭyasunvataḥ sunvatoS bhyādadhātyubhayasya rājā divyasya ca pārthivasya ca coṣūyamāṇa iti | coṣūyateścarkarītavṛttaṃ ‖*) (Ni. 6-22). The two words चोषूयमाणः (*coṣūyamāṇaḥ*) and चोषूयते (*coṣūyate*) are synonyms; that is, both words have one and the same meaning. The meaning of the Vedic sentence चोषूयमाण इंद्र भूरि वामं (*coṣūyamāṇa iṃdra bhūri vāmaṃ*) is दददिंद्र बहु वननीयं (*dadadiṃdra bahu vananīyaṃ*): Indra gives wealth that is much desired. Indra strikes down (scatters) and drives away the wicked people who do not press Soma, that is, who do not perform sacrifice, and who flourish with arrogance. He invites for protection the good people who press Soma and perform sacrifice; that is, he protects them. The main purport of the Nirukta is that Indra is king (lord) of both worlds, the world of heaven (svarga) and the earth.

#### Ṛk 17

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> परा॒ पूर्वे॑षां स॒ख्या वृ॑णक्ति वि॒तर्तु॑राणो॒ अप॑रेभिरेति ।  
> अना॑नुभूतीरवधून्वा॒नः पू॒र्वीरिंद्रः॑ श॒रद॑स्तर्तरीति ॥ १७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> परा । पूर्वेषां । सख्या । वृणक्ति । वृतर्तुराणः । अपरेभिः । एति ।  
> अननुभूतीः । अवधून्वानः । पूर्वीः । इंद्रः । शरदः । तर्तरीति ॥ १७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमिंद्रः पूर्वेषां पूर्वभाविनां प्रशस्तानां कर्मणां कुर्वतां
> पुरुषाणां सख्या सखित्वानि परा वृणक्ति । वर्जयति । अपि च वितर्तुराणस्तान्
> हिंसन् अपरेभिरपरैरमुख्यैरप्रशस्त्रैरपि कर्मानुष्ठातृभिरेति । सख्यं प्राप्नोति ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra turns away (परा वृणक्ति (*parā vṛṇakti*), he avoids) the friendships of the former men, those who came before and performed praised acts. Moreover, वितर्तुराणस्तान् (*vitarturāṇastān*), harming them, he goes (एति (*eti*)) with the others (अपरेभिः (*aparebhiḥ*)), the not-chief, not-praised performers of rites: he enters into friendship.

### Printed p. 207

> तथानानुभूतीरपरिचरणाः प्रजा अवधून्वानः परित्यजन् पूर्वीर्बह्वीः शरदः
> संवत्सरानयमिंद्रस्तर्तरीति । भृशमतिक्रामति । स्तोतृभिः सह सख्यं कुर्वन्
> चिरकालं वसतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Likewise, अनानुभूतीः (*anānubhūtīḥ*): he shakes off and abandons the people who do not serve him; for many years (शरदः (*śaradaḥ*)), that is, for many years, this Indra तर्तरीति (*tartarīti*), greatly passes beyond. The sense is that, making friendship with his praisers, he dwells with them for a long time.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- पूर्वेषां (*pūrveṣāṃ*)—of men who first performed excellent rites
- सख्या (*sakhyā*)—the friendship
- परा वृणक्ति (*parā vṛṇakti*)—abandons
- वितर्तुराणः (*vitarturāṇaḥ*)—harming them
- अपरेभिः (*aparebhiḥ*)—with those inferior to them
- एति (*eti*)—attains friendship
- अनानुभूतीः (*anānubhūtīḥ*)—those who do not worship him
- अवधून्वानः (*avadhūnvānaḥ*)—forsaking
- पूर्वीः (*pūrvīḥ*)—many
- शरदः (*śaradaḥ*)—years
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- तर्तरीति (*tartarīti*)—passes the time (with his praisers)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra abandons the friendship of the men who first performed excellent rites. Afterwards, harming them, he builds friendship with those who are inferior to them. Again, forsaking those who do not worship him, Indra passes many years with his praisers.

##### English translation
Indra rejects the friendship of those who are foremost (in pious acts), and, despoiling them, associates with (their) inferiors : or (again) shaking off those who neglect his worship, Indra abides many years with those who serve him.
