> इंद्रः । मायाभिः । पुरुरूपः । ईयते । युक्ताः । हि । अस्य । हरयः । शता ।  
> दश ॥ १८ ॥

### Printed p. 208

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमिंद्रः प्रतिरूपो रूपाणां प्रतिनिधिः सन् रूपं रूपं तत्तदग्न्यादिदेवतास्वरूपं बभूव । प्राप्नोति ॥ भू प्राप्ताविति धातुः ॥ इंद्रः स्वमाहात्म्येन
> तत्तद्देवतारूपो भवतीत्यर्थः । अस्य चेंद्रस्य तत्पाप्तमग्न्यादिदेवतास्वरूपं
> प्रतिचक्षणाय प्रतिनियतदर्शनायायमग्निरयं विष्णुरयं रुद्र इत्येवमसंकीर्णदर्शनाय भवति । अपि चायमिंद्रो मायाभिः ॥ ज्ञाननामैतत् ॥ ज्ञाननैरात्मीयैः
> संकल्पैः पुरुरूपो बहुविधशरीरः सन् ईयते । बहून्य जमानान् गच्छति । ननु
> द्वावेवास्याश्वानेकश्च रथः कथमनेन युगपद्वहून् गच्छतीत्यत आह । अस्येंद्रस्य
> हरयोऽश्वा युक्ता रथे योजिताः शता दश सहस्रसंख्याका अपरिमिताः संति । हि
> यस्मादेवं तस्माद्बहुशरीराणि स्वीकृत्य युगपद्धविष्मतो यजमानान् गच्छतीत्यर्थः ॥ अन्ये मन्यंते । इदि परमैश्वर्य इत्यस्य धातोरर्थानुगमादिंद्रः
> परमात्मा । स चाकाशवत्सर्वगतः सदानंदरूपः । स एवोपाधिभिरंतःकरणैः
> प्रतिशरीरमवच्चिन्नः सन् जीवात्मति व्यपदिश्यते । स एवानादिमायाशक्तिभिर्वियदादिजगदात्मना विवर्तते शब्दादिविषयहरणशीला इंद्रियवृत्तयश्च तेनैव संबद्धाः ।
> एतत्सर्वं तस्य परमात्मनो यद्वास्तवं रूपं तस्य दर्शनायेति । आयमर्थोऽनया
> प्रतिपाद्यते । रूपं रूपं । रूप्यत इति रूपं शरीरादि । प्रतिशरीरं चिद्रूपः
> सर्वगतः परमात्मा प्रतिरूपः प्रतिबिंबरूपः सन् सर्वाणि शरीराणि बभूव ।
> प्राप्नोत् । तच्च प्राप्तं प्रतिबिंबरूपमस्य परमात्मनः प्रतिचक्षणाय प्रतिनियता
> कारस्य दर्शनाय भवति । स चेंद्रः परमेश्वरो मायाभिर्मायाशक्तिभिः पुरुरूपो
> नियादादिभिर्बहुविधरूपैरुपेतः सन् ईयते । चेष्टते । एतदप्यस्य परमात्मनः
> प्रतिचक्षणाय भवति । अस्य च दश शता सहस्रसंख्याका हरयॆ इंद्रियवृत्तय
> युक्ता विषयग्रहणायोद्युक्ताः संति । तदप्यस्य वास्तवरूपस्य दर्शनायॆ भवतीति ।
> एवं स्कूलसूक्ष्मशरीरयोर्वियदादिमहाप्रपंचस्य च तत्त्वज्ञानहेतुत्वमनया
> प्रत्यपादीति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra, being the counterpart (प्रतिरूपो (*pratirūpo*)) of forms, a representative of forms, becomes each and every form, the form of each deity such as Agni; that is, he attains it, the root भू (*bhū*) being in the sense of attaining. The meaning is that by his own greatness Indra becomes the form of each deity. That form of Agni and the other deities which he has attained is for प्रतिचक्षणाय (*praticakṣaṇāya*), for distinct seeing, so that each is seen without confusion: "this is Agni, this is Viṣṇu, this is Rudra". Moreover, this Indra by his मायाभिः (*māyābhiḥ*) (this is a name for knowledge), that is, by his own resolves, having many bodies (पुरुरूपो (*pururūpo*)), goes (ईयते (*īyate*)) to many sacrificers. Objection: he has only two horses and one chariot; how can he go to many at the same time? Therefore Sāyaṇa says: his horses, the bay steeds, are yoked to the chariot, ten hundreds, a thousand in number, countless; since this is so, taking many bodies he goes at the same moment to the sacrificers who have oblations. Others hold: from the meaning of the root इदि (*idi*), supreme lordship, Indra is the Supreme Self (Paramātman). He is all-pervading like space and of the nature of bliss. He alone, limited in each body by the inner organs as limiting adjuncts, is called the individual self. He alone, by his beginningless powers of māyā, evolves as the world, ether and the rest; and the activities of the senses, which are apt to seize objects such as sound, are bound to him. All this is for the seeing of the real form of that Supreme Self; the verse teaches this meaning. रूपं रूपं (*rūpaṃ rūpaṃ*) is anything that is formed, a body and the like. In each body the Supreme Self, all-pervading, of the form of consciousness, being a counterpart, a reflection, became all bodies, that is, entered them. That reflected form is for the seeing of the definite form of this Supreme Self. And that Indra, the Supreme Lord, by his māyā powers, being furnished with many kinds of forms (**NOTE**), acts (ईयते (*īyate*), he exerts himself). This too is for the seeing of this Supreme Self. And his ten hundred bays are the activities of the senses, yoked, that is, eager to grasp their objects; this too is for the seeing of his real form. Thus by this verse he teaches that the gross and subtle bodies and the great expanse of the world, ether and the rest, are the cause of knowledge of the truth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- प्रतिरूपः (*pratirūpaḥ*)—being himself the counterpart of all the many forms such as Agni
- रूपं रूपं (*rūpaṃ rūpaṃ*)—having taken the many different forms such as Agni
- बभूव (*babhūva*)—has undergone change
- अस्य (*asya*)—for Indra
- तत् रूपं (*tat rūpaṃ*)—that particular form such as Agni
- प्रतिभक्षणाय (*pratibhakṣaṇāya*)—for being visible to the world in the many forms such as Agni (is the means)
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- मायाभिः (*māyābhiḥ*)—by his endless powers
- पुरुरूपः (*pururūpaḥ*)—having taken many forms such as Agni, Viṣṇu and Rudra

### Printed p. 209

- ईयते (*īyate*)—goes to the sacrificer
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Indra
- हरयः (*harayaḥ*)—the horses
- शता दश (*śatā daśa*)—numbering a thousand
- युक्ता हि (*yuktā hi*)—are they not yoked to the chariot?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra takes the many different forms such as Agni according to his wish. For this Indra that particular form such as Agni is the means of being visible to the whole world in the many forms such as Agni. Indra, by his endless powers, takes the many forms such as Agni, Viṣṇu and Rudra, and goes to the sacrificer. The horses of this Indra, numbering a thousand, are yoked to the chariot, are they not?

##### English translation
Indra, the prototype, has assumed various forms, and such is his form as that which (he adopts) for his manifestation : Indra, maltiform by his illusions, proceeds (to his many worshippers), for the horses yoked to his car are a thousand.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk Indra's greatness and his many kinds of power and capacity are described. The commentator has given two kinds of meaning for this Ṛk: one meaning describing Indra's surpassing power, and another explanation setting out the spiritual meaning that Indra himself is the Supreme Brahman. We shall explain the two separately.

(1) The meaning that explains Indra's power and capacities.

अयमिंद्रः प्रतिरूपः रूपंरूपं बभूव (*ayamiṃdraḥ pratirūpaḥ rūpaṃrūpaṃ babhūva*) — इंद्रः रूपाणां प्रतिनिधिः सन् तत्तवग्न्यादिदेवतास्वरूपं प्राप्नोति । (*iṃdraḥ rūpāṇāṃ pratinidhiḥ san tattavagnyādidevatāsvarūpaṃ prāpnoti |*). By his power and capacities Indra can take many forms. Taking also the forms of other deities such as Agni and Vāyu, he can perform the functions of those deities. Not only that: the main purport is that the deities such as Agni are parts of Indra, or other forms of him.

अस्य तत् रूपं प्रतिचक्षणाय (*asya tat rūpaṃ praticakṣaṇāya*) — Indra takes many kinds of form in order to make the many deities visible to people: "this is Agni, this is Vāyu", and so on.

इंद्रः मायाभिः पुरुरूपः ईयते (*iṃdraḥ māyābhiḥ pururūpaḥ īyate*) — Indra is able to take many forms by his own knowledges, which are of the nature of resolve. Why must he take many forms? What is the need? The answer is that many sacrificers on earth perform sacrifices and the like at one time, that is, on one and the same day, and invite Indra. For Indra to go to all those sacrifices he takes many forms (the forms of many Indras) and goes to the sacrificial ground of each sacrificer. For that very reason

### Printed p. 210

it is said that Indra takes many forms. Then, since Indra has one chariot and only two horses, the pair called Hari, how is it possible to go to many places at one time in a single chariot? The answer:

अस्य हरयः युक्ताः शता दश (*asya harayaḥ yuktāḥ śatā daśa*) — Indra's horses also have great capacity. Since they are changed into thousands in number and yoked to the chariot, the main purport is that the many forms of Indra, seated in many chariots, can go at one time to all places.

(2) Explanation of the spiritual meaning.

In this meaning, Indra himself is the Supreme Self. He is all-pervading like space and ever of the nature of bliss. That Supreme Self, through the limiting adjuncts called the inner organs, is spoken of by the name of individual selves in many bodies. Besides, by his beginningless power of māyā he is manifest also in the form of the world, beginning with space. All these are the many forms of that Indra, the Supreme Self. Here the word counterpart means a form that is a reflection: that is, reflecting his own form of consciousness in the many bodies of individual selves, he is the inner self in all living beings. The Vedic sentence अंतः प्रविष्टः शास्ता जनानाग्ं सर्वात्मा (*aṃtaḥ praviṣṭaḥ śāstā janānāgṃ sarvātmā*) (Tai. Ā. 3-11-2) **GLOSS** also supports this. Such a Supreme Self in the form of Indra, हरयः शता दश युक्ता ईयते-सहस्रसंख्याका इंद्रियप्रवृत्तिभिर्युक्ता सर्वप्राणिसु प्रविष्टः सन् लोकव्यवहाररूपेण चेष्टते । (*harayaḥ śatā daśa yuktā īyate-sahasrasaṃkhyākā iṃdriyapravṛttibhiryuktā sarvaprāṇisu praviṣṭaḥ san lokavyavahārarūpeṇa ceṣṭate |*): as the inner self in all living beings, through thousands of kinds of activities of the senses, he is engaged in the affairs of the world. This is the main purport of the spiritual meaning.

#### Ṛk 19

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यु॒जा॒नो ह॒रिता॒ रथे॒ भूरि॒ त्वष्टे॒ह रा॑जति ।  
> को वि॒श्वाहा॑ द्विष॒तः पक्ष॑ आसत उ॒तासी॑नेषु सू॒रिषु॑ ॥ १९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युजानः । हरिता । रथे । भूरि । त्वष्टा । इह । राजति ।  
> कः । विश्वाहा । द्विषतः । पक्षः । आसते । उत । आसिनेषु । सूरिषु ॥ १९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हरिता हरितावश्चौ रथे युजानो युंजन् त्वष्टा दीप्त इंद्र इह त्रैलोक्ये
> भूरि भूरिषु बहुषु प्रदेशेषु राजति । दीप्यते ! उतापि च सूरिषु स्तोतृस्मादृशेष्वासिनेषु सत्सु विश्वासा सर्वदेंद्रादन्यः को नाम द्विषतः शत्रोः पक्षः

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, bright, yoking the two bay horses to the chariot, shines here in the three worlds, in many places, in a great many regions. And while the praisers, such as ourselves, are seated, who other than Indra, at all times, would be on the side of the enemy, as a पाचको बाधकः (*pācako bādhakaḥ*)

### Printed p. 211

> पाचको बाधकः सन् आसते । आस्ते । इंद्र एव सर्वदा शत्रुन्बाधत इत्यर्थः ।
> यद्वा । पक्ष इति सकारांतं समीपवचनं । इंद्रादन्यः को नाम द्विषतः शत्रुः
> समीप आसते । आस्ते । इंद्र एव निर्भयः सन् शत्रुसमीपे स्थातुं शक्नोतीति भावः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

...a tormentor sits there. The meaning is that Indra alone at all times obstructs the enemies. Or else: the word पक्ष (*pakṣa*) ending in स (*sa*) is a word for "near"; what enemy other than Indra would sit near an enemy? The sense is that Indra alone, fearless, is able to stay near enemies.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हरिता (*haritā*)—his own green-hued horses
- रथे (*rathe*)—with the chariot
- युजानः (*yujānaḥ*)—having joined
- त्वष्टा (*tvaṣṭā*)—Indra, the shining
- इह (*iha*)—in these three worlds
- भूरि (*bhūri*)—exceedingly
- राजति (*rājati*)—shines
- उत (*uta*)—and
- विश्वाहा (*viśvāhā*)—on every day
- सूरिषु (*sūriṣu*)—among the praisers
- आसीनेषु (*āsīneṣu*)—among the gods who are seated
- कः (*kaḥ*)—who else (than Indra)
- द्विषतः (*dviṣataḥ*)—to the enemy
- पक्षः (*pakṣaḥ*)—as one who harms
- आसते (*āsate*)—could be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Having joined his green-hued horses to the chariot, Indra, the shining, shines exceedingly in these three worlds. And on every day, among the gods who are seated amid the praisers, who else than Indra could be a harmer to the enemy?

##### English translation
Yoking his horses to his car, Twashtri shines in many places here in the three worlds: who (else), sojourning daily amongst his present worshippers, is their protector against adversaries ?

#### Ṛk 20

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒ग॒व्यू॒ति क्षेत्र॒माग॑न्न देवा उ॒र्वी स॒ती भूमि॑रंहूर॒णाभू॑त् ।  
> बृह॑स्पते॒ प्र चि॑कित्स्या॒ गवि॑ष्टावि॒त्था स॒ते ज॑रि॒त्र इं॑द्र॒ पंथां॑॥२०॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अगव्यूति । क्षेत्रं । आ । अगन्न । देवाः । उर्वी । सती । भूमिः । अंहूरणा । अभूत् ।  
> बृहस्पते । प्र । चिकित्स्या । गोइष्टौ । इत्था । सते । जरित्रे । इंद्र । पंथां ॥ २० ।

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अत्रोक्तं । अरण्ये निर्जने गर्गो देवान् भूमिं बृहस्पतिं । इंद्रं
> चास्तौत्स्वरक्षार्थमृचा मार्गच्युतोऽनयेति ॥ आगव्यूतीत्यत्र देवान् पादे

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

On this verse it is said: Garga, who had lost his way in a desolate, uninhabited forest, praised with this verse the gods, the earth, Bṛhaspati and Indra, for his own protection. And further: in आगव्यूतीत्यत्र (*āgavyūtītyatra*) he praises the gods in the pāda

### Printed p. 212

> भूमिमथोत्तरे । बृहस्पतिं तृतीये तु इंद्र एवोत्तमे स्तुत इति च ॥ हे देवा
> अगव्यूत्यगोचरं गोसंचाररहितं निर्जनं क्षेत्रं देशमागन्म । आगताः स्मः । अतो
> मां रक्षतेति शेषः । उर्वि विस्तीर्णा सती भूमिश्चांहूरणा । अंहव आहंतारो
> दस्यवः । तेषां रमणा रमयित्र्यभूत् । भवति । अस्मानतः सापि रक्षतु । हे बृहस्पते
> त्वं गविष्टौ गवामन्वेषण उपाये प्र चिकित्स । प्रवेदय । इत्थेत्ममनेन प्रकारेण
> सते भवते दुःखमनुभवते जरित्रे स्तोत्रे मह्यं हे इंद्र पंथां पंथानं मार्गं
> प्र चिकित्स । प्रज्ञापय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

...the earth in the next one, Bṛhaspati in the third, and in the last Indra alone is praised. O gods, we have come to a region that is अगव्यूति (*agavyūti*), without the roaming of cattle, uninhabited; therefore protect me: this is to be supplied. And the earth, although vast, has become अंहूरणा (*aṃhūraṇā*): the अंहव (*aṃhava*) are the striking robbers, and she has become the one who gives them delight. May she too protect us. O Bṛhaspati, in the search for cows (गविष्टौ (*gaviṣṭau*)), make known the means. Thus, to me, being so, who suffer misery, a praiser, O Indra, make known the path, cause me to know it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—O gods
- अगव्यूति (*agavyūti*)—to the region that is uninhabited and without cattle
- क्षेत्रं (*kṣetraṃ*)—to the region
- आगन्म (*āganma*)—we have come
- भूमिः (*bhūmiḥ*)—the earth
- उर्वी सती (*urvī satī*)—being exceedingly vast
- अंहूरणा (*aṃhūraṇā*)—a place of roaming for all the destroying enemies
- अभूत् (*abhūt*)—has become
- बृहस्पते (*bṛhaspate*)—O Bṛhaspati
- गविष्टा (*gaviṣṭā*)—the means of searching for cows
- प्र चिकित्स (*pra cikitsa*)—teach us
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- इत्या (*ityā*)—in the manner described above
- सते (*sate*)—who am suffering misery
- जरित्रॆ (*jaritrè*)—to me, the praiser
- पंथां (*paṃthāṃ*)—a path that brings happiness (show)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, we have come wandering into a region that is without cattle and uninhabited. This region is exceedingly vast and is a place of roaming for all the enemies who would harm us. O Bṛhaspati, teach us the means of searching for cows. O Indra, in the manner described above, show a path that brings happiness to me, the praiser, who am suffering misery.

##### English translation
We have wandered, gods, into a desert where there is no track of cattle : the vast extant earth has become the protectress of murderers : direct us, Brihaspati, in our search for cattle : show the path, Indra, to your votary being thus astray.

#### Ṛk 21

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दि॒वेदि॑वे स॒दृशी॑र॒न्यमर्धं॑ कृ॒ष्णा अ॑सेध॒दप॒ सद्म॑नो॒ जाः ।  
> अह॑न्दा॒सा वृ॑ष॒भो व॑स्न॒यंतो॒दव्र॑जे व॒र्चिनं॒ शंब॑रं च ॥

### Printed p. 213

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दिवेऽदिवे । सृदृशीः । अन्यं । अर्धं । कृष्णाः । असेधत् । अप । सद्मनः । जाः ।  
> अहन् । दासा । वृषभः । वस्ययंता । उदव्रजे । वर्चिनं । शंबरं । च ॥ २१ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमिंद्रः सद्मनः सदनात्केयादंतरिक्षैकदेशात्सा नाज्ञा जायमानः
> सूर्यात्मना प्रादुर्भवन् सदृशीस्तुल्यरूपाः कृष्णाः कृष्णवर्णा रात्रीर्दिने दिने
> प्रतिदिवसमपासेधत् । निवर्तयति । किमर्थं । अन्यमर्धमहरर्धमपरं भागं
> प्रकाशयितुं । अहोरात्रात्मकस्य दिवसस्य हि रात्रिरेको भागः । अपि च वृषभो
> वर्षयिता स इंद्रो दासा दासावुपक्षपयितार् यज्ञकर्मणां वस्नयंता । वस्नं
> निवासहेतुभूतं धनं । तदिच्छंत् वर्चिनं शंबरं चैतत्संज्ञावसुरावुदव्रजे ।
> उदकानि व्रजंत्यस्मिन्नित्युदव्रजो देशविशेषः । तस्मिन्नहन् । हतवान् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, coming into being from his abode, a part of the atmosphere (**NOTE**), manifesting himself in the form of the sun, drives away every day the black nights, which are alike in appearance. For what purpose? To light up the other half, the day-half; for night is one part of the day in the sense of day-and-night together. Moreover, that showerer, Indra, slew in Udavraja the two slaves, Varcin and Śambara, the two destroyers of sacrificial rites, who desired vasna, that is, wealth which is the cause of dwelling. Udavraja is a particular region: waters go (vraj) in it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- सद्मनः (*sadmanaḥ*)—from his own abode, the atmosphere
- जाः (*jāḥ*)—manifesting himself in the form of the sun
- सदृशीः (*sadṛśīḥ*)—appearing in one and the same form
- कृष्णाः (*kṛṣṇāḥ*)—the nights, filled with pitch darkness, all of them
- दिवे दिवे (*dive dive*)—every day
- अन्यं अर्धं (*anyaṃ ardhaṃ*)—in order to light up the other half, the day
- अप असेधत् (*apa asedhat*)—makes them vanish
- वृषभः (*vṛṣabhaḥ*)—Indra, the cause of rain
- दासा (*dāsā*)—those who do us harm
- वस्ययंता (*vasyayaṃtā*)—who covet our wealth, which is the means of dwelling
- वर्चिनं (*varcinaṃ*)—the asura named Varcin
- शंबरं च (*śaṃbaraṃ ca*)—and the asura named Śambara
- उदव्रजे (*udavraje*)—in the region named Udavraja
- अहन् (*ahan*)—slew

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, manifesting himself in the form of the sun from his own abode, the atmosphere, makes all the nights, which are alike in form and filled with pitch darkness, vanish every day, in order to light up the other half, the day. Indra, the cause of rain, slew in the region named Udavraja the asura named Varcin and the asura named Śambara, who did us harm and coveted our wealth, the means of our dwelling.

##### English translation
Indra, becoming manifest from his abode (in the firmament), dissipates, day by day, the resembling glooms, (so that he may distinguish)
### Printed p. 214
the other portion, (or the day) : and the showerer has slain the two wealth-seeking slaves, Varchin and Sambara, in (the couutry of) Udavraja.
#### Ṛk 22

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र॒स्तो॒क इन्नु राध॑सस्त इंद्र॒ दश॒ कोश॑या॒र्दश॑ वा॒जिनो॑ऽदात् ।  
> दिवो॑दासादतिथि॒ग्वस्य॒ राधः॑ शांब॒रं वसु॒ प्रत्य॑ग्रभीष्म ॥ २२ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रस्तोकः । इत् । नु । राधसः । ते । इंद्र । दश । कोशयीः । दश । वाजिनः । अदात् ।  
> दिवोदासात् । अतिथिग्वस्य । राधः । शांबरं । वसु । प्रति । अग्रभीष्म ॥ २२ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एतदाद्यासु चतसृषु सृंजयपुत्रस्य प्रस्तोकस्य राज्ञो दानस्तुतिः । स एव
> दिवोदासोऽश्वथोऽतिथिग्व इति चाख्यायते । हे इंद्र ते, तव राधस आराधयितुः
> स्तोतुः ॥ चतुर्थ्यर्थे षष्ठी ॥ त्वां स्तुवते मह्यं गर्गाय प्रस्तोको राजा दश
> कोशयाः सुवर्णपूर्णान्दशसंख्याकान् कोशान्दश वाजिनोऽश्वांश्च नु क्षिप्रमदात् ।
> प्रायच्छत् । इदिति पूरणः । वयं च तस्माद्दिवोदासादेशत्संज्ञातृस्तोकाद्वसु
> धनं प्रत्यग्रभीष्म । प्रतिगृहीतवंतः । कीदृशं । अतिथिग्वस्य तस्यैव प्रस्तोकस्य
> स्वभूतं राधो राधकं शांबरं शंबरादसुरादागतं । शंबरं हत्वा त्वया दत्तं
> तदीयमित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Beginning with this verse, four verses form a praise of the gift of King Prastoka, son of Sṛñjaya; he is also called Divodāsa, Aśvatha and Atithigva. "O Indra, to the one who worships you", that is, to the praiser (the genitive is used in the sense of the dative): to me, Garga, who praise you, King Prastoka quickly gave ten purses filled with gold, and ten horses. The word "it" is to be supplied as a filler. And we received wealth from that Divodāsa, that is, from Prastoka (**NOTE**). Of what kind? It is wealth belonging to that same Prastoka, called Atithigva, bringing success, and coming from the asura Śambara: that is, it is his wealth, given by you after you had slain Śambara.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- ते (*te*)—your
- राधसः (*rādhasaḥ*)—to Garga, the worshipper
- प्रस्तोकः (*prastokaḥ*)—the king named Prastoka
- दश कोशयीः (*daśa kośayīḥ*)—ten treasure-purses filled with gold
- दश वाजिनः (*daśa vājinaḥ*)—ten horses
- नु (*nu*)—at once
- अदात् (इत्) (*adāt (it)*)—gave
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we also
- दिवोदासात् (*divodāsāt*)—from that Prastoka, who has the other name Divodāsa
- शांबरं (*śāṃbaraṃ*)—taken away from the asura Śambara
- अतिथिग्वस्य (*atithigvasya*)—belonging to that same Prastoka, who also has the name Atithigva
- राधः (*rādhaḥ*)—and worthy of honour
- वसु (*vasu*)—wealth
- प्रत्यग्रभीष्ट (*pratyagrabhīṣṭa*)—we have accepted
### Printed p. 215

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, King Prastoka at once gave to Garga, your worshipper, ten treasure-purses filled with gold and ten horses. We also have received from that Prastoka, who has the other name Divodāsa, wealth that was taken away from the asura Śambara, that belongs to that same Prastoka who has another name, Atithigva, and that is worthy of honour.

##### English translation
Prastoka has given to your worshipper, Indra, ten purses of gold, and ten horses, and we have accepted this treasure from Divodasa, the spoil won by Atithigwan from Shambara.
#### Ṛk 23

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दशाश्वा॒न्दश॒ कोशा॒न्दश॒ वस्त्राधि॑भोजना ।  
> दशो॑ हिरण्यपिं॒डान्दिवो॑दासादसानिषं ॥ २३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दश । अश्वान् । दश । कोशान् । दश । वस्त्रा । अधिभोजना ।  
> दशो इति । हिरण्यपिंडान् । दिवःसदासात् । असानिषं ॥ २३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दश दशसंख्याकानश्वान् दशसंख्याकान् हिरण्यपूर्णान् कोशानधिभोजना ।
> भोजनमिति धननाम । अधिकं धनं येषां मूल्यं । तादृशानि दशसंख्यानि
> वस्त्राणि च दशो दश च दशसंख्याकांश्च हिरण्यपिंडान् सुवर्णपिंडानेतान् सर्वान्निवोदासात्पस्तोकादहमसानिषं । समभजं । लब्धवानस्मि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Ten horses, and ten treasure-purses filled with gold; "adhibhojanā": bhojana is a name for wealth, so these are ten cloths of such a kind that their price is more than ordinary wealth; and "daśo", that is, ten, lumps of gold. All these, ten of each, I received from Divodāsa, that is, from Prastoka: "asāniṣam" means I shared in them, I obtained them.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- दिवोदासात् (*divodāsāt*)—from Prastoka, who has the other name Divodāsa
- दश (*daśa*)—ten in number
- अश्वान् (*aśvān*)—horses
- दश कोशान् (*daśa kośān*)—and ten treasure-purses
- अधि भोजना (*adhi bhojanā*)—and riches that bring enjoyment
- दश वस्त्रा (*daśa vastrā*)—ten cloths
- दशो हिरण्यपिंडान् (*daśo hiraṇyapiṃḍān*)—and ten pieces of gold
- असानिषं (*asāniṣaṃ*)—I obtained
### Printed p. 216

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

From Prastoka, who has the other name Divodāsa, I obtained ten treasure-purses, ten riches that bring enjoyment, ten cloths and ten lumps of gold.

##### English translation
I have received ten horses, ten purses, cloths and ample food, and ten lumps of gold from Divodasa.
#### Ṛk 24

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दश॒ रथा॒न्प्रष्टि॑मतः श॒तं गा अथ॑र्वभ्यः ।  
> अ॒श्व॒थः पा॒यवे॑दात् ॥ २४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दश । रथान् । प्रष्टिमतः । शतं । गाः । अथर्वभ्यः ।  
> अश्वथः । पायने । अदात् ॥ २४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दश दशसंख्याकान् प्रष्टिमतः । प्रष्टिस्त्रिपद आधारः । तद्वद्वहंतीति प्रष्टयोऽश्वाः । तद्युक्तान्रथान् शतं शतसंख्याका गाश्चाथर्वभ्योऽथर्वगोत्रेभ्य
> यषिभ्यः पायवे भरद्वाजपुत्रायैतत्संज्ञायास्मद्भात्रे चाश्वथोऽश्ववानेतत्वंज्ञः
> प्रस्तोकोऽदात् । दत्तवान् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Ten chariots with prṣṭi horses. A prṣṭi is a three-footed support; the horses that carry like it are prṣṭis. So: chariots yoked with such horses, and a hundred cows, were given by Aśvatha, that is, by Prastoka, the owner of horses, to the Atharvans, the sages of the Atharva lineage, and to Pāyu, the son of Bharadvāja, our brother.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्वथः (*aśvathaḥ*)—that Prastoka, who has the other name Aśvatha
- अथर्वभ्यः (*atharvabhyaḥ*)—to the sages born in the Atharva lineage
- हायपे (*hāyape*)—and to Pāyu, son of Bharadvāja **NOTE**
- दश (*daśa*)—ten in number
- प्रष्टिमतः (*praṣṭimataḥ*)—and provided with horses
- रथान् (*rathān*)—chariots
- शतं (*śataṃ*)—a hundred in number
- गाः (*gāḥ*)—cows
- अदात् (*adāt*)—gave

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

That same Prastoka, who has the other name Aśvatha, gave to the sages born in the Atharva lineage and to Pāyu, son of Bharadvāja, ten chariots provided with horses and a hundred cows.
### Printed p. 217

##### English translation
Ashwatha has given to Payu ten chariots with their horses, and a hundred cows to the priests
#### Ṛk 25

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> महि॒ राधो॑ वि॒श्वज॑न्यं॒ दधा॑नान्भ॒रद्वा॑जान्त्सार्ङ्ज॒यो अ॒भ्य॑यष्ट ॥ २५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> महि । राधः । विश्वजन्यं । दधानान् । भरत्वाजान् । साङ्ज्जयः । अभि । आयष्ट ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> विश्वजन्यं सर्वजनहितं महि महाद्राधो धनं दधानान्यारयतो भरद्वाजान्
> भरद्वाजपुत्रानस्मान् साञ्जयः सृंजयपुत्रः प्रस्तोकोऽभ्ययष्ट । अपूजयत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Prastoka, the son of Sṛñjaya, honoured us, the sons of Bharadvāja, who receive (**NOTE**) great wealth, which is beneficial to all people.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- विश्वजन्यं (*viśvajanyaṃ*)—beneficial to all people
- महि (*mahi*)—and great
- राधः (*rādhaḥ*)—wealth
- दधानान् (*dadhānān*)—who accept
- भरद्वाजान् (*bharadvājān*)—us, the sons of Bharadvāja
- सांर्जयः (*sāṃrjayaḥ*)—Prastoka, son of Sṛñjaya
- अभ्ययष्ट (*abhyayaṣṭa*)—has honoured

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Prastoka, the son of Sṛñjaya, has honoured us, the sons of Bharadvāja, who accept great wealth that is beneficial to all people.

##### English translation
The son of Srinjaya has reverenced the Bharadwajas who have accepted such great wealth for the good of all men.
#### Ṛk 26

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वन॑स्पते वी॒ड्वं॑गो॒ हि भू॒या अ॒स्मत्स॑खा प्र॒तर॑णः सु॒वीरः॑ ।  
> गोभिः॒ संन॑द्दो असि वी॒ळय॑स्वास्था॒ता ते॑ जयतु॒ जेत्वा॑नि ॥

### Printed p. 218

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वनस्पते । वीळु अंगः । हि । भूयाः । अस्मत् सखा । प्रतरणः । सुवीरः ।  
> गोभिः । संनद्धः । असि । वीळयस्व । आस्थाता । ते । जयतु । जेत्वानि ॥ २६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वनस्पत इत्येषा रथारोहणे विनियुक्ता । सूत्रितं च । वनस्पते वीड्वंगो हि
> भूया इत्येतयान्यान्यपि वानस्पत्यानि । आ. गृ. २-६-५ । इति ॥
> हे वनस्पते वनस्पतिविकार रथ त्वं वीड्वंगो दृढावयवो भूयाः । भव ।
> अस्मत्सखा वयं सखायो यस्य स तादृशश्च भव । प्रतरणः प्रवर्धयिता सुवीरः
> शोभनैर्विरैः शूरभटैः पुत्रादिभिर्वा युक्तश्च भव । अपि च त्वं गोभिर्गॊविकारैश्चर्मभिः संन्नद्योऽसि । सम्यग्बद्दो भवसि । तादृशस्त्वं वीळयस्त । आस्मान्दृढीकुरु । ते तवास्थाता त्वय्यवस्थितो रथी च जेत्वानि जेतव्यानि शत्रुसैन्यानि जयतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This verse, "Vanaspate", is applied in mounting the chariot. It is stated in the sūtra: "With 'Vanaspate vīḍvaṅgo hi bhūyāḥ' and other Vānaspatya verses" (Ā. Gṛ. 2-6-5 **NOTE**). O Vanaspati, that is, chariot made of wood, be firm of limb. Be one whose friends we are. Be also an increaser (**NOTE**) and furnished with good heroes, that is, brave warriors, or with sons and the like. Moreover, you are well bound with cows, that is, with products of the cow, leather straps. Being such, make us firm. May the charioteer who stands on you conquer the enemy armies that are to be conquered.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वनस्पते (*vanaspate*)—O chariot, made of wood
- वीड्वंगः (*vīḍvaṃgaḥ*)—having firm limbs
- भायाः (*bhāyāḥ*)—be
- अस्मत्सखा (*asmatsakhā*)—be a friend to all of us
- प्रतरणः (*prataraṇaḥ*)—be our protector
- सुवीरः (*suvīraḥ*)—and be provided with excellent heroes
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- गोभिः (*gobhiḥ*)—with cowhides
- संनद्धः आसि हि (*saṃnaddhaḥ āsi hi*)—you are indeed girt
- वीळयस्व (*vīḷayasva*)—make us strong too
- ते (*te*)—in you
- आस्थाता (*āsthātā*)—the charioteer too who sits
- जेत्वानि (*jetvāni*)—furnished with the means of winning victory
- जयतु (*jayatu*)—may he conquer the enemies

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O deity, made of wood and in the form of a chariot, be of firm limbs. Be our protector and be furnished with excellent heroes. You are girt with cowhides. Make us strong. May the charioteer who sits in you also, furnished with the means of winning victory, conquer the enemies.

##### English translation
(Chariot made of the) forest lord, be strong of fabric ; be our friend, be our protector, and be manned by warriors: you are girt with cow-hides keep us steady; and may he who rides in you be victorious over conquered (foes).

### Printed p. 219

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

This Ṛk beginning वनस्पते (*vanaspate*) is to be recited at the time of mounting the chariot, as is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Gṛhya-sūtra reading वनस्पते वीड्वंगो हि भूया इत्येशयान्यान्यपि वानस्पत्यानि (*vanaspate vīḍvaṃgo hi bhūyā ityeśayānyānyapi vānaspatyāni*) (Ā. Gṛ. 2-6-5)**NOTE**.

Yāska has given a nirvacana for this Ṛk. Here the word vanaspati is taken to mean a chariot made of wood:

वनस्पते दृढांगो हि भवास्मत्सखा प्रतरणः सुवीरः कल्याणवीरो गोभिः संनद्धो असि वीळयस्वेति संस्तंभ स्वास्थाता ते जयतु जेशव्यानि ॥ (*vanaspate dṛḍhāṃgo hi bhavāsmatsakhā prataraṇaḥ suvīraḥ kalyāṇavīro gobhiḥ saṃnaddho asi vīḷayasveti saṃstaṃbha svāsthātā te jayatu jeśavyāni ‖*) (Ni. 9-12)

O chariot, made of vanaspati (a tree, or wood), be firm in your limbs, that is, in the parts that take the form of the wheels and the rest; be firm, that is, solid. You are our friend, one who wishes our welfare, an excellent hero. Well bound with cowhide, you are strong, and you are steady, so as not to shake. May the charioteer who sits in you win what he desires to win. This is the main sense of the nirvacana.

#### Ṛk 27

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दि॒वस्पृ॑थि॒व्याः पर्योज॒ उद्भृ॑तं॒ वन॒स्पति॑भ्यः॒ पर्याभृ॑तं॒  ॑
> सहः ।  
> अ॒पामो॒ज्मानं॒ परि॒ गोभि॒रावृ॑त॒मिंद्र॑स्य॒ वज्रं॑ ह॒विषा॒ रथं॑  
> यज ॥ २७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दिवः । पृथिव्याः । परि । ओजः । उत्भृतं । वनस्पतिभ्यः । परि । आभृतं । सहः ।  
> अपां । ओज्मानं । परि । गोभिः । आवृतं । इंद्रस्य । वज्रं । हविषा । रथं । यज ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दिवो द्युलोकात्पृथिव्या भूमेश्चोद्धृतमुद्धृतं सारत्वेनोपात्तं यदोजो बलमस्ति तद्रूपं । वनस्पतिविकारत्वाद्रथस्य । वनस्पतयो हि पृथिव्याः सारभूताः
> ते च द्युसंबंधिभिरुदकैः प्रवृद्धाः । अतो रथस्य लोकद्वयसारत्वमुपपन्नं ॥ परि

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The strength (ओजः (*ojaḥ*)) that is drawn out from the world of heaven and from the earth, taken up as their essence, is of this form, since the chariot is a product of forest lords. For the forest lords are the essence of the earth, and they are grown by the waters that belong to heaven. Hence it is fitting that the chariot is the essence of the two worlds.

### Printed p. 220

> पंचम्यर्थानुवादी ॥ अपि च वनस्पतिभ्यो वृक्षेभ्यः । परि पूर्ववत् । आभृतमाहृतं
> सहो बलरूपमपामुदकानामोज्मानं प्रेरकं । यद्वा । ओज्मा वेगः । अपां वेग
> इव वेगवंतं गोभिर्गोविकारैश्चर्मभिः परि परित आवृतमावेष्टितमिंद्रस्य वज्रं
> वज्रैकदेशं । श्रूयते हि । इंद्रो वृत्ताय वज्रमुदयच्छत् स त्रेधा व्यभवत्
> वज्रस्तृतीयं रथस्तृतीयं यूपस्तृतीयमिति । एवंगुणविशिष्टं रथं हविषा
> पुरोडाशादिना हे अध्यर्यो यज ॥ नूनमेषोत्तरा च रथदेवत्यस्य हविषो
> याज्यानुवाक्ये लिंगादवगंतव्ये ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The word परि (*pari*) merely restates the sense of the ablative case. Further, वनस्पतिभ्यः (*vanaspatibhyaḥ*) means from trees; परि (*pari*) as before. आभृतं (*ābhṛtaṃ*) is brought; सहः (*sahaḥ*) is that which has the form of strength; अपां (*apāṃ*) means of the waters, and ओज्मानं (*ojmānaṃ*) the impeller. Or else ओज्मा (*ojmā*) is speed: having speed like the speed of the waters. It is covered all round with cowhides, that is, products of the cow, hides, and it is Indra's thunderbolt, that is, a part of the thunderbolt. For it is heard: इंद्रो वृत्ताय वज्रमुदयच्छत् स त्रेधा व्यभवत् वज्रस्तृतीयं रथस्तृतीयं यूपस्तृतीयमिति । (*iṃdro vṛttāya vajramudayacchat sa tredhā vyabhavat vajrastṛtīyaṃ rathastṛtīyaṃ yūpastṛtīyamiti |*) **GLOSS** Such a chariot, O Adhvaryu, worship with oblation, the puroḍāśa and the rest. This Ṛk and the next, it must be understood from the indication, are the yājyā and anuvākyā of the oblation to the chariot deity.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—(O Adhvaryu) from the world of heaven
- पृथिव्याः परि (*pṛthivyāḥ pari*)—from the earth
- उद्भृतं (*udbhṛtaṃ*)—that which has come forth
- ओजः (*ojaḥ*)—that which is endowed with strength
- वनस्पतिभ्यः परि (*vanaspatibhyaḥ pari*)—from the forest lords
- अभृतं (*abhṛtaṃ*)—that which has been procured
- सहः (*sahaḥ*)—that which is of the form of power
- अपां (*apāṃ*)—for the waters
- ओज्मानं (*ojmānaṃ*)—that which is the impeller
- इंद्रस्य (*iṃdrasya*)—of Indra
- वज्रं (*vajraṃ*)—that which is a part of the thunderbolt
- गोभिः (*gobhiḥ*)—with cowhides
- परि अवृतं (*pari avṛtaṃ*)—that which is covered all round
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—the chariot
- हविषा (*haviṣā*)—with the puroḍāśa and the other offerings
- यज (*yaja*)—worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Adhvaryu, worship with the puroḍāśa and other offerings the chariot which has been drawn out from the world of heaven and from the earth as the essence of these two worlds and as of the nature of strength, which has been procured from the forest lords, which is full of vigour, which is the impeller of the waters, which is a part of Indra's thunderbolt, and which is covered all round with cowhides.

##### English translation
Worship with obtations the chariot constructed of the substance of heaven and earth, the extracted essence of the forest lords; the velocity of the waters; the encompassed with the cow-hide; the thunderbolt (of Indra).

#### Ṛk 28

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॑स्य॒ वज्रो॑ म॒रुता॒मनी॑कं मि॒त्रस्य॒ गर्भो॒ वरु॑णस्य॒  ॑
> नाभिः ।  
> सेमां नो॑ ह॒व्यदा॑तिं जुषा॒णो देव॑ रथ॒ प्रति॑ ह॒व्या  ॑
> गृभाय ॥ २८ ॥

### Printed p. 221

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्रस्य । वज्रः । मरुतां । अनीकं । मित्रस्य । गर्भः । वरुणस्य । नाभिः ।  
> स: । इमां । नः । हव्यदातिं । जुषाणः । देव । रथ । प्रति । हव्या । गृभाय ॥ २८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यो रथ इंद्रस्य वज्र उक्तप्रकारेण वज्रैकदेशो मरुतामेतत्संज्ञानां
> देवगणानामनीकमग्रभूतं तद्वच्छिघ्रगामी मित्रस्याहरभिमानिनो देवस्य गर्भो
> गर्भवदंतर्वर्तमानः । अहनि हि रथः संचरति । वरुणस्य रात्र्यभिमानिदेवस्य
> नाभिर्नाभिस्थानीयः । नाभिर्यथा देहमध्यॆ नैश्चल्येनावतिष्ठते तद्वद्रात् क्वचिदेव
> सितः । हे देव द्योतमान रथ य उक्तगुणः स तादृशस्त्वमिमां नोऽस्मदीयां
> हव्यदातिं हविर्दानवतीं यागक्रियां जुषाणः सेवमानः सन् हव्या हव्यान्यस्मदीयानि हवींषि प्रति गृभाय । प्रतिगृहाण ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The chariot which, in the manner stated, is Indra's thunderbolt, that is, a part of the thunderbolt, is the अनीकं (*anīkaṃ*), the front, of the Maruts, the troops of gods so named; like it, it is swift-going. It is the गर्भः (*garbhaḥ*) of Mitra, the deity presiding over the day, abiding within him as in a womb; for the chariot moves about in the day. It is the नाभिः (*nābhiḥ*) of Varuṇa, the deity presiding over the night, that is, it stands in the place of the navel: as the navel stays motionless in the middle of the body, so at night it stays in some one place.**NOTE** O shining chariot-deity, you who are of the qualities stated, accepting this our sacrificial act that gives oblations, accept our oblations.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देव (*deva*)—shining one
- रथ (*ratha*)—O god, the presiding deity of the chariot
- इंद्रस्य (*iṃdrasya*)—of Indra
- वज्रः (*vajraḥ*)—being a part of the thunderbolt
- मरुतां (*marutāṃ*)—of the Maruts
- अनीकं (*anīkaṃ*)—being the leader
- मित्रस्य (*mitrasya*)—for the god Mitra
- गर्भः (*garbhaḥ*)—being the one in the middle
- वरुणस्य (*varuṇasya*)—for the god Varuṇa
- नाभिः (*nābhiḥ*)—being the one who stands in the place of the navel
- सः (*saḥ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—offered by us
- इमां (*imāṃ*)—this
- हव्यदातिं (*havyadātiṃ*)—sacrificial act
- जुषाणः (*juṣāṇaḥ*)—enjoying
- हव्या (*havyā*)—the oblations
- प्रति गृभाय (*prati gṛbhāya*)—accept

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O shining god, presiding deity of the chariot, you who are a part of Indra's thunderbolt, the leader of the Maruts, the embryo of the god Mitra and the navel of the god Varuṇa, enjoying this sacrificial act offered by us, accept the oblations.

##### English translation
Do you, divine chariot, who are the thunderbolt of Indra, the precursor of the Maruts, the embryo of Mitra, the navel of Varuna, propitiated by this our sacrifice, accept the oblation.

### Printed p. 222
#### Ṛk 29

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उप॑ श्वासय पृथि॒वीमु॒त द्यां पु॑रु॒त्रा ते॑ मनुतां॒ विस्मि॑तं॒  ॑
> जगत् ।  
> स दुं॑दुभे स॒जूरिंद्रे॑ण दे॒वैर्दू॒राद्दवी॑यो॒ अप॑ सेध॒  ॑
> शत्रून् ॥ २९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उप । श्वासय । पृथिवीं । उत । द्यां । पुरुत्रा । ते । मनुतां । विस्मितं । जगत् ।  
> सः । दुंदुभे । सजूः । इंद्रेण । देवैः । दूरात् । दवीयः । अप । सेध ।  
> शत्रून् ॥ २९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> परेण तृचेन संग्रामे दुंदुभिरभिमर्शनीयः । सूत्रितं च । उप श्वासय
> पृथिवीमुत द्यामिति तृचेन दुंदुभिमभिमृशेत् । आ. गृ. ३-१२-१७ । इति ॥
> हे दुंदुभे पृथिवीमुतापि च द्यां दिवमुप श्वासय । आत्मीयेन जयघोषेणाश्वासय । यथा लोकद्वयं त्वदीयेन शब्दीनापूरितं भवति तादृशं शब्दं
> कुर्वित्यर्थः । अपि च विस्थितं विशेषेण स्थितं स्थावरं जंगमं चोभयविधं
> प्राणिजातं ते त्वदीयं शब्दं पुरुत्रा बहुधा मनुतां । मन्यतां । जानातु ।
> हे दुंदुभे स त्वमिंद्रेणान्वैर्देवैश्च सजूः सह दूराद्दवीयो दूरादपि
> दूरतॆरं शत्रुनस्मदीयानप सेध । आपगमय! अत्र निरुक्तं। दुंदुभिरिति शब्दानुकरणं
> द्रुमो भिन्न इति वा दुंदुभ्यतेर्वा स्याद्वधकर्मणः । नि. ९-१२ । इत्यादि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In battle the dundubhi is to be touched with the following tṛca. It is laid down in the sūtra: the dundubhi is to be touched with the tṛca beginning उप श्वासय (*upa śvāsaya*) (Ā. Gṛ. 3-12-17)**NOTE**. O dundubhi, make the earth and also heaven draw breath: reassure them with your own cry of victory; make such a sound that both worlds are filled with your sound. This is the meaning. Further, all living beings, both the standing (the immovable) and the moving, who stand in their various ways, should in many ways (पुरुत्रा (*purutrā*), that is, बहुधा (*bahudhā*)) take note of your sound; let them know it. O dundubhi, you, united with Indra and the other gods, drive away our enemies from far, from farther than far; make them go away. Here the Nirukta says: शब्दानुकरणं द्रुमो भिन्न इति वा दुंदुभ्यतेर्वा स्याद्वधकर्मणः (*śabdānukaraṇaṃ drumo bhinna iti vā duṃdubhyatervā syādvadhakarmaṇaḥ*) (Ni. 9-12).**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दुंदुभे (*duṃdubhe*)—O dundubhi, special instrument of war so named
- पृथिवीं (*pṛthivīṃ*)—the earth
- उत (*uta*)—and
- द्यां उप (*dyāṃ upa*)—the world of heaven (so as to pervade it fully)
- श्वासय (*śvāsaya*)—make a loud sound
- विस्थितं (*visthitaṃ*)—that which stands firm
- जगत् (*jagat*)—that which is of the nature of the moving; the beings of the two kinds
- ते (*te*)—your sound
- पुरुत्रा (*purutrā*)—in many ways
- मनुतां (*manutāṃ*)—let them know; (O dundubhi)
- सः (*saḥ*)—you, the famous one
- इंद्रेण (*iṃdreṇa*)—with Indra
- देवैः (*devaiḥ*)—with other gods

### Printed p. 223
- सजूः (*sajūḥ*)—united
- दूराद्दवीयः (*dūrāddavīyaḥ*)—even though at the greatest distance
- शत्रून् (*śatrūn*)—the enemies
- अप सेध (*apa sedha*)—drive them away and make them vanish

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O dundubhi, special instrument of war so named, make a loud sound so as to pervade fully the earth and the world of heaven. May the beings of the two kinds, the immovable and the moving, know your sound in many ways. O dundubhi, the famous one, united with Indra and the other gods, drive away the enemies and make them vanish, even though they be at the greatest distance.

##### English translation
War drum, fill with your sound both heaven and earth; and let all things, fixed or moveable, be aware of it: do you, who are associated with Indra and the gods, drive away our foes to the remotest distance.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In time of battle the dundubhi (the nagāri drum) is to be touched with the tṛca (three Ṛks) beginning उप श्वासय (*upa śvāsaya*), as is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Gṛhya-sūtra: उप श्वासय पृथिवीमुत द्यामिति तृचेन दुंदुभिमभिमृशेत् (*upa śvāsaya pṛthivīmuta dyāmiti tṛcena duṃdubhimabhimṛśet*) (Ā. Gṛ. 3-12-17)**NOTE**.

In the course of explaining the meaning of the word dundubhi, Yāska has given a nirvacana for this Ṛk:

दुंदुभिः । दुंदुभिरिति शब्दानुकरणं । द्रुमो भिन्न इति वा । दुंदुभ्यतेर्ना स्याच्छब्दकर्मणः ॥ तस्यैषा भवति ॥ (*duṃdubhiḥ | duṃdubhiriti śabdānukaraṇaṃ | drumo bhinna iti vā | duṃdubhyaternā syācchabdakarmaṇaḥ ‖ tasyaiṣā bhavati ‖*)

उप श्वासय पृथिवीं च दिवं च बहुधा ते घोषं मन्यतां विष्ठितं स्थावरं जंगमं च यत् स दुंदुभे सहजोषण इंद्रेण च देवेभ्यश्च दूरात् दूरतरमपसेध शत्रून् ॥ (*upa śvāsaya pṛthivīṃ ca divaṃ ca bahudhā te ghoṣaṃ manyatāṃ viṣṭhitaṃ sthāvaraṃ jaṃgamaṃ ca yat sa duṃdubhe sahajoṣaṇa iṃdreṇa ca devebhyaśca dūrāt dūrataramapasedha śatrūn ‖*) (Ni. 9-13)**NOTE**

Dundubhi means the nagāri drum; because it makes the sound dhuṃ dhuṃ, it has come by the name dundubhi. Or else, drumas means a tree; because this is made by splitting and cutting it, it is named dundubhi. Or it may also be derived from the word दुंदुभ्य (*duṃdubhya*), which means making a sound.

### Printed p. 224
O dundubhi, with your sound make the earth and heaven pervaded and filled, that is, let your firm sound pervade the earth and the intermediate region. May all things, of the nature of the moving and the unmoving, or all beings, know you (your sound). And, O dundubhi, you, together with Indra and the gods, make our enemies flee far and farther, that is, very far. This is the main sense of the nirvacana.

#### Ṛk 30

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ क्रं॑दय॒ बल॒मोजो॑ न॒ आ धा॒ निः ष्ट॑निहि दुरि॒ता बाध॑मानः ।  
> अप॑ प्रोथ दुंदुभे दु॒च्चुना॑ इ॒त इंद्र॑स्य मु॒ष्टिर॑सि वी॒ळय॑स्व ॥ ३० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । क्रंदय । बलं । ओजः । नः । आ । धाः । निः । स्तनिहि । दुः इता । बाधमानः ।  
> अप । प्रोथ । दुंदुभे । दुच्चुनाः । इतः । इंद्रस्य । मुष्टिः । असि । वीळयस्व । ३०॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे दुंदुभे आ क्रंदय । अस्मच्छत्रून्नोदय । बलं सेनारूपमोजः
> शरीरबलं च नोऽस्मभ्यमा धाः । आधेहि । प्रयच्छ । कथा दुरिता दुरितानि दुर्गमनानि
> बाधमानो हिंसंस्त्वं निः स्तनिहि । नितरां शब्दं कुरु ॥ स्तन शब्द इति धातुः ॥
> हे दुंदुभे दुच्चना अस्मद्दुःखहेतुभूतं शुनं सुखं यासां तादृशीः शत्रुसेना
> इतोऽस्मत्स्थानादप प्रोथ । बाधस्व । त्वं चेंद्रस्य मुष्टिरसि । मुष्टिरिव शूत्रोणां
> हंता भवसि । अतोऽस्मान्वीळयस्व । दृढीकुरु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O dundubhi, आ क्रंदय (*ā kraṃdaya*): urge on our enemies. Give us (आ धाः (*ā dhāḥ*): bestow, grant) strength (बलं (*balaṃ*)) in the form of an army and bodily strength (ओजः (*ojaḥ*)). How? Harassing, that is, hurting, the दुरिता (*duritā*), the hard-to-traverse (misfortunes), you thunder aloud (निः स्तनिहि (*niḥ stanihi*): make a sound continuously). The root is स्तन (*stana*), in the sense of sound. O dundubhi, those armies of enemies (दुच्चना (*duccanā*)) whose happiness (शुनं (*śunaṃ*), that is, सुखं (*sukhaṃ*)) is the cause of sorrow to us, drive away (अप प्रोथ (*apa protha*): repel) from this, our place. And you are Indra's fist; like a fist you are the slayer of the enemies. Therefore make us firm, make us strong (वीळयस्व (*vīḷayasva*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दुंदुभे (*duṃdubhe*)—O dundubhi
- आ क्रंदय (*ā kraṃdaya*)—destroy our enemies
- बलं (*balaṃ*)—the strength in the form of an army
- ओजः (*ojaḥ*)—our bodily strength
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- आ धाः (*ā dhāḥ*)—bestow
- दुरिता (*duritā*)—the enemies of sinful mind
- बाधमानः (*bādhamānaḥ*)—harassing
- निः स्तनिहि (*niḥ stanihi*)—make a loud sound
- दुच्चुनाः (*duccunāḥ*)—the armies of enemies that cause us sorrow
- इतः (*itaḥ*)—from this place
- अप प्रोथ (*apa protha*)—drive far away
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you indeed
- इंद्रस्य (*iṃdrasya*)—for Indra
- मुष्टिः असि (*muṣṭiḥ asi*)—you are of the form of a fist
- वीळयस्व (*vīḷayasva*)—(therefore) make us strong

### Printed p. 225

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O dundubhi, make our enemies weep. Provide strength in the form of an army, and for our body power of its own. Harassing the enemies of sinful mind, make a loud sound. Drive far from this place the armies of enemies that cause us sorrow. O dundubhi, you are of the form of a fist for Indra. Therefore make us strong.

##### English translation
Sound loud against the (hostile) host : animate our prowess: thunder aloud, terrifying the evil-minded : repel, drum, those whose delight it is to harm us : you are the fist of Indra; inspire us with fierceness.

#### Ṛk 31

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आमूर॑ज प्र॒त्याव॑र्तये॒माः के॑तु॒मद्दुं॑दु॒भिर्वा॑वदीति ।  
> समश्व॑पर्णा॒श्चरं॑ति नो॒ नरो॒ऽस्माक॑मिंद्र र॒थिनो॑ जयंतु ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । अमूः । अज । प्रतिआवर्तय । इमाः । केतुमत् । दुंदुभिः । नावदीति ।  
> सं । अश्वपर्णाः । चरंति । नः । नरः । अस्माकं । इंद्र । रथिनः । जयंतु ॥ ३१ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्याः पूर्वार्धो दुंदुभिदेवत्य उत्तरार्धश्चैंद्रः । यद्वा । उभावप्यर्धर्चावैंद्रौ । हे इंद्र अमूः शत्रुषु स्थितास्ता गा आज । आस्मान्प्रत्यागमय ।
> इमा अस्मदीया गाः शत्रुभिर्जिघृक्षिताः प्रत्यावर्तय । प्रतिनिवर्तय । अयं च
> दुंदुभिः केतुमत् प्रज्ञानवत् यथा सर्वैर्ज्ञायते तथा वावदीति । भृशं शब्दं
> करोति । अश्वपर्णा अश्वपतना अश्ववाहाश्च नोऽस्मदीयाश्च नरः पुरुषाः सं चरंति ।
> शत्रुभिर्युध्यमाना वर्तंते । तथा सति हे इंद्र अस्माकं रथिनो रथारूढाः
> पुरषाः शत्रून् जयंतु ॥
> XXI 15

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The first half of this Ṛk has the drum (dundubhi) as deity and the latter half is addressed to Indra. Or else both half-verses are addressed to Indra. O Indra, bring to us (आज (*āja*)) those cows of ours that stand among the enemies; make them come to us. Turn back (प्रत्यावर्तय (*pratyāvartaya*)) these cows of ours that the enemies wish to seize; make them return. And this dundubhi sounds loudly (वावदीति (*vāvadīti*): it makes a mighty sound), केतुमत् (*ketumat*), that is, so as to be known to all. Our men, अश्वपर्णा (*aśvaparṇā*), those who go with horses and who ride horses, move about together (सं चरंति (*saṃ caraṃti*)); they are engaged in fighting with the enemies. That being so, O Indra, may our men who are mounted on chariots conquer the enemies.

### Printed p. 226

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- अमूः (*amūḥ*)—our cows that are among the enemies
- आ अज (*ā aja*)—make them come and join us
- इमाः (*imāḥ*)—these very cows of ours
- प्रत्यावर्तय (*pratyāvartaya*)—turn them back to us
- दुंदुभिः (*duṃdubhiḥ*)—this instrument of war
- केतुमत् (*ketumat*)—so as to come to the notice of all
- वावदीति (*vāvadīti*)—makes a sound
- अश्वपर्णाः (*aśvaparṇāḥ*)—mounted on horses
- नः (*naḥ*)—and belonging to us
- नरः (*naraḥ*)—men
- सं चरंति (*saṃ caraṃti*)—are moving about on the battlefield
- अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*)—belonging to us
- रथिनः (*rathinaḥ*)—men mounted on chariots
- जयंतु (*jayaṃtu*)—may they conquer the enemies

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, make our cows that are among the enemies come and join us. Turn back to us these very cows of ours. This war-dundubhi also sounds so that all may know it. Our warriors, mounted on horses and on chariots, are moving about on the battlefield. May all of them be victorious over the enemies.

##### English translation
Recover these our cattle, Indra ; bring them back : the drum sounds repeatedly as a signal : our leaders, mounted on their steeds, assemble : may our warriors, riding in their cars, Indra, be victorious.

वेदार्थस्य प्रकाशेन तमो हार्दं निवारयन् । पुमर्थांश्च तुरो देयाद्विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरः ॥ (*vedārthasya prakāśena tamo hārdaṃ nivārayan | pumarthāṃśca turo deyādvidyātīrthamaheśvaraḥ ‖*) **GLOSS**

इति श्रीमद्राजाधिराज परमेश्वर वैदिकमार्गप्रवर्तक श्री वीरबुक्कभूपालसाम्राज्यधुरंधरेण सायणाचार्येण विरचिते माधवीये वेदार्थप्रकाशे ऋक्संहिताभाष्ये चतुर्थाष्टके सप्तमोऽध्यायः समाप्तः ॥ (*iti śrīmadrājādhirāja parameśvara vaidikamārgapravartaka śrī vīrabukkabhūpālasāmrājyadhuraṃdhareṇa sāyaṇācāryeṇa viracite mādhavīye vedārthaprakāśe ṛksaṃhitābhāṣye caturthāṣṭake saptamo 'dhyāyaḥ samāptaḥ ‖*) ओं तत्त्वत् (*oṃ tattvat*) **GLOSS**

Here ends the seventh adhyāya, in the fourth aṣṭaka of the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, which has the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya and a translation in the Kannada language.

यदक्षरपदभ्रष्टं मात्रहीनं तु यद्भवेत् । तत्सर्वं क्षम्यतां देव वागीश्वर नमोऽस्तुते ॥ (*yadakṣarapadabhraṣṭaṃ mātrahīnaṃ tu yadbhavet | tatsarvaṃ kṣamyatāṃ deva vāgīśvara namo 'stute ‖*) **GLOSS** शुभं भूयात् (*śubhaṃ bhūyāt*) मंगळ (*maṃgaḷa*)

ओं (*oṃ*) श्रीः (*śrīḥ*) श्री गणाधिपतये नमः (*śrī gaṇādhipataye namaḥ*)

वागीशाद्याः सुमनसः सर्वार्थानामुपक्रमे । यं नत्वा कृतकृत्याः स्युस्तं नमामि गजाननं ॥ (*vāgīśādyāḥ sumanasaḥ sarvārthānāmupakrame | yaṃ natvā kṛtakṛtyāḥ syustaṃ namāmi gajānanaṃ ‖*) **GLOSS**

यस्य निश्वसितं वेदा यो वेदेभ्योऽखिलं जगत् । निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरं ॥ (*yasya niśvasitaṃ vedā yo vedebhyo 'khilaṃ jagat | nirmame tamahaṃ vaṃde vidyātīrthamaheśvaraṃ ‖*) **GLOSS**

वेदः शिवः शिवो वेदो वेदाध्यायिया सदाशिवः । तस्मात्सर्वप्रयत्नेन वेदमेव सदा जपेत् ॥ (*vedaḥ śivaḥ śivo vedo vedādhyāyiyā sadāśivaḥ | tasmātsarvaprayatnena vedameva sadā japet ‖*) **GLOSS**

The Ṛgveda-saṃhitā with the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (with the explanation of the translation of the meaning in the Kannada language).

चतुर्थाष्टके अष्टमोऽध्यायः (*caturthāṣṭake aṣṭamo 'dhyāyaḥ*)

The eighth adhyāya in the fourth aṣṭaka. The forty-eighth sūkta of the sixth maṇḍala.

## Sūkta 48 (Maṇḍala 6)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अथाष्टमाध्याय आरभ्यते । षष्ठमंडलस्य चतुर्थेऽनुवाके चत्वारि सूक्तानि
> व्याख्यातानि । यज्ञायज्ञेति द्वाविंशत्यृचं पंचमं सूक्तं बृहस्पतिपुत्रस्य
> शंयोरार्षं अत्रानुक्रम्यते । यज्ञायज्ञा द्वैधिका शंयुस्तृणपाणिकं
> प्रश्निसूक्तं प्रगाथौ बृहती महासतोबृहती महाबार्हतबार्हतौ प्रगाथावित्याग्नेय्यः काकुभः प्रगाथः पुरउष्णिग्बृहत्यतिजगतीति मारुत्योऽंत्यास्तिस्रो वासां
> लिंगोक्तदेवताः काकुभः प्रगाथः पुरउष्णिग्बृहतीति पौष्टो बृहती महाबृहती
> यवमध्यांत्यानुष्टुब्दावाभूम्योर्वा पृश्नेर्वेति । यज्ञायज्ञा व इत्येषा

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Now the eighth adhyāya begins. In the fourth anuvāka of the sixth maṇḍala four sūktas have been explained. Here is set out the fifth sūkta, beginning यज्ञायज्ञेति (*yajñāyajñeti*), of twenty-two Ṛks, whose seer is Śaṃyu, son of Bṛhaspati. The Anukramaṇī says: यज्ञायज्ञा द्वैधिका शंयुस्तृणपाणिकं प्रश्निसूक्तं प्रगाथौ बृहती महासतोबृहती महाबार्हतबार्हतौ प्रगाथावित्याग्नेय्यः काकुभः प्रगाथः पुरउष्णिग्बृहत्यतिजगतीति मारुत्योऽंत्यास्तिस्रो वासां लिंगोक्तदेवताः काकुभः प्रगाथः पुरउष्णिग्बृहतीति पौष्टो बृहती महाबृहती यवमध्यांत्यानुष्टुब्दावाभूम्योर्वा पृश्नेर्वेति । (*yajñāyajñā dvaidhikā śaṃyustṛṇapāṇikaṃ praśnisūktaṃ pragāthau bṛhatī mahāsatobṛhatī mahābārhatabārhatau pragāthāvityāgneyyaḥ kākubhaḥ pragāthaḥ purauṣṇigbṛhatyatijagatīti mārutyo 'ṃtyāstisro vāsāṃ liṃgoktadevatāḥ kākubhaḥ pragāthaḥ purauṣṇigbṛhatīti pauṣṭo bṛhatī mahābṛhatī yavamadhyāṃtyānuṣṭubdāvābhūmyorvā pṛśnerveti |*).**NOTE** Of these, the Ṛk यज्ञायज्ञा व (*yajñāyajñā va*) is a bṛhatī, since the mark of that metre is present: the third pāda has twelve syllables.

### Printed p. 228

> बृहती । तृतीयो द्वादशक इति तल्लक्षणसद्भावात् । द्वितीया सतोबृहती । आयुजौ
> जागतौ सतोबृहतीत्युक्तलक्षणोपेतत्वात् । तृतीयाचतुर्थि बृहतीसतोबृहत्यौ
> पंचमी बृहती षष्ठी महासतोबृहती । अष्टाक्षरास्त्रयः पादा द्वादशाक्षरा द्यौ
> पादौ सा महासतोबृहती । सप्तमी महाबृहती । अष्टाक्षराश्चत्वारः पादा एको
> द्वादशाक्षरः सा महाबृहती । अष्टमी महासतोबृहती । अष्टिनस्त्रयो द्यौ जागतौ
> सा महासतो बृहती । नवमी बृहती दशमी सतोबृहती । एता दशाग्नि देवत्याः ।
> एकादशी ककुप् । मध्यमो यदि द्वादशाक्षर उभयतोऽष्टकौ द्यौ पादौ सा ककुप् ।
> द्वादशी सतोबृहती त्रयोदशी पुरउष्णिक् । आद्यो जागतो द्वितीयतृतीयौ
> गायत्रौ सा पुरउष्णिक् । चतुर्दशी बृहती पंचदश्यतिजगती द्वापंचाशदक्षरा ।
> एकादश्याद्याः पंच मरुद्देवताकाः । त्रयोदश्यादीनां तिसृणां लिंगोक्ता
> इंद्रादयो ना देवताः । षोडशी ककुप् सप्तदशी सतोबृहत्यष्टादशी पुरउष्णिगेकोनविंशी बृहती । षोडश्यादीनां चतसृणां पूषा देवता । विंशी बृहत्येकविंशी
> यवमध्या महाबृहती । आद्यौ द्वावष्टकौ तृतीयो द्वादशकश्चतुर्थपंचमावष्टकौ
> सा यवमध्या महाबृहती । विंशैकविंश्योः पृश्नि सूक्तमित्युक्तत्वान्रुतां माता
> पृश्निर्देवता । द्वाविंश्यनुष्टुप् । स चापि पृश्नादेवत्या द्यावाभूमिदेवत्या वा ॥
> सूक्तविनियोगो लैंगिकः । अग्नि मारुते यज्ञायज्ञाव इत्ययं स्तोत्रियप्रगाथः ।
> सूत्रितं च । यज्ञायज्ञा वो अग्नये देवो वो द्रविणोदा इति प्रगाथौ
> स्तोत्रियानुरूपौ ॥ आ. ५-२० । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The second Ṛk is a satobṛhatī, since it has the stated mark: its odd pādas (आयुजौ (*āyujau*)) are of the jagatī kind. The third and fourth are a bṛhatī and a satobṛhatī, the fifth a bṛhatī. The sixth is a mahāsatobṛhatī: three pādas of eight syllables and two of twelve. The seventh is a mahābṛhatī: four pādas of eight syllables and one of twelve. The eighth is a mahāsatobṛhatī: three of eight syllables and two of the jagatī kind. The ninth is a bṛhatī and the tenth a satobṛhatī. These ten are addressed to Agni. The eleventh is a kakubh: if the middle pāda has twelve syllables and the two pādas on either side eight. The twelfth is a satobṛhatī; the thirteenth a puraūṣṇih: the first pāda of the jagatī kind, the second and third of the gāyatrī kind. The fourteenth is a bṛhatī; the fifteenth an atijagatī of fifty-two syllables. The five Ṛks from the eleventh on have the Maruts as deity. For the three from the thirteenth on the deities are those stated by the indication, Indra and the others.**NOTE** The sixteenth is a kakubh, the seventeenth a satobṛhatī, the eighteenth a puraūṣṇih, the nineteenth a bṛhatī; for these four from the sixteenth on Pūṣan is the deity. The twentieth is a bṛhatī; the twenty-first a yavamadhyā mahābṛhatī: the first two pādas of eight syllables, the third of twelve, the fourth and fifth of eight. Because the twentieth and twenty-first are called a Pṛśni sūkta, Pṛśni, the mother of the Maruts, is their deity. The twenty-second is an anuṣṭubh, and it too is addressed to Pṛśni or to Heaven and Earth. The application of the sūkta is by indication: at the Agnimāruta this pragātha beginning यज्ञायज्ञाव (*yajñāyajñāva*) is the stotriya pragātha. This is laid down in the sūtra: the two pragāthas, stotriya and anurūpa (Ā. Sū. 5-20).

##### Anuvāda

Since the explanation of the seventh adhyāya of the fourth aṣṭaka in the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā has ended, we now begin the explanation of the eighth adhyāya. The four sūktas of the fourth anuvāka of the sixth maṇḍala were explained in the previous adhyāya. The sūkta beginning यज्ञायज्ञा (*yajñāyajñā*) is the fifth sūkta in the fourth anuvāka. It has twenty-two Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Śaṃyu, the son of Bṛhaspati. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: यज्ञायज्ञा द्व्यधिका शंयुस्तृणपाणिकं प्रश्निसूक्तं प्रगाथौ बृहती महासतोबृहती महाबार्हतबार्हतौ प्रगाथावित्याग्नेय्यः काकुभः प्रगाथः पुरउष्णिग्रहत्यतिजगतीति मारुत्योऽन्तास्तिस्रो वासां लिंगोक्तदेवताः काकुभः प्रगाथः पुरउष्णिग्रहतीति पौष्ठो बृहती महाबृहती यवमध्यांत्यानुष्टुप् द्यावाभूम्योर्वा पृश्नेर्वा (*yajñāyajñā dvyadhikā śaṃyustṛṇapāṇikaṃ praśnisūktaṃ pragāthau bṛhatī mahāsatobṛhatī mahābārhatabārhatau pragāthāvityāgneyyaḥ kākubhaḥ pragāthaḥ purauṣṇigrahatyatijagatīti mārutyo 'ntāstisro vāsāṃ liṃgoktadevatāḥ kākubhaḥ pragāthaḥ purauṣṇigrahatīti pauṣṭho bṛhatī mahābṛhatī yavamadhyāṃtyānuṣṭup dyāvābhūmyorvā pṛśnervā*). The first Ṛk of this sūkta, यज्ञायज्ञा वः (*yajñāyajñā vaḥ*), is of the bṛhatī metre. If the third pāda has twelve syllables and the remaining pādas eight each, that metre is called bṛhatī. If in a Ṛk there are five

### Printed p. 229
pādas, of which four pādas have eight syllables each and the remaining one pāda twelve syllables, it is called mahābṛhatī. The eighth Ṛk of this sūkta is of the mahāsatobṛhatī metre. If in a Ṛk there are five pādas, of which three pādas have eight syllables each and the remaining two pādas twelve each, it is called mahāsatobṛhatī. The ninth Ṛk is of the bṛhatī metre. The tenth Ṛk is of the satobṛhatī metre. The first ten Ṛks of this sūkta have Agni as deity. The eleventh Ṛk is of the kakubh metre. If in a Ṛk there are three pādas, of which the first and the last have eight syllables each and the middle one twelve, it is called kakubh. The twelfth Ṛk is of the satobṛhatī metre. The thirteenth is of the metre called puraūṣṇih. If in a Ṛk there are three pādas, of which the first has twelve syllables as in the jagatī metre, and the second and third have eight each as in the gāyatrī metre, it is called puraūṣṇih. The fourteenth Ṛk is of the bṛhatī metre. The fifteenth is of the metre called atijagatī. If in a Ṛk there are five pādas, the first three of twelve syllables each and the last two of eight each, fifty-two syllables in all, it is called atijagatī. The five Ṛks from the eleventh on have the Maruts as deity. For the three Ṛks from the thirteenth on the deities are those named by the indication, or Indra and the others. The sixteenth Ṛk is of the kakubh metre. The seventeenth is of the satobṛhatī metre. The eighteenth is of the puraūṣṇih metre. The nineteenth is of the bṛhatī metre. For the four Ṛks from the sixteenth on Pūṣan is the deity. The twentieth Ṛk is of the bṛhatī metre. The twenty-first Ṛk is of the metre called yavamadhyāmahābṛhatī. If in a Ṛk there are five pādas, with eight syllables each in the first two pādas, twelve in the third, and eight each in the last two, it is called yavamadhyāmahābṛhatī. Since the twentieth and twenty-first Ṛks are called the Pṛśni sūkta, Pṛśni, the mother of the Maruts, is the deity of those Ṛks. The twenty-second Ṛk is of the anuṣṭubh metre. Pṛśni or Bhūmi may be the deity of this Ṛk. The application of the sūkta is by indication. At the time of reciting the Agnimāruta śastra mantras, this tṛca beginning यज्ञायज्ञा वः (*yajñāyajñā vaḥ*) is to be recited as the stotriya pragātha tṛca, as is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, यज्ञायज्ञा वो अग्नये देवो वो द्रविणोदा इति प्रगाथौ स्तोत्रियानुरूपौ (*yajñāyajñā vo agnaye devo vo draviṇodā iti pragāthau stotriyānurūpau*) (Ā. Sū. 5-20).

##### Particulars

Sūkta 48. Maṇḍala 6. Anuvāka 4. Sūkta 48. Aṣṭaka 4. Adhyāya 8. Vargas 1, 2, 3, 4. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 22. Seer: Śaṃyu Bārhaspatya. Tṛṇapāṇika, a Pṛśni sūkta. Deity: Ṛks 1-10, Agni; 11, 12, 20, 21, the Maruts; 13-15, the Maruts or the deities named by the indication; 16-19, Pūṣan; 22, Pṛśni, or Heaven and Earth. Metre: Ṛks 1, 3, 5, 9, 14, 19, 20, bṛhatī; 2, 4, 10, 12, 17, satobṛhatī; 6, 8, mahāsatobṛhatī; 7, mahābṛhatī; 11, 16, kakubh; 13, 18, puraūṣṇih; 15, atijagatī; 21, yavamadhyā mahābṛhatī; 22, anuṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> य॒ज्ञाय॑ज्ञा वो अ॒ग्नये॑ गि॒रागि॑रा च॒ दक्ष॑से ।  
> प्रप्र॑ व॒यम॒मृतं॑ जा॒तवे॑दसं प्रि॒यं मि॒त्रं न शं॑सिषं ॥१॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यज्ञायज्ञा । वः । अग्नये । गिरागिरा । च । दक्षसे ।  
> प्रप्र । वयं । अमृतं । जातवेदसं । प्रियं । मित्रं । न । शंसिषं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे स्तोतारो वो यूयं यज्ञा यज्ञा यज्ञे यज्ञे सर्वॆषु यागेषु
> दक्षसे प्रवृद्धायाग्नये गिरा गिरा स्तुतिरूपया वाचा वाचा स्तोत्रं कुरुतेति
> शेषः । चशब्दो भिन्नक्रमो व इत्यस्मात्परो द्रष्टव्यः । यूयं च स्तोत्रं कुरुत ।
> वयमपि तमग्निं प्र शंसिषं ॥ प्रसमुपोदः पादपूरण इति प्रशब्दस्य द्विरुक्तिः
> पादपूरणार्था । व्यत्ययेनैकवचनं । छांदसो लुज् ॥ प्रशंसाम । कीदृशं ।
> अमृतं मरणरहितं जातवेदसं जातानां वेदितारं जातप्रज्ञं जातधनं वा मित्रं
> न सखिभूतनिव प्रियमनुकूलं ॥ यद्वा । व्यत्ययेन त्वमित्यस्य वसादेशः । अग्निय
> इति च कर्मणि चतुर्थी । क्रियाग्रहणमपि कर्तव्यमिति कर्मणः संप्रदानत्वात् ।
> चशब्दश्चण्णिति निपातश्चेदर्थे वर्तते । दक्षस इति च दक्षेर्वृद्दिकर्मणोऽंतर्भानि-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O praisers, in every sacrifice (यज्ञा यज्ञा (*yajñā yajñā*): in each and every rite) offer praise to Agni, अग्नये (*agnaye*), who is दक्षसे (*dakṣase*), grown great, with every praise (गिरा गिरा (*girā girā*): with speech after speech in the form of praise): this has to be supplied. The word च (*ca*) is to be seen after व (*va*), out of its order. And you offer praise; we too praise him. How is he? Immortal (अमृतं (*amṛtaṃ*): free from death), जातवेदसं (*jātavedasaṃ*): the knower of all that is born, or one whose knowledge is born, or one whose wealth is born; dear and favourable, like a friend. Or else, by inversion, वो (*vo*) stands for त्वं (*tvaṃ*), you, and अग्नये (*agnaye*) is a dative in the sense of the object, since the object is an indirect recipient.

### Printed p. 231

> तण्यर्थाल्लट रूपं । चणा योगात् निपातैर्यद्यदिहंतेति निघातप्रतिषेधः ॥
> तत्रायमर्थः । हे स्तोतस्त्वं यज्ञे यज्ञ इममग्निं गिरा गिरा स्तुत्या दक्षसे च
> वर्धयसि चेत् वयमप्यमृतत्वादिगुणकं तं प्रशंसाम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The particle च (*ca*) here has the sense of "if". The meaning is this: O praiser, if you in every sacrifice make this Agni grow with every praise, and make him thrive, then we too praise him, who has the qualities of immortality and the rest.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वः (*vaḥ*)—(O praisers) you
- यज्ञा यज्ञा (*yajñā yajñā*)—in each and every sacrifice
- दक्षसे (*dakṣase*)—who has abundant power
- अग्नये (*agnaye*)—with reference to Agni
- गिरागिरा (*girāgirā*)—praise with many kinds of praise
- वयं च (*vayaṃ ca*)—we also
- अमृतं (*amṛtaṃ*)—who is free from the nature of death
- जातवेदसं (*jātavedasaṃ*)—who is of born wisdom
- मित्रं न (*mitraṃ na*)—like a friend
- प्रियं (*priyaṃ*)—and beneficent, Agni
- प्र शंसिषं (*pra śaṃsiṣaṃ*)—we praise and worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O praisers, in every sacrifice praise with many kinds of praise Agni, who has abundant power. We also praise and worship Agni, who is free from the nature of death, of born wisdom, and beneficent like a friend.

##### English translation
At every sacrifice (honour) the mighty Agni with your reiterated praise, whilst we glorify him, the immortal, who knows, all things, our dear friend.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

As has been said earlier in the application of this sūkta, this Ṛk beginning यज्ञा यज्ञा (*yajñā yajñā*) is to be recited as the stotriya pragātha mantra in the Agnimāruta śastra mantras.

The Aitareya Brāhmaṇa says thus in this matter:

यज्ञायज्ञावो अग्नये देवो वो द्रविणोदा इति मध्ये योनिं चानुरूपं च शंसति तद्यन्मध्ये योनिं चानुरूपं च शंसति तस्मान्मध्ये योनिर्धृता । यदु द्वे सूक्ते शस्त्रा शंसति प्रतिष्ठयोरेव तदुपरिष्टात्पृजननं दधाति प्रजात्यै प्रजायुते प्रजया पशुभिर्य एवं वेद ॥ (*yajñāyajñāvo agnaye devo vo draviṇodā iti madhye yoniṃ cānurūpaṃ ca śaṃsati tadyanmadhye yoniṃ cānurūpaṃ ca śaṃsati tasmānmadhye yonirdhṛtā | yadu dve sūkte śastrā śaṃsati pratiṣṭhayoreva tadupariṣṭātpṛjananaṃ dadhāti prajātyai prajāyute prajayā paśubhirya evaṃ veda ‖*) (Ai. Brā. 1-35)

The hotṛ recites at the beginning of the Agnimāruta śastra mantras the sūkta of the nature of Agni Vaiśvānara, वैश्वानराय पृथुपाजसे (*vaiśvānarāya pṛthupājase*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-3). After that he recites, addressed to the Maruts, the sūkta of the nature of the Marut deities, प्रत्वक्षसः प्रतवसः (*pratvakṣasaḥ pratavasaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-87). For the śastra mantras, after

### Printed p. 232
these two sūktas have been recited, the hotṛ recites the stotriya and the anurūpa pragātha mantras. Here the Ṛk now in hand, यज्ञायज्ञावो आग्नये (*yajñāyajñāvo āgnaye*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-48-1), is the stotriya mantra. This is the first pragātha mantra. The Ṛk देवो वो द्रविणोदा (*devo vo draviṇodā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-16-11), recited as corresponding to it, is the anurūpa pragātha mantra. This is the second. In the first pragātha, the tṛca beginning यज्ञायज्ञा (*yajñāyajñā*), there are three Ṛks. These are sung by the Sāmagas (Sāmaveda 35; 703). In response, the ṛtviks of the hotṛ class recite the anurūpa mantras देवो वो द्रविणोदाः (*devo vo draviṇodāḥ*). The reason for reciting these two mantras in the middle of the śastra mantras is this: the Ṛk यज्ञायज्ञा (*yajñāyajñā*), the stotriya mantra, is called the womb (yoni). The two sūktas mentioned above, वैश्वानराय (*vaiśvānarāya*) and प्रत्यक्षसः (*pratyakṣasaḥ*), are the two thighs. Since the womb-region of women lies between the two thighs, in the middle region of the body, these stotriya and anurūpa mantras, of the form of the womb, are put in the middle of the śastra mantras, after the recitation of the two sūktas. By doing so the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice is helped to obtain offspring. The man who knows this also rejoices, furnished with offspring and wealth of cattle.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऊ॒र्जो नपा॑तं॒ स हि॒नायम॑स्म॒युदाशे॑म ह॒व्यदा॑तये ।  
> भुव॒द्वाजे॑ष्ववि॒ता भुव॑द्वृ॒ध उ॒त त्रा॒ता त॒नूनां॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ऊर्जः । नपातं । सः । हिन । अयं । अस्मयुः । दाशेम । हव्यदातये ।  
> भुवत् । वाजेषु । अविता । भुवत् । वृधः । उत । त्राता । तनूनां ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऊर्जोऽन्नस्य बलस्य वा नपातं पुत्रं प्र शंसिषमित्यनुषंगात् प्रशंसामेत्यर्थः । हिनेति निपातद्वयसमुदायो हीत्यस्यार्थे । स खल्वयमग्निरस्मयुरस्मान्कामयमानो भवति । वयं च हव्यदातॆये हव्यानां हविषां देवेभ्यो
> दात्रे तस्मा आग्नये दाशेम । हवींषि दद्याम । स चाग्निर्वाजेषु संग्रामेष्टविता
> रक्षिता वृधो वर्धकश्चास्माकं भुवत् । भवतु । उतापि च तनूनां तनयानामस्मत्पुत्राणां च त्राता रक्षिता भवतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ऊर्जः (*ūrjaḥ*): of food or of strength; नपातं (*napātaṃ*): the son. "We praise" is to be supplied from the preceding Ṛk by continuation; the meaning is: we praise the son of strength. हिन (*hina*) is a combination of two particles in the sense of हि (*hi*), "for". This Agni indeed is अस्मयुः (*asmayuḥ*), one who desires us. And we would give (दाशेम (*dāśema*): may we give oblations) to that Agni, हव्यदातये (*havyadātaye*), the giver of oblations, of offerings, to the gods. And may that Agni be, in battles (वाजेषु (*vājeṣu*): in conflicts), our protector (अविता (*avitā*)) and our increaser (वृधः (*vṛdhaḥ*)). And may he also be the protector of our bodies (तनूनां (*tanūnāṃ*)), that is, of our children, our sons.
